\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} heuristic classification of methods (Remark~\ref{thm:fourway});
no insufficiency theorem is proved.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[wrong sign: Hilbert--P\'olya]\label{ob:wrongsign}
The Hilbert--P\'olya programme seeks a self-adjoint operator $H$ with $\{i(\rho-\tfrac12):\zeta(\rho)=0\}$
as its spectrum; if such $H$ exists, the spectrum is real and RH follows. The constructions considered give at best
\[
   Q(f,f)\ \ge\ -C\|f\|^2\qquad\text{with some }C\ge0;
\]
for $f$ in the classes considered, with $C$ depending on the support of $f$ and on the norm
chosen ($L^2$ in the cases examined); RH is the case $C=0$. The tools examined --- operator norm, Carleson constant, kernel diagonal,
spectral-shift derivative --- control the size of the possible negativity, not its sign, in the cases
examined. Tightening $C$ to a smaller positive constant does not give $C=0$.

Heuristically, consider the localized Weil form restricted to a one-parameter family $f_\lambda$
concentrated near a single zero $\rho_j=\tfrac12+b_j+i\gamma_j$. Formally, to second order in the transverse
displacement $b_j$, one expects
\[
   Q(f_\lambda,f_\lambda)\ =\ \underbrace{Q_0}_{\ge0\text{ if all zeros on line}}
   \ -\ b_j^2\cdot\underbrace{Q_2}_{>0}\ +\ O(b_j^4).
\]
This expansion is heuristic (no derivation of $Q_2>0$ is given here); it expresses the expectation that $Q$
decreases as the zero moves off the line, so that the critical line is a local maximum of the form in the
transverse direction. Formally, if the zeros $\pm\gamma_j\pm ib$ are displaced with $b$ as parameter and
$F=\widehat f$, each such quadruple contributes
\[
   -8\Bigl[\Re\bigl(F''(\gamma_j)\,\overline{F(\gamma_j)}\bigr)+|F'(\gamma_j)|^2\Bigr]
\]
to $\partial_b^2Q|_{b=0}$ (the pair $\gamma_j\pm ib$ contributes $2\Re[F(\gamma_j+ib)\overline{F(\gamma_j-ib)}]$,
whose second $b$-derivative at $b=0$ is $-4[\Re(F''\bar F)+|F'|^2](\gamma_j)$, and the mirror pair at
$-\gamma_j$ contributes equally when $f$ is even; a zero of multiplicity $c_j$ multiplies the term by $c_j$).
For \emph{detector test functions}, i.e.\ those with $F(\gamma_j)=0$ at the sampled ordinates, the first
term drops and the contribution is non-positive, consistent with the curvature computation
$C(\gamma)>0$ under RH (No-go~\ref{ng:curvature}). Consequently a variational argument that seeks a
minimum of $Q$ in the transverse direction, for these test functions, moves away from the critical line.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} heuristic classification of methods (the four functionals
listed, in the cases examined); no insufficiency theorem is proved. The target $Q\succeq0$ is of class
$E$ by W\ref{ob:weil}.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[infinite-dimensional Hasse--Minkowski failure]\label{ob:hassemink}
The Hasse--Minkowski theorem is a local--global principle for isotropy of rational quadratic forms. A
positivity analogue for the Weil form would ask that $Q_v\succeq0$ for all $v$ imply $Q\succeq0$. It fails
at two levels.

\textbf{Failure at the factorization level.} With $h=f\star\tilde f$ and the normalization of
\eqref{eq:weilform}, the explicit formula reads
\[
   \underbrace{\sum_\rho \widehat h(\gamma_\rho)}_{M_{\mathrm{zeros}}}\ =\ \mathcal W(h)\ =\
   \widehat h(\tfrac i2)+\widehat h(-\tfrac i2)
   \ -\ \underbrace{\sum_p\sum_{k\ge1}\frac{\log p}{p^{k/2}}\bigl(h(k\log p)+h(-k\log p)\bigr)}_{M_{\mathrm{arith}}}
   \ +\ W_\infty(h).
\]
The arithmetic side $M_{\mathrm{arith}}$ factors over primes:
$M_{\mathrm{arith}}=\sum_p Q_p$ with $Q_p(h)=\sum_{k\ge1}\frac{\log p}{p^{k/2}}\bigl(h(k\log p)+h(-k\log p)\bigr)$ a
local datum. The zero side $M_{\mathrm{zeros}}(h)=\sum_\rho\widehat h(\gamma_\rho)$ carries the
Hypothesis; it is \emph{global} in the sense that no zero is attached to any prime. The factorization
$Q=\sum_v Q_v$ would require each $Q_v$ to carry information about which zeros are off-line; since no
Euler factor has any such information, the factorization does not exist on the side that matters
(No-go~\ref{ng:nofactor}).

\textbf{Failure of the infinite-dimensional principle.} Even granting a hypothetical factorization, there
is no known Hasse--Minkowski-type principle for positivity in infinite dimensions. The passage fails
for the truncations that actually occur here: the finite forms $Q_X$ are \emph{not} compressions of a
fixed limit form --- the arithmetic side itself changes with the truncation --- so positivity of every
$Q_X$ controls the limit only if the convergence $Q_X\to Q$ is uniform, which is precisely the
uniform-norm wall (W\ref{ob:uniform}); see also No-go~\ref{ng:hm}.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} heuristic/observation (No-gos \ref{ng:nofactor},
\ref{ng:hm}); no insufficiency theorem is proved. The residual convergence statement belongs to
W\ref{ob:uniform}.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[per-place reflection positivity fails]\label{ob:perplace}
Osterwalder--Schrader reconstruction produces a positive Hamiltonian from Euclidean data satisfying
reflection positivity (together with regularity and growth conditions). For a prime $p$ and $r\in\R$ put
\[
   g_p(r)\ =\ \sqrt p\,\cos(r\log p)-1 .
\]
Then $g_p(0)=\sqrt p-1>0>-\sqrt p-1=g_p(\pi/\log p)$, and $g_p$ is not of positive type, since its
Fourier transform $\tfrac{\sqrt p}{2}(\delta_{\log p}+\delta_{-\log p})-\delta_0$ is not a positive
measure (Bochner). The per-prime kernel of the attempt recorded in No-go~\ref{ng:perplace} is
proportional to $g_p$ (its derivation is not reproduced here), so that attempt has no per-place positive
kernel, and the hypothesis of the Osterwalder--Schrader reconstruction is not available place by place.
This is an observation about that attempt, not a theorem about all per-place constructions.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} heuristic/observation (No-go~\ref{ng:perplace}); no
insufficiency theorem is proved.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[the cohomological-Frobenius wall]\label{ob:cohom}
For curves over finite fields Weil's proof \cite{Weil1948} obtains $|\alpha|=\sqrt q$ for every
eigenvalue $\alpha$ of Frobenius from the positivity of an intersection pairing (Castelnuovo--Severi
inequality / Hodge index theorem). (Deligne's general proof \cite{Deligne} proceeds differently; the
corresponding positivity statement in higher dimension, the Hodge standard conjecture, is open.)
Transporting the curve mechanism to $\zeta$ requires a cohomology with a Frobenius-type operator and a
positive-definite invariant pairing. No such object is known.

\textbf{Scaling and conjugation.} The dilation $(D_tf)(x)=f(e^tx)$ commutes with the conjugation
$Jf(x)=\overline{f(\bar x)}$, since $e^t$ is real. The scaling action therefore supplies no Hodge-type
decomposition by itself: the missing ingredient is a polarization, not a linearity property.
(No-go~\ref{ng:antilinear} concerns a different, quaternionic complex structure in a finite-window model
and is recorded there as an observation.)

\textbf{Gapless polarization.} For curves over a finite field the positivity of the intersection pairing
forces $|\alpha|=\sqrt q$. For the finite-window polarization form of
No-go~\ref{ng:gapless}, in the truncations examined numerically the smallest eigenvalue decreased
geometrically as the truncation grew (no reproducible script is supplied), so no uniform spectral gap was
observed. This is an observation about one truncation family, not a theorem.

The object that would close this route is a $\mathbb Z$-cohomological Frobenius with a pairing that is
positive definite with a lower bound uniform in the truncation --- precisely the unbuilt object of the adelic programme \cite{connesconsani-site} --- and its
construction is a problem of algebraic geometry, not of classical analysis.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} $F(X;C)$ (heuristic) with $X=$ the Hodge--Riemann/Frobenius
transplant and $C=$ the existence of a cohomological Frobenius with a positive-definite invariant pairing over
$\Spec\Z$; that $C$ would imply RH is the heuristic content of the Hodge-index mechanism (no such object
is defined, so no implication is proved), and no equivalence is claimed.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[sign, not magnitude]\label{ob:magphase}
Remark~\ref{thm:marginality} identifies, heuristically, the distinguishing feature between $\zeta$ and an
off-line-zero falsifier as the \emph{sign} of the von Mangoldt data: $\zeta$ has $\Lambda(n)\ge0$, while
the Davenport--Heilbronn function $L_{\mathrm{DH}}$, which has zeros off the critical line, has
sign-indefinite coefficients $\Lambda_{\mathrm{DH}}(n)$, defined by
$-L_{\mathrm{DH}}'/L_{\mathrm{DH}}(s)=\sum_n\Lambda_{\mathrm{DH}}(n)n^{-s}$ ($L_{\mathrm{DH}}$ has no Euler
product, so these are not supported on prime powers). Positivity is a sign
property, and a functional that reads only the sizes $\{|c(n)|\}$ of a coefficient sequence takes the
same value on $\Lambda$ and on every sign-changed sequence $\varepsilon(n)\Lambda(n)$, $\varepsilon(n)\in\{\pm1\}$,
so it cannot certify $\Lambda\ge0$.

The practical consequence is that any positivity argument that operates through size bounds of the form
$\|M_{\mathrm{arith}}\|\le C$ or $|\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-s}|\le C(\sigma)$ cannot reach the Hypothesis: such
bounds are insensitive to signs. Heuristically, what could distinguish the two functions is the
positivity $\Lambda(n)\ge0$ itself, in contrast with the sign-indefinite coefficients
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{DH}}$. The implication ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH of Part~II uses Nevanlinna--Pick
interpolation and Montel's theorem (Steps~8--11) and no arithmetic input. A sufficient condition for
ARP-P is tower-ARP (Definition~\ref{def:ARP}); a family of self-adjoint operators $A_P^\circ$ with
uniformly bounded source maps would supply it (Remark~\ref{rmk:operator-towers}, \S\ref{sec:shorting}),
but no such family is constructed in this paper.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} heuristic classification of methods (functionals reading only
the magnitudes $\{|c(n)|\}$, target the positivity $\Lambda\ge0$); no insufficiency theorem is
proved.
\end{obstruction}

\begin{obstruction}[uniform norm: the rank-one escape]\label{ob:uniform}
A spectral route that assembles a finite Weil operator $K_P=\sum_{n\le P}\Lambda(n)K_n$, where $K_n$
denotes the kernel contributed by the term $n$ of the prime side of the explicit formula (the description
here is schematic; the operators are not constructed in this paper), meets a further barrier. If the
normalization is such that $\Tr K_n=n^{-1}\log(P/n)$, then
\[
   \Tr K_P\ =\ \sum_{n\le P}\frac{\Lambda(n)}{n}\log\frac Pn\ \sim\ \tfrac12(\log P)^2,
\]
since $\sum_{n\le x}\Lambda(n)/n=\log x+O(1)$ (Mertens). Heuristically this divergence is carried by a
single positive rank-one mode (the pole of $\zeta$ at $s=1$); after removing this mode by Feshbach (Schur) shorting, the shorted trace is
bounded. Let $\Pi^\circ$ denote the orthogonal projection onto the complement of the pole mode. The
question is then whether the \emph{operator norm} of the shorted primitive piece is bounded:
\[
   \sup_P\bigl\|\Pi^\circ K_P\Pi^\circ\bigr\|\ <\ \infty\quad\text{?}
\]
Heuristically, by the explicit formula this norm is governed by the off-line zeros: an off-line zero
$\rho$ should produce growth of $P^{\,|\Re\rho-1/2|}$ type. The uniform-norm bound is therefore expected
to be of the same strength as the Hypothesis, but no equivalence is proved here; needing it as an input
would be circular.

The following points sharpen this wall heuristically. The finite Weil--Gram kernels are
positive \emph{by construction} (a Schur/Haynsworth complement of a positive canonical Gram), so no
positivity remains to be proved at the finite level --- the escape sidesteps the Weil-positivity wall
(W\ref{ob:weil}) there. What is not free is a \emph{convergence}: the shorted primitive kernels
$K_P^\circ$ must converge, in the finite-Gram topology at the critical boundary, to the genuine
Kre\u{\i}n--Langer kernel of $\Xi$; equivalently $\sup_P\|\Pi^\circ K_P\Pi^\circ\|<\infty$. Two facts
delimit the gap. The leading trace divergence $\tfrac12(\log P)^2$ is carried by the single rank-one pole
mode --- but leading-trace rank-one does \emph{not} imply primitive boundedness: a subleading escape
direction of size $o(\log P)^2$ that still diverges is invisible to the trace, so the rank-one trace alone
does not bound the endpoint index. And the naive de Branges packaging fails at the archimedean factor and
at the scalar determinant, which is index-blind (No-go~\ref{ng:debranges}). The residue is thus a single
index-continuity statement, expected (but not proved) to be of the strength of the Hypothesis.

The conditional ARP-P route of Part~II does not use this bound. It never forms the operator $K_P$ or its
shorted version as an operator on an infinite-dimensional space: the implication ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH
uses Nevanlinna--Pick interpolation and Montel's theorem on the finite-channel functions $\GXi^F$
(Steps~8--11). In the conditional realization layer (\S\ref{sec:shorting}) the uniform bound assumed is
the source bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:sourcebound}, a bound per vector of a fixed finite source frame, not
an operator norm over the whole space. The difficulty is not removed: it reappears in the open Step~5.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Classification:} $F(X;C)$ with $X=$ the spectral route assembling the
full shorted Weil operator and $C=$ the uniform primitive-norm bound
$\sup_P\|\Pi^\circ K_P\Pi^\circ\|<\infty$; heuristically $C$ is of RH strength (an off-line zero should
produce growth of $P^{\,|\Re\rho-1/2|}$ type), but no equivalence is proved here.
\end{obstruction}

\subsection{The master quantifier}\label{sec:masterquant}

\paragraph{Heuristic principle.} The seven walls W\ref{ob:propagation}--W\ref{ob:uniform} can be read as one obstruction in seven forms. The
single sentence beneath them all is a heuristic statement about quantifiers.

\begin{center}
\emph{The Riemann Hypothesis is always the passage from average, almost-all, or interior\\
to specific, uniform, or boundary.}
\end{center}

Heuristically, a single off-line zero is invisible to averaged statistics of $\zeta$ (height, density,
correlation): zero-density theorems bound the number of off-line zeros but do not exclude a sparse set of
them. Each wall above can be read as an instance:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item \textbf{Propagation wall (W\ref{ob:propagation})}: interior ($\Re s>1$) $\not\to$ boundary
($\Re s=\tfrac12$). Arithmetic data from the region of absolute convergence do not reach the strip.
\item \textbf{Wrong-sign wall (W\ref{ob:wrongsign})}: lower bound with some constant
($\lambda_{\min}\ge-C$) $\not\to$ the sharp constant ($\lambda_{\min}\ge0$). Tools control the size of the
negativity, not its sign.
\item \textbf{Hasse--Minkowski wall (W\ref{ob:hassemink})}: local ($Q_v\ge0$ at every $v$) $\not\to$
global ($Q\ge0$). The local-to-global principle fails in infinite dimension.
\item \textbf{Per-place wall (W\ref{ob:perplace})}: no per-prime positive kernel is available in the
attempt examined, so there is nothing local to assemble.
\item \textbf{Frobenius wall (W\ref{ob:cohom})}: function field (positive intersection pairing exists)
$\not\to$ $\mathbb Z$ (no positive pairing over $\Spec\mathbb Z$ constructed).
\item \textbf{Magnitude wall (W\ref{ob:magphase})}: generic (magnitudes $|\Lambda(n)|$) $\not\to$
specific (positivity $\Lambda(n)\ge0$). Sizes do not determine signs.
\item \textbf{Uniform-norm wall (W\ref{ob:uniform})}: pointwise (each compressed channel bounded)
$\not\to$ uniform (operator norm over the whole space bounded). Per-channel control does not give a
uniform operator bound.
\end{itemize}

\noindent The conditional ARP-P route of \S\ref{sec:live-proof-steps} is organized around a rigidity
theorem rather than an estimate: if Step~5 holds, a normal family of Nevanlinna--Pick interpolants is shown to
have a limit that stays holomorphic across the critical strip (Nevanlinna--Pick interpolation and
Montel's theorem, Steps~8--11); this implication uses no arithmetic input. This does not cross the walls above: the whole difficulty is concentrated in Step~5,
which reduces to the open input $\Omega_7$, equivalent to RH.

For each wall one may name the event that would be needed to cross it:
\begin{align*}
   \mathcal E_1 &: \text{arithmetic data from }\Re s>1\text{ give an upper bound on }b_j\text{ tending to }0\text{,}\\
   \mathcal E_2 &: \text{the Weil form has a global minimum at the critical line,}\\
   \mathcal E_3 &: Q=Q_\infty+\sum_p Q_p\text{ (local-global factorization on the zero side),}\\
   \mathcal E_4 &: \text{the Hodge--Riemann polarization has a uniform spectral gap.}
\end{align*}
The following observations were made: arithmetic data from
$\Re s>1$ give zero-free regions whose width tends to $0$, hence no upper bound on $b_j$ tending to
$0$ (Remark~\ref{thm:fourway}, a
classification of known methods); for detector test functions the second variation in the transverse
direction is non-positive; the zero side does not factor over primes (No-go~\ref{ng:nofactor});
numerically the tested polarization has no uniform gap (No-go~\ref{ng:gapless}). None of these is a
theorem that the corresponding event fails in general. The conditional route uses none of these events;
conditionally on Step~5, it obtains a normal family from Nevanlinna--Pick interpolants and applies
Montel's theorem.

\subsection{Statements recorded for orientation, with their logical type}\label{sec:positive}

Besides the observations above, a number of positive statements are recorded here, each with its logical
type: proved statements, classical facts, and numerical observations.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=2.2em]

\item \emph{The forced-negativity statement (unproved here).} It is expected that an off-line zero at
real-part displacement $\delta>0$ in the window of a localized Weil form forces
\[
   \lambda_{\min}(Q)\ \le\ -c(\delta,\sigma,J)\ <\ 0,
\]
for a positive constant $c$ depending on $\delta$, the localization scale $\sigma$, and the test interval
$J$. No proof is given in this paper, and the converse (positivity of the localized form when the window
contains no off-line zero) is not claimed.

\item \emph{Truncation error (observation).} Numerically, the truncation error $\eta(X)=Q_X-Q_\infty$ of
the truncated Weil form with Gaussian localization of scale $\sigma$ behaves like
\[
   \eta(X)\ \approx\ A\cdot\exp\bigl(-\alpha(\log X)^2\bigr),\qquad\alpha\approx\tfrac{\sigma^2}{8};
\]
this is an empirical fit (no reproducible script is supplied), not a proved bound.

\item \emph{The curvature identity.} Under RH,
\[
   \partial_\sigma^2\log|\xi(\tfrac12+\sigma+i\gamma)|\big|_{\sigma=0}
   \ =\ C(\gamma)\ =\ \sum_\rho\frac{1}{(\gamma-\gamma_\rho)^2}\ >\ 0
\]
for $\gamma$ not a zero ordinate (Hadamard product; No-go~\ref{ng:curvature}). An off-line pair contributes
a negative term $-2/b^2$ at its own height, but this does not by itself force negativity; no converse is
claimed.

\item \emph{The second-variation formula (formal).} With $F=\widehat f$, a quadruple of zeros
$\pm\gamma_j\pm ib$ displaced with parameter $b$ contributes
\[
   -8\Bigl[\Re\bigl(F''(\gamma_j)\,\overline{F(\gamma_j)}\bigr)+|F'(\gamma_j)|^2\Bigr]
\]
to $\partial_b^2Q|_{b=0}$ (two-line computation in W\ref{ob:wrongsign}); this is non-positive for the
detector test functions, those with $F(\gamma_j)=0$. For these test functions the critical line is therefore a local maximum of $Q$ in
the transverse direction, so a variational argument seeking a minimum there does not pin the zeros.

\item \emph{The sector decomposition.} Writing $\xi(s)=\tfrac12 s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)$, the
second derivative $\partial_t^2\log|\xi(\tfrac12+it)|$ factors into three computable sectors:
\begin{align}
   G_{\mathrm{poly}}(t) &= \frac{2(\tfrac14-t^2)}{(t^2+\tfrac14)^2},\label{eq:gpoly2}\\
   G_\Gamma(t) &= -\tfrac14\,\Re\,\psi^{(1)}\!\bigl(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}{2}\bigr),\label{eq:ggamma2}\\
   G_\zeta(t) &= \partial_t^2\log|\zeta(\tfrac12+it)|,\label{eq:gzeta2}
\end{align}
where $\psi^{(1)}$ is the trigamma function. The first two sectors, $G_{\mathrm{poly}}$ and $G_\Gamma$,
are known functions of $t$ that can be tabulated to arbitrary precision. The third depends on the zeros.
Under RH, $G_{\mathrm{poly}}+G_\Gamma+G_\zeta=-\sum_\gamma(t-\gamma)^{-2}<0$ away from the zeros; this
concavity of $t\mapsto\log|\xi(\tfrac12+it)|$ is a consequence of RH, and no converse is claimed here.
The decomposition is used in \S\ref{sec:arcD}.

\item \emph{The Jensen-polynomial reformulation.} RH is equivalent to the hyperbolicity of all Jensen
polynomials attached to the Taylor coefficients of $\Xi$ (P\'olya). The Tur\'an inequalities, the
degree-two case, hold unconditionally \cite{CsordasNorfolkVarga1986} and therefore carry no information
about RH.

\item \emph{The four variational attempts (heuristic).} The four attempts of \S\ref{sec:arcD}
(pinning at a minimum, a concavity criterion, a sector inequality, an $\eta$-invariant argument) all
concern consequences of the Laguerre--P\'olya property of $\Xi$, which is equivalent to RH; no
equivalence between any one of the four statements and RH is proved here.

\item \emph{The sign principle (heuristic; Remark~\ref{thm:marginality}).} A functional of the
magnitudes $\{|c(n)|\}$ takes the same value on $\Lambda$ and on every sign-changed sequence
$\varepsilon(n)\Lambda(n)$, so it cannot certify the property $\Lambda\ge0$. $L_{\mathrm{DH}}$ gives an
example of sign-indefinite data with off-line zeros: $\Lambda_{\mathrm{DH}}(3)=a_3\log3=-\kappa\log3<0$,
where $a_3=-\kappa<0$ is the third Dirichlet coefficient of $L_{\mathrm{DH}}$; its magnitudes differ from
those of $\zeta$ (for instance $|\Lambda_{\mathrm{DH}}(2)|=\kappa\log2\ne\log2$).

\item \emph{The per-place indefiniteness.} The function $g_p(r)=\sqrt p\cos(r\log p)-1$, to which the
per-prime kernel of the attempt of No-go~\ref{ng:perplace} is proportional, is not of positive type
(elementary; W\ref{ob:perplace}).

\item \emph{The de Bruijn--Newman constant.} In the heat-flow deformation
$H_\lambda(z)=\int e^{\lambda u^2}\Phi(u)\cos(zu)\,du$ (with $\Phi$ the Riemann kernel, so that $H_0$ is
proportional to $\Xi$), there is a constant $\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}$ such that $H_\lambda$ has only real
zeros iff $\lambda\ge\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}$; RH is the statement $\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}\le0$. If every
zero of $H_0$ lies in $|\Im z|\le\Delta$, then $\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}\le\Delta^2/2$ \cite{deBruijn}; also
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}\ge0$ \cite{RodgersTao} and $\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}\le0.2$ \cite{PolymathDBN};
hence RH $\Longleftrightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}=0$. As $\lambda$ decreases through
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{dBN}}$, pairs of real zeros may coalesce and split into complex-conjugate pairs. Reading
the number of non-real pairs as a Kreĭn--Langer index is an interpretation, not a theorem proved here.

\item \emph{The Lefschetz/ample-class dichotomy.} The Hodge--Riemann approach requires an ample class
over $\Spec\mathbb Z$. In a finite model examined numerically (not defined here) the candidate
polarization form was indefinite; no statement is claimed. No arithmetic N\'eron--Severi group over
$\Spec\mathbb Z$ is defined here.

\item \emph{The zero-side tautology (remark).} A Gram matrix
$\bigl[\sum_\rho m_\rho v_i(\rho)\overline{v_j(\rho)}\bigr]_{i,j}$, with $m_\rho>0$ and functions $v_i$
on the zeros, is positive semidefinite for any multiset of zeros, so its least eigenvalue carries no
information about their location and cannot distinguish $\zeta$ from $L_{\mathrm{DH}}$.

\item \emph{The de Branges/Hilbert space canonicity.} The Hermite--Biehler structure function $E$ of the
canonical system \cite{deBranges} satisfies $|E(z)|>|E^*(z)|$ for $\Im z>0$, where $E^*(z)=\overline{E(\bar z)}$.
For such $E$ the multiplication operator in the de Branges space $\mathcal H(E)$ has self-adjoint
extensions \cite{deBranges}. A self-adjoint operator $A$ on a Hilbert space has no non-real
eigenvalues and satisfies $\|(A-z)^{-1}\|\le|\Im z|^{-1}$; a family of such finite operators with
uniformly bounded source maps would supply tower-ARP (Remark~\ref{rmk:operator-towers},
\S\ref{sec:shorting}), but no such family is constructed in this paper.

\item \emph{The DH-falsifier computation (observation).} In the truncations computed, the detector gave
$\mu_0(L_{\mathrm{DH}})<0$ while $\mu_0(\zeta)\ge0$ (no reproducible script is supplied, so this is an
observation). No explanation of this observation is proved.

\end{enumerate}

\noindent These items are recorded as context for the conditional ARP-P route of
\S\ref{sec:live-proof-steps}; the implication ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH uses none of them. The route is
conditional on the open Step~5.

\section{Related work: the adelic--spectral programme}\label{sec:relwork}

The construction here is operator-theoretic and adelic in spirit, and it could not have been arrived at
without the body of work that recast the explicit formula as a trace formula and the Riemann Hypothesis as
a positivity statement in noncommutative geometry. We summarize the debts, citing the published
mathematics; we make no claim about any individual's view of the present paper.

The trace-formula framing \cite{connes1999} expresses the explicit formula as a trace identity on the
adelic class space and recasts the Hypothesis as the positivity of a Weil distribution; the validity of
the global trace formula is equivalent to RH \cite{connes1999}. The archimedean refinement
\cite{connesconsani2021} made the semilocal Weil form and its positivity explicit, and is the direct
ancestor of the localized detector used in the numerical observations above. The scaling- and arithmetic-site
programme \cite{connesconsani-site} supplied the geometric language in which the missing object --- a
suitable cohomology of $\Spec\mathbb Z$ with a Frobenius and a positive pairing --- is precisely named;
the recognition that this object is \emph{unbuilt} is exactly wall~\ref{ob:cohom}. Most recently, the prolate and
spectral-triple realizations of the zeros \cite{ccm-prolate,ccm-zeta} gave concrete self-adjoint models
whose spectra are reported there to approximate the low-lying zeros numerically, sharpening the question of what additional input
forces reality of the spectrum. The Hecke-algebra and symmetry-breaking work \cite{bostconnes} stands
behind the whole adelic picture, and the more recent absolute-geometry developments --- the
$\mathrm{BC}$-system and quantized-calculus framework \cite{ConnesConsaniBC} and the Riemann--Roch theory
for $\Spec\mathbb Z$ \cite{ConnesConsaniRR} --- continue to build the geometric object whose absence is
wall~\ref{ob:cohom}.

Classical inputs also appear: the Mertens and Chebyshev estimates \cite{IwaniecKowalski,Davenport} give
the $\tfrac12(\log P)^2$ asymptotic of W\ref{ob:uniform}; the Kreĭn--Langer theory of generalized
Nevanlinna functions \cite{KreinLanger} supplies a count of non-real poles that motivates the signature kernel; Hadamard's factorization
\cite{Titchmarsh} controls the entire function $\Xi$; and Nevanlinna--Pick interpolation \cite{FoiasFrazho} with Montel's
theorem \cite{Remmert} is used in the conditional implication ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH (Steps 8--11),
which uses no arithmetic input.

The Beurling--Nyman and de Branges reformulations of RH \cite{Beurling,BaezDuarte,deBranges} are part of
the background. The de Bruijn--Newman connection \cite{deBruijn,Newman,RodgersTao,PolymathDBN} and the
Beurling--Nyman distance \cite{Beurling,BaezDuarte} appear in the no-go ledger as directions that have
not yielded lower bounds.

The present route can be read as a deliberate \emph{retreat} from the hardest part of the adelic programme:
where the arithmetic site seeks the positive Frobenius cohomology directly on $\Spec\mathbb Z$, the
present route instead tests a Pick positivity condition on explicit finite-channel data at nodes in the
region $\Re s>1$. Since ARP-P is equivalent to RH (Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}), this route
relocates the difficulty and does not avoid it.

The spectral randomness and universality of $\zeta$ that motivates the GUE hypothesis --- the pair
correlation of Montgomery \cite{Montgomery}, the GUE statistics of Odlyzko's computations
\cite{Odlyzko}, the random-matrix value distribution of Keating--Snaith \cite{KeatingSnaith} --- is the
empirical background, and the generic-vs-specific distinction of the master quantifier
(\S\ref{sec:masterquant}) suggests why these generic results do not by themselves decide the
Hypothesis.

The canonical-system framework used in this paper was developed by de Branges \cite{deBranges} and
extended to indefinite metrics by Kreĭn and Langer \cite{KreinLanger}. For a Hermite--Biehler function
$E$, multiplication by $z$ in $\mathcal H(E)$ is a closed symmetric operator with deficiency indices
$(1,1)$, and its self-adjoint extensions have simple spectrum \cite{deBranges}. In the indefinite theory
\cite{KreinLanger}, a generalized Nevanlinna function of class $N_\kappa$ (a function meromorphic in
$\C\setminus\R$ whose Pick kernel has $\kappa$ negative squares) has at most $\kappa$ poles in the open
upper half-plane; this theory motivates the signature kernel of the Prologue, but the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh} uses only Nevanlinna--Pick interpolation and a normal-family argument.

Tur\'an's power-sum method gives lower bounds for power sums and is used in zero-density arguments;
it yields aggregate statements, not the location of an individual zero (heuristic remark).

\section{Three guiding analogies}\label{sec:analogies}

Three physical analogies guided the search. None is a proof, and we are careful to label them as
heuristics; but each carries real mathematics, and each pointed, in the end, toward the same construction.

\paragraph{Wave--particle duality.} In quantum mechanics a duality is resolved not by choosing a side but
by exhibiting one object with two representations related by the Fourier transform. The explicit formula
plays exactly this role for $\zeta$: it is a Fourier--Mellin change of basis between the \emph{primes}
(the multiplicative/scale side) and the \emph{zeros} (the spectral/frequency side),
\[
   \sum_\rho \widehat h(\rho)\ =\ \widehat h\text{-terms at }\infty\ -\ \sum_{p,k}\frac{\log p}{p^{k/2}}\,
   h(k\log p),
\]
the two sides being the two ``representations'' of a single distribution. The difficulty, and the reason
the analogy does not by itself close, is that the inner product making the spectral side unitary --- the
``conserved energy'' of the duality --- is precisely the Weil form $Q$ that encodes $\zeta$; the duality is
real but is not resolved by structure alone. (An operator realization of this analogy proposed earlier is
false; see \S\ref{sec:withdrawn}.)

\paragraph{A Maxwell-style unification.} A second picture treats the two sides of the wall --- the
arithmetic side and the spectral side --- not as competitors but as two components of a single field, in
the way the electric and magnetic fields are two faces of one electromagnetic object exchanged by a change
of frame. On this view the explicit formula is the ``field equation'' coupling primes and zeros, and the
Riemann Hypothesis is a statement that the combined object has a definite polarization. This picture is
genuinely unifying and it is what motivates seeking a \emph{single} self-adjoint object carrying both the
prime data and the spectral data (through its eigenvalues), an object not constructed here; but it is a conceptual frame, not a computation, and we present it only as such.

\paragraph{Quantum mechanics and Stone's theorem.} The deepest analogy, and the one that finally clarified
the logic, is with the spectral theory of Hamiltonians. In quantum mechanics the reality of a spectrum
follows from Stone's theorem: a strongly continuous one-parameter \emph{unitary} group $U(t)=e^{itH}$ has
self-adjoint generator, hence real spectrum --- and unitarity is guaranteed by an inner product given in
advance by physics. The Berry--Keating Hamiltonian \cite{BerryKeating}
\[
   H_{\mathrm{BK}}\ =\ \tfrac12(xp+px),
\]
where $x$ is position and $p=-i\partial_x$ is momentum, has the formally attractive property that its
classical trajectories $\dot x=x$, $\dot p=-p$ are hyperbolic and not time-reversal invariant, and with
the Berry--Keating cut-offs $x\ge l_x$, $p\ge l_p$, $l_xl_p=2\pi$, the semiclassical count
\[
   N_{\mathrm{cl}}(T)\ =\ \frac{\mathrm{Area}\,\{l_x\le x,\ l_p\le p,\ xp\le T\}}{2\pi}
   \ =\ \frac{T}{2\pi}\Bigl(\log\frac{T}{2\pi}-1\Bigr)+1
\]
has the leading terms of the Riemann--von Mangoldt formula $N(T)\sim(T/2\pi)\log(T/2\pi e)$.
This is striking, but it stops there: on $L^2(0,\infty)$ the operator $H_{\mathrm{BK}}$ is essentially
self-adjoint with purely continuous spectrum $\mathbb R$, and does not reproduce the zeros of $\zeta$. The model
identifies the right classical action but not the right quantization.

The adelic scale flow carries this further: on the id\`ele class space $L^2(C_{\mathbb Q})$ the
multiplicative group $\mathbb R_+^*$ acts by scaling and realizes a natural ``quantum Hamiltonian'' whose
absorption spectrum formally consists of the zeros \cite{connes1999}. But exhibiting a Hilbert space on
which this flow is unitary is \emph{logically the same problem} as exhibiting the inner product that makes
the zeros real, which is, once more, the Weil positivity. This is the \emph{inverted Stone} principle: for
$\zeta$ there is \emph{no} inner product given in advance by physics; every candidate Hilbert space that
makes the spectrum real must itself encode the Hypothesis.

The only RH-free symmetries are $\Xi(-z)=\Xi(z)$ (the functional equation) and
$\overline{\Xi(\bar z)}=\Xi(z)$ (reality); together they make the zero set invariant under $z\mapsto\bar z$
and $z\mapsto-z$. Neither is an evolution, so neither can play the role of $U(t)=e^{itH}$.

For this reason the conditional route does not try to make the scale flow unitary by fiat. A
realization of this kind would require a family of finite self-adjoint operators with uniformly bounded
source maps (Remark~\ref{rmk:operator-towers}, \S\ref{sec:shorting}), not constructed here. The
implication ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH itself uses Nevanlinna--Pick interpolation and Montel's theorem and
needs no knowledge of where the zeros are. The difficulty is not removed: it is concentrated in Step~5.

\paragraph{Summary of the three analogies.} The wave--particle analogy identified the structure (a
Fourier--Mellin duality with the explicit formula as field equation) but could not close because the
inner product was not given. The Maxwell-unification analogy identified the kind of single object that
should carry both sides, but provided no route to constructing it without circular assumptions. The
inverted-Stone analogy explained the precise logical obstacle: the inner product must be built, not
assumed, and no finite construction of it is given here. The three analogies motivate the conditional
route of \S\ref{sec:live-proof-steps}; they are heuristics, not arguments.


%% ===== Proof-path sections (Overview, Notation, Live proof) =====
\part{The conditional route: the ARP-P chain and its single open input}

\section{Overview of the conditional route}\label{sec:live-chain}

The path is organized around a single equivalence,
\[
   \boxed{\textnormal{ARP-P}\Longleftrightarrow\textnormal{RH}},
\]
and the fifteen steps below are the ordered construction of that equivalence together with the one-way
bridge that would turn it into a proof of RH once Step~5 is supplied.  This section explains why the path
takes the shape it does;
Section~\ref{sec:live-proof-steps} carries out each step in full.

The path begins by moving the problem into the variable $z$ with $s=\half+iz$, so that
$\Xi(z)=\xi(\half+iz)$ and the critical line becomes the real axis: RH is exactly the statement that
every zero of $\Xi$ is real (Step~1).  Starting here is what makes the rest possible, because it turns a
statement about the location of zeros into a statement about a \emph{positivity} that complex analysis
can control.  Onto this geometric picture we graft arithmetic through the Weil explicit formula:
Steps~2--4 build finite \emph{channels} $F$ and an explicit meromorphic target $\GXi^F$ whose data is
fixed by the archimedean, pole, and prime terms with no holomorphic ambiguity.  Step~5 then isolates
the one arithmetic input, ARP-P: a Pick (positive-kernel) condition on this explicit data, tested only
at finite node sets in the lower half-plane $U_-$.

Everything after Step~5 is a closed analytic bridge that converts that positivity into the reality of
the zeros.  Positive Cauchy kernels furnish Nevanlinna--Pick interpolants (Steps~6--8); Montel
normality extends them holomorphically from $U_-$ across $\Omega_-$ (Step~9); any off-line zero would
have to appear as a finite-channel residue and is therefore excluded (Steps~10--11); and the
functional-equation symmetry $\Xi(-z)=\Xi(z)$ together with conjugation propagates the conclusion to
the whole plane (Steps~12--13), giving RH.  Steps~14--15 prove the converse direction
RH~$\Rightarrow$~ARP-P, closing the equivalence.  The design deliberately concentrates all the
difficulty into Step~5: every other step is a classical or complex-analytic implication whose
hypotheses are checked where they are used, so that establishing ARP-P alone would complete a proof of
RH.  Step~5 is the single open input; since it is equivalent to RH, this concentration reformulates the
difficulty and does not reduce it.  The remainder of the paper is devoted to Step~5.

\subsection{Claims and dependency map}\label{sec:status-map}

This subsection lists the logical type of each statement: the typed vocabulary, the two
dependency maps, and the tables of logical types.

\paragraph{Logical objects.}  Five kinds of objects appear, and they are never interchangeable.
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.8em]
\item A \textbf{Step} is a numbered logical stage in the fifteen-step architecture.  A Step may state a
construction, an implication, an equivalence, or an input condition; its logical type is recorded in the
table below.
\item A \textbf{logical relation} is the typed relation (implication, equivalence, reduction) asserted
between statements; arrows in the maps below carry their type.
\item An \textbf{H-level} (H0--H8) is a layer of the internal analysis of Step~5
(\S\ref{sec:live-proof-steps}); H-levels are not Steps.
\item An \textbf{$\Omega$-link} ($\Omega_1$--$\Omega_6$) is a proved characterization or a scoped
conditional transport statement in the difficulty-localization analysis inside Step~5
(\S\ref{subsec:omega7-attack}).  These entries do not form an unconditional level-by-level transport
of positivity.
\item The \textbf{terminal residue} is $\Omega_7$: the terminal statement the $\Omega$-chain isolates.
It is not a link, and it is not a Step.
\end{itemize}
Of the fifteen Steps, eleven (Steps 1--4, 6--8, 10, 12--14) are unconditional statements; Steps 9 and
11 are proved consequences of ARP-P; Step~15 assembles them; Step~5 only defines the open condition
ARP-P.  ARP-P is equivalent to RH (Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}), hence to Li's criterion
$\lambda_n\ge0$ for all $n$ \cite{Li}; the $\Omega$-links re-express this equivalence and do not shorten
it.  The chain reformulates RH and does not reduce its difficulty.

\paragraph{Proof-dependency map (implications; every arrow is proved, conditionally where marked).}
\[
   \boxed{\textnormal{ARP-P (Step 5)}}
   \;\Longrightarrow\;
   \textnormal{NP interpolants}
   \;\Longrightarrow\;
   \textnormal{normal family}
   \;\Longrightarrow\;
   \textnormal{no off-real poles}
   \;\Longrightarrow\;
   \textnormal{RH}
\]
\[
   \textnormal{RH}\;\Longrightarrow\;\textnormal{ARP-P}
   \qquad\text{(Step 14, Theorem~\ref{thm:rh-pick}; the forward chain is Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh})},
\]
closing the equivalence $\textnormal{ARP-P}\Longleftrightarrow\textnormal{RH}$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}).

\paragraph{Difficulty-localization map (reductions, $\rightsquigarrow$ = ``is reduced to / is serialized
as''; these arrows re-express the open input, they do not discharge it).}
\[
   \boxed{\textnormal{Step 5}}
   \;\rightsquigarrow\;
   \textnormal{H0--H8}
   \;\rightsquigarrow\;
   \textnormal{terminal defect }\delta_N
   \;\rightsquigarrow\;
   \textnormal{boundary positivity}
   \;\Longleftrightarrow\;
   \lambda_n\ge0\ \ (\Omega_7)
\]
(both sides being equivalent to RH; Theorem~\ref{thm:li-dictionary}), and
\[
   \boxed{\;\Omega_7\Longleftrightarrow\textnormal{RH}\;}
   \qquad\text{[Li \cite{Li}, classical].}
\]

\subsection{What is proved about Step 5}\label{subsec:live-step5}

Step~5 is the single open input.  The present paper does not claim to prove ARP-P; it decomposes it
into precise closed subcases, equivalent formulations, and the routes attempted against it.

\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.10\linewidth}p{0.48\linewidth}p{0.34\linewidth}@{}}

Substep & What is proved & What remains \\

H0 & Step~5 is equivalent to RH (Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}) & --- \\
H1 & Proved: one-node Pick positivity of the scalar coordinate $M_\Xi$, i.e.\ $\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)\ge0$ for $\Re s>1$ (classical); not a case of ARP-P & Matrix-channel level one is not routine; it has phase content \\
H2 & Exact Gershgorin criterion at arbitrary height; effective fixed-band criterion proved only for sufficiently high separated nodes & Bounded-height configurations require direct row domination or a separate argument; compactness alone gives no sign \\

H4 & Proved as determinant serialization: RH $\Longleftrightarrow d_N^{(k)}>0$ for all $k,N$ & Optional: rigorous determinant margins \\

\section{Notation}\label{sec:notation}

This section is deliberately a self-contained symbol table: its entries repeat, verbatim, the
definitions introduced in Steps~1--2, so that the reader can consult them without searching the chain.
The governing statements are the ones in \S\ref{sec:live-proof-steps}.

\begin{notation}[variables and regions]\label{not:variables}
We use
\[
   s=\half+iz,\qquad z=\frac{s-\half}{i},\qquad
   \Re s=\half-\Im z.
\]
Thus
\[
   \Omega_-:=\{z:\Im z<0\},\qquad
   U_-:=\{z:\Im z<-\half\}=\{s:\Re s>1\}.
\]
A nontrivial zero $\rho=\beta+i\gamma$ of $\zeta$ corresponds to
\[
   z_\rho=\gamma-i(\beta-\half).
\]
The Riemann Hypothesis is equivalent to all $z_\rho$ being real.
\end{notation}

\begin{notation}[completed function and Herglotz coordinate]\label{not:xi}
Let
\[
   \xi(s)=\half s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s),\qquad
   \Xi(z)=\xi(\half+iz).
\]
The fixed one-variable coordinate is
\[
   M_\Xi(z):=-\frac{\Xi'(z)}{\Xi(z)}.
\]
Since $\Xi'(z)=i\,\xi'(s)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:change}
   M_\Xi(z)=i\left(-\frac{\xi'(s)}{\xi(s)}\right),\qquad s=\half+iz.
\end{equation}
This factor $i$ is part of every endpoint identity below.
\end{notation}

\begin{notation}[finite channels]\label{not:channels}
For a finite source plane $F=\operatorname{span}\{\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m\}$, define the source functional
\[
   \ell_a(\phi)=\int_0^\infty \phi(t)e^{-iat}\,dt.
\]
The target finite-channel function is the explicit meromorphic sum
\begin{equation}\label{eq:GXiF}
   \GXi^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}
   :=
   \sum_\rho
   \frac{
      m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   }{z_\rho-z},
\end{equation}
where the sum is over the zeros of $\Xi$, counted with multiplicity $m_\rho$.  Thus ARP refers to this
specific meromorphic matrix function, not merely to a prescribed principal part.  It is not the scalar
multiple $M_\Xi(z)[\langle\phi_\beta,\phi_\alpha\rangle]$.

For this matrix function form the Pick kernel
\[
   \KXi^F(z,w)=\frac{\GXi^F(z)-\GXi^F(w)^*}{z-\bar w}.
\]
Vitali is applied to the one-variable functions $\GXi^F$.  Negative squares are read only from the
two-variable kernels $\KXi^F$.

Here $\phi_j$ range over the source core $\cS_a^\circ$ fixed in Definition~\ref{def:core}; its
Laplace transforms have rapid horizontal decay on a closed strip containing $|\Im a|\le\half$, so that
$\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_j)$ is defined for every zero and the sum converges locally uniformly off the zeros
(Lemma~\ref{lem:GXi-meromorphic}).

This notation is a global preview.  The governing finite-channel definition used in the chain is
Definition~\ref{def:target-channel}; the formulas are identical.
\end{notation}

\section{The chain, step by step}\label{sec:live-proof-steps}

This section is the ordered chain of implications of the paper.  The headings follow the fifteen Steps of
Section~\ref{sec:live-chain}.  A step marked ``closed'' is either proved in-line below or reduced to a
named classical theorem whose hypotheses are checked at the point of use.  No mathematical assertion
needed by the route is left to an appendix.  The single non-closed input is Step~5, ARP-P.

\subsection{Step 1: variables, completed function, and the RH translation}

Set
\[
   s=\half+iz,\qquad z=\frac{s-\half}{i},\qquad
   \Xi(z)=\xi(\half+iz),
\]
where
\[
   \xi(s)=\half s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s).
\]
Then
\[
   \Re s=\half-\Im z,\qquad
   U_-=\{z:\Im z<-\half\}=\{s:\Re s>1\}.
\]
If $\rho=\beta+i\gamma$ is a nontrivial zero of $\zeta$, the corresponding zero of $\Xi$ is
\[
   z_\rho=\frac{\rho-\half}{i}=\gamma-i(\beta-\half).
\]
The logarithmic derivative coordinate used throughout is
\[
   M_\Xi(z):=-\frac{\Xi'(z)}{\Xi(z)}
   =
   i\left(-\frac{\xi'(s)}{\xi(s)}\right),\qquad s=\half+iz.
\]
The factor $i$ is fixed here and is not a later normalization.  The whole route rests on the following
elementary but decisive translation, which turns a statement about the location of the zeros into a
statement about the real axis.

\begin{lemma}[basic properties of $\Xi$]\label{lem:xi-basic}
The function $\Xi(z)=\xi(\half+iz)$ is entire, and:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item its zero multiset is exactly $\{z_\rho=(\rho-\half)/i\}$, $\rho$ running over the nontrivial
zeros of $\zeta$ with multiplicity; in particular every zero satisfies $|\Im z_\rho|<\half$;
\item $\Xi(-z)=\Xi(z)$;
\item $\Xi(\bar z)=\overline{\Xi(z)}$; in particular $\Xi$ is real on $\R$, and the zero multiset is
invariant under $z\mapsto\bar z$ and $z\mapsto-z$ with multiplicities preserved.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\xi(s)=\half s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)$ is entire: $s-1$ cancels the pole of $\zeta$ at
$s=1$, the poles of $\Gamma(s/2)$ at $s=0,-2,-4,\ldots$ are cancelled by the factor $s$ and by the
trivial zeros of $\zeta$, and $\xi(0)=\xi(1)=\half\ne0$ \cite[Ch.~II]{Titchmarsh}.  Since
$\Gamma(s/2)$ never vanishes and $\zeta(s)\ne0$ for $\Re s\ge1$ (Euler product and the classical
zero-free line), the functional equation $\xi(s)=\xi(1-s)$ excludes zeros in $\Re s\le0$ as well;
hence the zeros of $\xi$ are precisely the nontrivial zeros of $\zeta$, all in $0<\Re s<1$, which is
$|\Im z_\rho|<\half$ in the $z$-coordinate.  This proves (i).  For (ii),
$\Xi(-z)=\xi(\half-iz)=\xi(1-(\half-iz))=\xi(\half+iz)=\Xi(z)$ by the functional equation.  For (iii),
$\xi$ is real on $(1,\infty)$, so Schwarz reflection gives $\xi(\bar s)=\overline{\xi(s)}$; therefore
$\overline{\Xi(z)}=\xi(\half-i\bar z)=\xi(\half+i\bar z)=\Xi(\bar z)$, using the functional equation
once more.  The zero-multiset symmetries follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[RH is realness of the $\Xi$-zeros]\label{lem:rh-translation}
The Riemann Hypothesis is equivalent to the assertion that every nontrivial zero of $\Xi(z)$ is real.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a nontrivial zero $\rho=\beta+i\gamma$ of $\zeta$, the change of variables gives
$z_\rho=(\rho-\half)/i=\gamma-i(\beta-\half)$, and by Lemma~\ref{lem:xi-basic}(i) the zeros of $\Xi$
are exactly the $z_\rho$, with multiplicity.  Hence $z_\rho\in\R$ if and only if $\beta=\half$, so every
zero of $\Xi$ is real precisely when every $\rho$ has $\beta=\half$, which is RH.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Step 2: finite channels and the target \texorpdfstring{$\GXi^F$}{GXiF}}

Steps 2--4 are carried out formally in Definition~\ref{def:core}, Definition~\ref{def:target-channel},
Lemma~\ref{lem:GXi-meromorphic}, Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-admissible}, and Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel};
the surrounding discussion explains their content but the formal statements are authoritative.

\begin{definition}[source core]\label{def:core}
The source core $\cS_a^\circ$ is the set of functions $\phi:(0,\infty)\to\C$ satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(S\arabic*)}]
\item \textnormal{(profile decay)} there is $a_\phi>\half$ such that, for every $N$,
$|\phi(t)|\le C_{N,\phi}\,e^{-a_\phi t}(1+t)^{-N}$;
\item \textnormal{(enlarged-strip transform)} there is $\eps_\phi\in(0,a_\phi-\half)$ such that
\[
   \ell_w(\phi)=\int_0^\infty\phi(t)e^{-iwt}\,dt
\]
is holomorphic on $|\Im w|<\half+\eps_\phi$ and rapidly decreasing horizontally, uniformly on the
closed substrip: for every $N$,
\[
   |\ell_w(\phi)|\le C_{N,\phi}(1+|\Re w|)^{-N},
   \qquad |\Im w|\le\half+\tfrac{\eps_\phi}{2}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Every $\phi\in C_c^\infty(0,\infty)$ belongs to $\cS_a^\circ$ with arbitrary $a_\phi>\half$ and
$\eps_\phi\in(0,a_\phi-\half)$: \textnormal{(S1)} is trivial by compact support, and
\textnormal{(S2)} follows by repeated integration by parts, since
$|e^{-iwt}|\le e^{(\half+\eps_\phi)T_\phi}$ for $0<t\le T_\phi$, where $\supp\phi\subset(0,T_\phi]$
(the constants $C_{N,\phi}$ depend on $T_\phi$).  Note that \textnormal{(S1)} makes
the absolute convergence in \textnormal{(S2)} automatic; the content of \textnormal{(S2)} is the
horizontal rapid decrease.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[realization-layer compatibility]\label{rmk:core-compat}
The conditional realization layer of Section~\textnormal{\ref{sec:shorting}} uses the source core
defined here, with the same notation; the hat always denotes the Fourier transform of Step~4.
\end{remark}

\noindent With this test space fixed, the object the whole chain interpolates is the following explicit
meromorphic matrix function.

\begin{definition}[finite channels and the target $\GXi^F$]\label{def:target-channel}
For a finite source plane $F=\operatorname{span}\{\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m\}\subset\cS_a^\circ$ and sources
$\phi_\alpha,\phi_\beta\in F$, set $\ell_a(\phi)=\int_0^\infty \phi(t)e^{-iat}\,dt$ and define the target
channel
\[
   \GXi^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}
   =
   \sum_\rho
   \frac{
      m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   }{z_\rho-z},
\]
the sum running over the zeros $z_\rho$ of $\Xi$ with multiplicity $m_\rho$.
\end{definition}

\noindent This is not defined only up to a holomorphic summand: the residues, source weights, zero
multiplicities, and denominator convention are all fixed in the formula.

\subsection{Step 3: meromorphy and no holomorphic ambiguity}

Lemma~\ref{lem:GXi-meromorphic} shows that $\GXi^F$ is meromorphic with exactly the displayed principal
parts, so the target carries no holomorphic ambiguity.


\begin{lemma}[target channel meromorphy]\label{lem:GXi-meromorphic}
For every finite source plane $F\subset\cS_a^\circ$, the series \eqref{eq:GXiF} converges locally uniformly
away from the zeros of $\Xi$ and defines a meromorphic matrix function.  At a zero $z_\rho$ its principal
part is
\[
   \operatorname{pp}_{z=z_\rho}\GXi^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}
   =
   \frac{m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}}{z_\rho-z}.
\]
Moreover $\GXi^F(\bar z)=\GXi^F(z)^*$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Definition~\ref{def:core}, source vectors have Laplace transforms with rapid horizontal decay uniformly
on a closed strip containing $|\Im a|\le\half$; compact logarithmic tests have this property by the
Paley--Wiener/integration-by-parts estimate, and the general source core is defined to keep the same
estimate.  Since the zeros of $\Xi$ lie in $|\Im a|<\half$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:xi-basic}(i) and satisfy
$N(T)=O(T\log T)$, for every $N$ and every compact $K$ avoiding the zeros,
\[
   \left|
   \frac{
      \ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   }{z_\rho-z}
   \right|
   \le
   C_{K,F,N}(1+|\Re z_\rho|)^{-N}(1+|z_\rho|)^{-1}.
\]
Choosing $N$ large gives a summable majorant over the zero set.  The only singularities are the displayed
simple poles, with multiplicities inherited from $\Xi$.

Two points deserve record.  First, near a fixed zero $z_{\rho_0}$ the series splits as the displayed
principal part plus the sum over $z_\rho\ne z_{\rho_0}$, which converges locally uniformly on a
punctured disk by the same majorant; hence $z_{\rho_0}$ is a simple pole of the matrix function with
exactly the displayed principal part, and $\GXi^F$ is meromorphic.  Second, the factor
$w\mapsto\overline{\ell_{\bar w}(\phi_\alpha)}$ is itself holomorphic, by the identity
\[
   \overline{\ell_{\bar w}(\phi_\alpha)}
   =\int_0^\infty\overline{\phi_\alpha(t)}\,e^{iwt}\,dt ,
\]
convergent on the same strip by \textnormal{(S1)}.  Finally, the conjugation symmetry of the zero
multiset used in pairing $z_\rho$ with $\bar z_\rho$ in \eqref{eq:GXiF} is
Lemma~\ref{lem:xi-basic}(iii), and it gives $\GXi^F(\bar z)=\GXi^F(z)^*$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Step 4: Weil explicit formula on \texorpdfstring{$U_-$}{U-}}

For $z\in U_-$ the channel entries are values of the Weil functional at explicit admissible tests
(Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-admissible}, Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}).

\begin{lemma}[admissibility of the channel tests]\label{lem:weil-admissible}
Let $z\in U_-$ and $\phi_\alpha,\phi_\beta\in\cS_a^\circ$, and set
$\eps:=\tfrac12\min\{\eps_{\phi_\alpha},\eps_{\phi_\beta},\,|\Im z|-\half\}>0$.  Then
$h_{\alpha\beta,z}(w)=\ell_w(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar w}(\phi_\alpha)}\,(w-z)^{-1}$ is
holomorphic on $|\Im w|\le\half+\eps$ and satisfies there, for every $N$,
\[
   |h_{\alpha\beta,z}(w)|\le C_N(1+|\Re w|)^{-N}.
\]
Consequently: \textnormal{(i)} the pole terms $h_{\alpha\beta,z}(\pm i/2)$ are finite;
\textnormal{(ii)} the archimedean integrals converge absolutely against the logarithmic growth of
$\Gamma'/\Gamma$; \textnormal{(iii)} shifting the contour of
$\widehat h(u)=\int_\R h(t)e^{-iut}\,dt$ to $\Im w=\mp(\half+\eps)$ gives
\[
   |\widehat h(\log n)|+|\widehat h(-\log n)|\le C\,n^{-(\half+\eps)},
\]
so the prime side is dominated by $\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1-\eps}<\infty$ and converges absolutely.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Both Laplace factors are holomorphic and rapidly decreasing on $|\Im w|\le\half+\eps$ by
\textnormal{(S2)} of Definition~\ref{def:core}, the second via
$\overline{\ell_{\bar w}(\phi_\alpha)}=\int_0^\infty\overline{\phi_\alpha(t)}e^{iwt}dt$.  The
denominator satisfies $|w-z|\ge|\Im z|-\half-\eps\ge\eps>0$ on the strip, since
$\Im z\le-\half-2\eps$; hence $h$ is holomorphic there with the displayed decay.  \textnormal{(i)}
follows because $\pm i/2$ is interior to the strip and distinct from $z$.  \textnormal{(ii)} is the
decay against $\log(2+|t|)$.  For \textnormal{(iii)}: for $u=\log n>0$ shift the contour to
$\Im w=-(\half+\eps)$, legal by holomorphy and horizontal decay, so that
$|e^{-iuw}|=e^{u\Im w}=n^{-(\half+\eps)}$; for $u=-\log n$ shift to
$\Im w=+(\half+\eps)$.  In both cases no pole is crossed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[explicit channel formula on $U_-$]\label{thm:explicit-channel}
Let $z\in U_-$ and define, for $\phi_\alpha,\phi_\beta\in F$,
\[
   h_{\alpha\beta,z}(w)
   :=
   \frac{\ell_w(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar w}(\phi_\alpha)}}{w-z}.
\]
Then $h_{\alpha\beta,z}$ is holomorphic in a strip containing all zeros of $\Xi$ and rapidly decreasing
on horizontal lines in that strip, and
\[
   \GXi^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}=\mathcal W_\Xi(h_{\alpha\beta,z}),
\]
where $\mathcal W_\Xi$ is the classical Weil explicit-formula functional
\begin{align*}
   \mathcal W_\Xi(h)
   &=
   h(i/2)+h(-i/2)
   -\frac{\log\pi}{2\pi}\int_\R h(t)\,dt \\
   &\quad
   +\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_\R h(t)\,
      \Re\frac{\Gamma'}{\Gamma}\!\left(\frac14+\frac{it}{2}\right)\,dt
   -\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
      \bigl(\widehat h(\log n)+\widehat h(-\log n)\bigr),
\end{align*}
with Fourier convention
\[
   \widehat h(u)=\int_\R h(t)e^{-iut}\,dt .
\]
The displayed identity is understood in the standard Weil explicit-formula sense for admissible
strip-holomorphic rapidly decreasing tests; equivalently, by inserting the usual exponential smoothing in
the zero and prime sums and then removing the smoothing.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-admissible} makes $h_{\alpha\beta,z}$ an admissible test function for the Weil
explicit formula.  The prime side is the standard continuous extension of the explicit-formula
functional from exponentially smoothed tests: apply the formula first to
$h_{\alpha\beta,z,\varepsilon}(w)=e^{-\varepsilon w^2}h_{\alpha\beta,z}(w)$, where the smoothed sums are absolutely convergent, and let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ in
the Weil test topology.  This is the classical formulation of the explicit formula for admissible
Schwartz strip tests.

By Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-admissible} all three sides of the smoothed identity are dominated, uniformly in
$\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, by convergent majorants: the zero side by the majorant of
Lemma~\ref{lem:GXi-meromorphic}, the archimedean side by \textnormal{(ii)}, and the prime side by
\textnormal{(iii)}, since $|e^{-\varepsilon w^2}|\le e^{\varepsilon(\half+\eta)^2}\le C$ on the strip,
where $\eta$ is the positive strip margin supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-admissible}.
Dominated convergence therefore passes the classical formula \cite{Weil1952} to the limit test
$h_{\alpha\beta,z}$, term by term.

Applying the Weil explicit formula to $h_{\alpha\beta,z}$ gives
\[
   \mathcal W_\Xi(h_{\alpha\beta,z})
   =
   \sum_\rho m_\rho h_{\alpha\beta,z}(z_\rho)
   =
   \sum_\rho
   \frac{
      m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   }{z_\rho-z}
   =
   \GXi^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}.
\]
Equivalently, the Cauchy denominator may be written in $U_-$ as the unilateral Laplace kernel
\[
   \frac1{z_\rho-z}
   =
   -i\int_0^\infty e^{-izt}e^{iz_\rho t}\,dt,
\]
because $\Im z<-\half$ and $|\Im z_\rho|<\half$.  This is the transfer-side representation of the same target value.  It does
not prove ARP; it proves that the ARP target on $U_-$ is a completely specified arithmetic distribution,
with arithmetic side fixed by the Weil formula and no unspecified holomorphic summand.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Step 5: ARP-P}

Step~5, ARP-P, is not proved.  Under RH the Pick matrices are Gram matrices (every $z_\rho$ is real,
so $g^F(z)=\sum_\rho m_\rho v_\rho v_\rho^*/(z_\rho-z)$ with $(v_\rho)_\alpha=\overline{\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}$,
and the $(i,j)$ block of the Pick matrix is
$\sum_\rho m_\rho v_\rho v_\rho^*/((z_\rho-z_i)(z_\rho-\bar z_j))$), so RH $\Rightarrow$ ARP-P is
immediate, and Steps~6--13 prove ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH (Steps~9 and~11 as conditional consequences of
Step~5; see \S\ref{sec:status-map}).  Thus ARP-P is a reformulation of RH and carries its full
difficulty.  Its further reduction (Section~\ref{subsec:omega7-attack}) ends at the terminal-defect
inequality
\[
   \delta_N\ge0\ \ \text{for all }N\qquad(\Omega_7),
\]
which is the classical Li criterion $\lambda_n\ge0$ for all $n$, itself equivalent to RH.  No step of
this section makes progress towards RH.  The quantitative statements below carry additional stated
uniformity hypotheses and are not links in the chain; the remainder of Step~5 records scoped
reductions of $\Omega_7$, none of which proves it.

ARP-P is the assertion that for every finite source plane $F$ and every finite node set
$z_1,\ldots,z_N\in U_-$, the block Pick matrix
\[
   \mathsf P[g^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]
   =
   \left[
      \frac{g^F(z_i)-g^F(z_j)^*}{z_i-\bar z_j}
   \right]_{i,j=1}^N
\]
is positive semidefinite, where $g^F=\GXi^F|_{U_-}$.  The rest of the paper proves that this
assertion is equivalent to RH and decomposes it into lower-dimensional regimes, some proved, some
open.


\begin{definition}[arithmetic channel data]\label{def:arith-data}
For a finite source plane $F\subset\cS_a^\circ$ define
\[
   g^F(z):=\GXi^F(z),\qquad z\in U_-.
\]
The values are computed through Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}: for each $z\in U_-$,
$g^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}$ is the Weil explicit-formula functional applied to the admissible test
$h_{\alpha\beta,z}$.  Thus the arithmetic side is fixed by the archimedean, pole/degree, and prime terms,
with the standard Weil smoothing convention; no unspecified holomorphic summand remains.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[ARP-P: arithmetic Pick positivity]\label{def:ARP-P}
The channel data satisfy \emph{arithmetic Pick positivity} (ARP-P) if, for every finite source plane
$F=\operatorname{span}\{\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m\}\subset\cS_a^\circ$ and every finite node set
$\{z_1,\ldots,z_N\}\subset U_-$, the block Pick matrix
\[
   \mathsf P[g^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]
   :=
   \left[
      \frac{g^F(z_i)-g^F(z_j)^*}{z_i-\bar z_j}
   \right]_{i,j=1}^{N}
   \in M_{Nm}(\C)
\]
is positive semidefinite.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Step 5 substructure: proved subcases and open problems}

This subsection descends one level in the logical hierarchy of \S\ref{sec:status-map}: from the
fifteen-step chain into the internal H-level analysis (H0--H8) of the single open Step.  Nothing here is
a Step of the chain; everything here lives inside Step~5.

Step~5 is decomposed as follows:
\[
   \textnormal{ARP-P}
   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   \textnormal{positive arithmetic Pick kernels for all finite channels}
\]
\[
   \textnormal{and all finite node sets in }U_-.
\]
The items below are statements about Step~5; those marked proved are proved here, the others are
stated as open problems.

\subsubsection*{H0: the open hypothesis}

Let $S_1,\ldots,S_k$ be assertions.  If
\[
   S_1\wedge\cdots\wedge S_k\Longrightarrow\mathrm{RH}
\]
and every $S_j$ is proved unconditionally from standard theorems, then RH is proved by modus ponens.
Therefore, in any chain of proved implications ending in RH, at least one hypothesis is unproved; here
it is Step~5, which is equivalent to RH (Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}).

\subsubsection*{H1: one-node Pick positivity of the scalar coordinate $M_\Xi$}

Here $M_\Xi$ is not a channel function $g^F$ (Notation~\ref{not:channels}); H1--H3 concern the scalar
coordinate $M_\Xi=-\Xi'/\Xi$, the formal unweighted analogue of $g^F$, and are not special cases of
Definition~\ref{def:ARP-P}.  For the scalar function
\[
   M_\Xi(z)=i\left(-\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)\right),\qquad s=\half+iz,
\]
the one-node Pick quotient is
\[
   \frac{M_\Xi(z)-M_\Xi(z)^*}{z-\bar z}
   =
   \frac{\Im M_\Xi(z)}{\Im z}.
\]
Writing $s=\sigma+it$ and $z=x+iy$, one has $y=-(\sigma-\half)$.  Since
$M_\Xi=i(-\xi'/\xi)$,
\[
   \Im M_\Xi(z)=-\Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s).
\]
Thus
\[
   \frac{\Im M_\Xi(z)}{\Im z}
   =
   \frac{-\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)}{-(\sigma-\half)}
   =
   \frac{\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)}{\sigma-\half}.
\]
The scalar one-node Pick condition on $U_-$ is therefore equivalent to
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)\ge0,\qquad \Re s>1,
\]
because $\sigma-\half>0$.

The inequality is classical.  From the Hadamard product for $\xi$, with the standard real normalization
of the constant term,
\[
   \frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)
   =
   \sum_\rho\left(\frac1{s-\rho}+\frac1\rho\right)+B,
\]
and after pairing zeros symmetrically, the real parts of the constant terms cancel in the usual limiting
sense.  Hence, for $s=\sigma+it$,
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)
   =
   \sum_\rho\frac{\sigma-\beta}{|s-\rho|^2}.
\]
The classical zero-free line and functional equation give $0<\beta<1$ for nontrivial zeros.  Therefore
for $\sigma>1$ every summand is nonnegative, which proves H1.

\subsubsection*{H2 (proved separated-node criteria; no compactness extension)}

Fix a vertical band
\[
   1<\sigma_0\le\sigma\le\sigma_1<\infty.
\]
For scalar nodes $s_j=\sigma_j+it_j$ in this band, let $z_j=(s_j-\half)/i$ and form the scalar Pick
matrix
\[
   K_{ij}
   =
   \frac{M_\Xi(z_i)-\overline{M_\Xi(z_j)}}{z_i-\bar z_j}.
\]
There are effective constants $a_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1},B_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}>0$ such that, for all
sufficiently large heights in the band,
\[
   K_{ii}\ge a_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}\log(3+|t_i|),
\]
and for $i\ne j$,
\[
   |K_{ij}|
   \le
   B_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}
   \frac{\log(3+|t_i|)+\log(3+|t_j|)}
        {\max\{|t_i-t_j|,\,2(\sigma_0-\half)\}}.
\]
The diagonal bound is H1 plus Stirling's formula for the $\Gamma'/\Gamma$ term and the boundedness of
$\zeta'/\zeta$ in $\sigma\ge\sigma_0$.  The off-diagonal bound follows from
$|M_\Xi(z_i)|\ll_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}\log(3+|t_i|)$ and
\[
   |z_i-\bar z_j|\ge\max\{|t_i-t_j|,\,2(\sigma_0-\half)\}.
\]
Therefore, if
\[
   \sum_{j\ne i}
   \frac{\log(3+|t_i|)+\log(3+|t_j|)}
        {\max\{|t_i-t_j|,\,2(\sigma_0-\half)\}}
   \le
   \frac{a_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}}{2B_{\sigma_0,\sigma_1}}\log(3+|t_i|)
\]
for every $i$, then
\[
   \sum_{j\ne i}|K_{ij}|\le\frac12 K_{ii}.
\]
The matrix is Hermitian, so Gershgorin's theorem implies that it is positive semidefinite.  This closes
H2 as a verifiable separated-node criterion.

There is no automatic extension from this high-node estimate to a compact set of bounded heights.
Continuity on a compact parameter set only ensures that the smallest eigenvalue attains a minimum; it
does not determine the sign of that minimum.  Positive diagonal entries are likewise insufficient for
positive semidefiniteness.  At bounded heights the valid criterion is the exact Gershgorin condition
\[
   \sum_{j\ne i}|K_{ij}|\le K_{ii}\qquad\text{for every }i,
\]
or any other independently proved control of the principal minors.

A proved concrete subcase is geometric separation.  If, for some fixed $Q>1$ and after ordering so
that $|t_1|<|t_2|<\cdots$,
\[
   |t_j|\ge T_*Q^j,\qquad
   |t_i-t_j|\ge\tfrac12\max\{|t_i|,|t_j|\}\quad(i\ne j),
\]
then
\[
   \sum_{j<i}|K_{ij}|
   \le
   C_1\frac{\log(3+|t_i|)}{|t_i|}\#\{j<i\}
   \le
   C_2\frac{(\log(3+|t_i|))^2}{|t_i|},
\]
while
\[
   \sum_{j>i}|K_{ij}|
   \le
   C_3\sum_{j>i}\frac{\log(3+|t_j|)}{|t_j|}
   \le
   C_4\frac{\log(3+|t_i|)}{|t_i|}.
\]
Here $\#\{j<i\}\le\log(|t_i|/T_*)/\log Q$, because $T_*Q^j\le|t_j|<|t_i|$ for $j<i$; for $j>i$ the
separation condition forces $|t_{j'}|\ge2|t_j|$ for consecutive nodes $t_j,t_{j'}$ ($j<j'$) of the same
sign, so (using that $x\mapsto\log(3+x)/x$ is decreasing for $x\ge3$, and
$\log(3+2^kx)\le k\log2+\log(3+x)$) the second sum is at most
$2\sum_{k\ge0}(k\log2+\log(3+|t_i|))/(2^k|t_i|)=(4\log2+4\log(3+|t_i|))/|t_i|\le C_4\log(3+|t_i|)/|t_i|$.
For $T_*$ large (in particular larger than the height beyond which the two displayed bounds on $K_{ii}$
and $K_{ij}$ hold) these row sums are smaller than $\frac12K_{ii}$.  Hence geometrically separated high
nodes give a proved positive scalar Pick subcase.

\subsubsection*{H3 (proved in a logarithmic region and almost everywhere): infinitesimal two-node inequality}

The scalar coincident two-node limit is the Schwarz--Pick inequality
\[
   \left(\sigma-\half\right)
   \left|\left(\frac{\xi'}{\xi}\right)'(s)\right|
   \le
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s),
   \qquad \sigma=\Re s>1.
\]
It is not closed globally.  Two substantial regions are closed.

\emph{Deterministic logarithmic region.}  There exist effective constants $c>0$ and $T_0\ge3$ such that
the inequality holds whenever
\[
   \sigma\ge1+\frac{c}{\sqrt{\log(3+|t|)}}
\]
and $|t|\ge T_0$.  The proof splits into two regions.  In the band $1+\epsilon\le\sigma\le2$,
$0<\epsilon\le1$, the absolutely convergent Dirichlet series gives
\[
   \left|\left(\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}\right)'(s)\right|
   \le
   \sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)(\log n)n^{-1-\epsilon}
   \le C_1\epsilon^{-2}+C_2.
\]
The elementary factors satisfy
\[
   \left|\frac14\psi'(s/2)-s^{-2}-(s-1)^{-2}\right|
   \le C_3(1+|t|)^{-1},
\]
and therefore
\[
   \left(\sigma-\half\right)
   \left|\left(\frac{\xi'}{\xi}\right)'(s)\right|
   \le C_4\epsilon^{-2}+C_5.
\]
On the other hand, Stirling plus
$\Re(\zeta'/\zeta)(s)\ge-\sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)n^{-1-\epsilon}\ge-C_6\epsilon^{-1}-C_7$
gives
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)
   \ge
   \frac12\log\frac{|t|}{2\pi}-C_6\epsilon^{-1}-C_7.
\]
Choosing $c$ large enough and then $T_0$ large enough makes the right side dominate the left when
$\epsilon\ge c/\sqrt{\log(3+|t|)}$.

For $\sigma\ge2$, the Euler derivative has exponential decay:
\[
   \left|\left(\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}\right)'(s)\right|
   \le
   2^{-(\sigma-2)}\sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)(\log n)n^{-2}
   \le C_8\,2^{-\sigma}.
\]
Stirling gives $|\frac14\psi'(s/2)|\le(2|s|)^{-1}+O(|s|^{-2})$, so
\[
   \left(\sigma-\half\right)
   \left|\left(\frac{\xi'}{\xi}\right)'(s)\right|
   \le C_9\sigma2^{-\sigma}+\frac12+O(|s|^{-1}).
\]
For large $\sigma$ and $|t|$ this is $<1$, while
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)\ge\frac12\log\frac{|s|}{2\pi}-O(1)\ge1.
\]
The finite band $2\le\sigma\le\sigma_2$ is absorbed by the same fixed-band estimates as above.  This
closes the logarithmic region.

\emph{Almost-everywhere edge region.}  There are effective $C,T_1>0$ such that for
$T\ge T_1$ and $\epsilon\in[T^{-1/5},1]$, the two-node inequality holds at
$s=1+\epsilon+it$ for all $t\in[T,2T]$ outside a set (depending on $\epsilon$) of measure at most
$CT(\log T)^{-2}$.  In the band $1<\sigma\le2$, it is enough to have
\[
   \tfrac32\left|\left(\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}\right)'(1+\epsilon+it)\right|
   +
   \left|\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(1+\epsilon+it)\right|
   \le \tfrac14\log T.
\]
The Montgomery--Vaughan mean-value theorem \cite{MontgomeryVaughanMVT},
$\int_T^{2T}|\sum a_nn^{-it}|^2dt=\sum|a_n|^2(T+O(n))$ whenever $\sum n|a_n|^2<\infty$, gives, uniformly in
$\epsilon\in[T^{-1/5},1]$,
\[
   \int_T^{2T}\left|\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(1+\epsilon+it)\right|^2dt\le C_1T,
\]
and
\[
   \int_T^{2T}
   \left|\left(\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}\right)'(1+\epsilon+it)\right|^2dt\le C_2T.
\]
The square sums
$\sum\Lambda(n)^2n^{-2-2\epsilon}$ and
$\sum\Lambda(n)^2(\log n)^2n^{-2-2\epsilon}$ remain bounded at $\epsilon=0$, and the
Montgomery--Vaughan error terms $\sum n|a_n|^2$ are $\ll\epsilon^{-2}$, respectively
$\ll\epsilon^{-4}\le T^{4/5}$, hence absorbed in the range $\epsilon\ge T^{-1/5}$.  Chebyshev's
inequality then gives, for each fixed $\epsilon$, an exceptional set of measure $O(T(\log T)^{-2})$.

\subsubsection*{H4: determinant serialization}

The statement proved is Theorem~\ref{thm:det-serialization}: after fixing the dense detection family
$\{\phi_k\}$ and a nested node sequence $\{z_j\}\subset U_-$,
\[
   \mathrm{RH}\Longleftrightarrow d_N^{(k)}>0\quad\text{for all }k,N.
\]
Thus H4 is a countable determinant serialization of RH.  For each finite channel $F$, the Schur--Nevanlinna algorithm applied to the finite
data
\[
   g^F(z_1),\ldots,g^F(z_N)
\]
produces contractivity parameters $\gamma_N^F$ whenever the preceding Pick matrices are positive
definite.  If all Pick matrices along a node sequence are invertible, positivity of these matrices is
equivalent to $\|\gamma_N^F\|<1$ for all $N$ (classical Schur--Nevanlinna theory; not used and not
proved here); the statement proved in this paper is
Theorem~\ref{thm:det-serialization}.  Degenerate Pick matrices must be handled by the degenerate Nevanlinna--Pick theory, equivalently by
Moore--Penrose Schur complements plus the range condition.  In scalar nondegenerate normalization one
expects
\[
   1-|\gamma_N|^2
   =
   C_N(z_1,\ldots,z_{N+1})
   \frac{\det P_{N+1}\det P_{N-1}}{(\det P_N)^2},
   \qquad C_N>0,
\]
with the geometric constant fixed by the chosen Cayley normalization (this formula is not used and is
not proved here).  Theorem~\ref{thm:det-serialization} is the governing proved statement: the Schur parameters are the same
serialization in recursive coordinates, while $d_N^{(k)}>0$ avoids any dependence on a chosen
normalization constant.

\subsubsection*{H5 (proved equivalence): windowed Weil positivity \texorpdfstring{$\Longleftrightarrow$}{<=>} ARP-P}

For $z\in U_-$ the correct unilateral kernel is
\[
   \frac1{z_\rho-z}=-i\int_0^\infty e^{-izt}e^{iz_\rho t}\,dt,
\]
because $\Im z<-\half$ and $|\Im z_\rho|<\half$.  Write formally
\[
   g^F(z)=-i\int_0^\infty e^{-izt}\psi^F(t)\,dt,
   \qquad
   \psi^F(t)_{\alpha\beta}
   =
   \sum_\rho
   m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)
   \overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   e^{iz_\rho t},
\]
and extend $\psi^F(-\tau)=\psi^F(\tau)^*$.  For compactly supported smooth vector tests
$f:[0,\infty)\to\C^m$, define
\[
   Q^F(f)=
   \iint_{[0,\infty)^2}f(t')^{\mathsf T}\,\psi^F(t'-t)\,\overline{f(t)}\,dt\,dt',
\]
and write $K(z,w):=(g^F(z)-g^F(w)^*)/(z-\bar w)$ for $z,w\in U_-$, so that
$\mathsf P[g^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]=[K(z_i,z_j)]_{i,j}$.  (The orientation of $Q^F$ matters for $m\ge2$:
the form $\iint f(t)^*\psi^F(t'-t)f(t')$ produces the block matrix $[K(z_j,z_i)]_{i,j}$, whose blocks
are the adjoints of those of the Pick matrix, and which for $m\ge2$ need not have the same spectrum.)
Then the following are equivalent:
\[
   \mathsf P[g^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]\succeq0
   \quad\text{for all finite node sets in }U_-,
\]
and
\[
   Q^F(f)\ge0\quad\text{for every compactly supported smooth window test }f.
\]

\begin{lemma}[transfer kernel identity]\label{lem:transfer-kernel}
For $z,w\in U_-$,
\[
   \iint_{[0,\infty)^2}\overline{e^{-iwt}}\,\psi^F(t'-t)\,e^{-izt'}\,dt\,dt'
   =\frac{g^F(z)-g^F(w)^*}{z-\bar w}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Insert $\psi^F(\tau)=\sum_\rho m_\rho W_\rho\,e^{iz_\rho\tau}$ with
$W_\rho=\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}$:
\[
   \int_0^\infty e^{i(\bar w-z_\rho)t}\,dt=\frac{-i}{z_\rho-\bar w},
   \qquad
   \int_0^\infty e^{i(z_\rho-z)t'}\,dt'=\frac{i}{z_\rho-z},
\]
both convergent since $|\Im z_\rho|<\half<-\Im z,-\Im w$; the product is
$m_\rho W_\rho/((z_\rho-z)(z_\rho-\bar w))$, summable by Lemma~\ref{lem:psi-growth}, and equals the
displayed quotient by Lemma~\ref{lem:cauchy-pick}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[kernel orientation]\label{rmk:kernel-orientation}
The identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:transfer-kernel} requires the orientation $\psi^F(t'-t)$.  With
$\psi^F(t-t')$ the same double integral equals $K(-\bar w,-\bar z)$, the kernel at the reflected nodes
with its arguments exchanged.  For $m=1$ this gives the complex conjugate of the Pick matrix at the
reflected nodes $\{-\bar z_j\}$, so the equivalence is unaffected; for $m\ge2$ it gives the
block-swapped matrix $[K(-\bar z_j,-\bar z_i)]_{i,j}$, and the equivalence with ARP-P is not asserted
for that convention.
\end{remark}

The key representation is Laplace inversion along a horizontal line.  Fix $y_0>\half$ and
$z(x)=x-iy_0$.  If $f\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty);\C^m)$, set
\[
   c(x)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_0^\infty \overline{f(t)}\,e^{-i\overline{z(x)}t}\,dt
        =\frac1{2\pi}\int_0^\infty \overline{f(t)}\,e^{y_0t}e^{-ixt}\,dt.
\]
Since $f(t)e^{y_0t}$ is compactly supported smooth, $c$ is Schwartz and Fourier inversion gives
\[
   f(t)=\int_\R \overline{c(x)}\,e^{-iz(x)t}\,dx .
\]

If window positivity holds, apply it to truncated functions
\[
   f_L(t)=\eta_L(t)\sum_j \bar c_j e^{-iz_jt},
\]
where $\eta_L$ is a smooth cutoff, $=1$ on $[0,L-1]$ and $=0$ on $[L,\infty)$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:transfer-kernel}
and dominated convergence (majorant from Lemma~\ref{lem:psi-growth}) give
\[
   \lim_{L\to\infty}Q^F(f_L)
   =
   \sum_{i,j}c_i^*K(z_i,z_j)c_j=c^*\mathsf P[g^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]\,c,
\]
so all Pick matrices are positive.  Conversely, if all Pick matrices are positive, use the horizontal-line
representation of $f$ and Fubini:
\[
   Q^F(f)=
   \iint_{\R^2}c(x)^*K(z(x),z(x'))c(x')\,dx\,dx'.
\]
Every Riemann sum of this integral is nonnegative by finite Pick positivity; hence the integral is
nonnegative.  This proves $Q^F(f)\ge0$ for $f\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty);\C^m)$.  Since $\psi^F$ is
bounded on compact sets (Lemma~\ref{lem:psi-growth}), $Q^F$ is continuous on $L^2([0,L];\C^m)$ for each
$L$, and $C_c^\infty((0,L);\C^m)$ is dense there; hence $Q^F(f)\ge0$ for every compactly supported
smooth $f:[0,\infty)\to\C^m$, in particular for the $f_L$.  This proves the equivalence.  Thus window positivity for all tests is equivalent to ARP-P, hence to RH
(Theorems~\ref{thm:pick-rh} and~\ref{thm:rh-pick}).

\subsubsection*{H6 (qualitative witness proved; quantitative tail domination open)}

If RH fails, then ARP-P fails for some finite source plane and some finite node set.  This is the
contrapositive of ARP-P $\Rightarrow$ RH (Steps~6--13):
\[
   \textnormal{ARP-P}\Longrightarrow\textnormal{RH}.
\]
Equivalently, an off-line zero forces a finite Pick matrix with a negative eigenvalue.  The qualitative
statement holds as far as Steps~6--13 are proved.  Proposition~\ref{prop:H6-witness}, Lemma~\ref{lem:H6-phase}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:H6-closed} give a phase-adjusted rational Rayleigh bound for each single-cluster
configuration.  They do not by themselves prove that the negative local term dominates the far-zero
tail.  The domination of the far-zero tail by the negative local term (the strict inequality
under which the witness of Theorem~\ref{thm:H6-closed} is negative) is the remaining quantitative target.  The
local obstruction block has vector
\[
   v_i=\frac1{z_\rho-z_i},
\]
and the first quantitative target is a single-cluster estimate.

\subsubsection*{H7: two-node determinants and the Andreief form}

Under RH, for a scalar channel $F=\C\phi$ one has $g(z)=\int d\Sigma(t)/(t-z)$ with
$\Sigma=\sum_\rho m_\rho|\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi)|^2\delta_{z_\rho}$, so the Pick entries are
$\int\phi_{z_i}\overline{\phi_{z_j}}\,d\Sigma$ and the two-node Pick determinant has the positive
Andreief form
\[
   \det P_2
   =
   \frac12
   \iint
   \left|
      \phi_{z_1}(t)\phi_{z_2}(t')
      -
      \phi_{z_1}(t')\phi_{z_2}(t)
   \right|^2
   d\Sigma(t)d\Sigma(t'),
   \qquad
   \phi_z(t)=\frac1{t-z}.
\]
The integrand is an antisymmetrized Cauchy/Vandermonde square and vanishes on the diagonal $t=t'$.  The
open H7 target is to express the corresponding arithmetic two-point form, after pole shorting, as a
manifestly nonnegative sum of squares.  Corollaries~\ref{cor:H7-nondegenerate} and
\ref{cor:below-threshold} prove two-node Pick positivity of the scalar coordinate $M_\Xi$ (not of a
channel function $g^F$) in the nondegenerate and fixed coalescent large-height regimes; no two-node case
of ARP-P for a channel $g^F$ is proved, and the sum-of-squares certification is open.  The remaining SOS target is the multiscale/arithmetically degenerate certification not covered by
single-cluster positivity.  This is not a restatement of H1: it tests the antisymmetrized two-point
correlation.

\subsubsection*{H8: fixed-$N$ scalar clusters and terminal gauges}

For scalar node clusters of fixed pattern, H8 holds in the large-height regime by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cluster}.  If
\[
   z_j=t+a_j-iy_j,\qquad y_j\ge\half+\epsilon_0,
\]
and the pairs $(a_j,y_j)$ are distinct and fixed, then the scalar Pick matrix of $M_\Xi$ is positive
for all sufficiently large $|t|$.  The proof isolates the Szeg\H{o} Gram matrix
\[
   S_{ij}=\frac{-i}{z_i-\bar z_j},
\]
whose smallest eigenvalue supplies the cluster margin, while the archimedean contribution grows like
$\log |t|\,S$ and the prime contribution is bounded in the fixed band $\Re s\ge1+\epsilon_0$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-explicit} evaluates $\det S$ by the Cauchy determinant formula, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:cluster-quantitative} makes this uniform over boxes:
\[
   \prod_{i<j}|w_i-w_j|^2\gtrsim\frac1{\log |t|}
   \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
   \mathsf P[M_\Xi;z_1,\ldots,z_N]\succ0.
\]
Thus a cluster with minimum pairwise gap $h$ is covered down to
$h\gtrsim(\log|t|)^{-1/(N(N-1))}$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:one-point-jets} gives the complementary exact serialization: for any fixed
