\begin{remark}[logical type of the terminal inequality]\label{rmk:terminal-status}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:minimal-input}, the exact terminal condition in
Definition~\ref{def:terminal-inequality} for one accumulating scalar sequence is equivalent to RH.
The displayed norm inequality is a useful sufficient quantitative form, not an equivalent reformulation
unless the norm loss is removed.  The left side is governed by the Newton--Szeg\H{o} lower bound
$c_N^{\mathcal S}$; the right side is governed by divided differences of the prime kernel.  The crude
Cauchy estimate grows exponentially in $N$, while the confluent Szeg\H{o}/Laguerre constants may decay
exponentially.  The remaining essential problem is therefore cancellation in the prime divided
differences strong enough to control this race uniformly in $N$ for a fixed height.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[sequence independence of the terminal input]\label{thm:sequence-gauge}
Let $\{z_j\}$ and $\{z_j'\}$ be two pairwise distinct node sequences contained in interior boxes of
$U_-$, each with an accumulation point in its box.  Then
\[
   \bigl[\mathsf P[M_\Xi;z_1,\ldots,z_N]\succeq0\ \textnormal{for every }N\bigr]
   \Longleftrightarrow
   \bigl[\mathsf P[M_\Xi;z_1',\ldots,z_N']\succeq0\ \textnormal{for every }N\bigr]
   \Longleftrightarrow
   \mathrm{RH}.
\]
Thus the choice of an accumulating sequence is a coordinate gauge for attacking the terminal input, not
a weakening of its mathematical content.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Prefix positivity for either sequence implies RH by Theorem~\ref{thm:minimal-input}, applied to the
corresponding box and accumulation point.  Conversely, RH implies scalar Pick positivity for every finite
node set by Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel}(iv).  Applying this to all prefixes of
either sequence proves the equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[effective Pascal--Laguerre floor]\label{lem:pascal-effective}
Let
\[
   P_N=\left[\binom{j+k}{j}\right]_{j,k=0}^{N-1},
   \qquad
   L_N=\left[\binom{j}{k}\right]_{0\le k\le j<N}.
\]
Then $P_N=L_NL_N^*$ and
\[
   \|L_N\|^2\le4^N,\qquad \|L_N^{-1}\|^2\le4^N,
\]
hence
\[
   4^{-N}\le\lambda_{\min}(P_N)\le\lambda_{\max}(P_N)\le4^N .
\]
Consequently the Szeg\H{o} jet Gram matrix of Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet} satisfies
\[
   \lambda_N^{\mathrm{jet}}(y)\ge4^{-N}(2y)^{-(2N-1)},\qquad y\ge\half .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Cholesky identity is Vandermonde convolution:
\[
   \binom{j+k}{j}=\sum_{r\ge0}\binom{j}{r}\binom{k}{r}.
\]
The inverse of the lower binomial triangle is the signed lower binomial triangle,
$(L_N^{-1})_{jk}=(-1)^{j-k}\binom{j}{k}$.  The row and column sums of $|L_N|$ and $|L_N^{-1}|$ are
bounded by powers of $2$; in particular
\[
   \|L_N\|^2\le\|L_N\|_1\|L_N\|_\infty\le4^N,
   \qquad
   \|L_N^{-1}\|^2\le4^N .
\]
Therefore
$\lambda_{\max}(P_N)=\|L_N\|^2\le4^N$ and
$\lambda_{\min}(P_N)=\|L_N^{-1}\|^{-2}\ge4^{-N}$.

By Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet}, after removing unimodular phase factors the $N$-jet Szeg\H{o} Gram is
$D_yP_ND_y$, where $D_y=\diag((2y)^{-j-1/2})_{j=0}^{N-1}$.  For $y\ge\half$,
$\min_j(D_y)_{jj}=(2y)^{-N+1/2}$, so
\[
   \lambda_{\min}(D_yP_ND_y)
   \ge (2y)^{-2N+1}\lambda_{\min}(P_N)
   \ge4^{-N}(2y)^{-(2N-1)}.
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[archimedean whitening in its positive range]\label{thm:whitened-terminal}
Fix $z_0=t_0-iy\in U_-$ and an integer $N$ such that the archimedean jet matrix
$J_N^\infty(z_0)$ is positive definite.  Define
\[
   T_N(z_0):=-
   \bigl(J_N^\infty(z_0)\bigr)^{-1/2}
   J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}(z_0)
   \bigl(J_N^\infty(z_0)\bigr)^{-1/2},
\]
where $J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}(z_0)$ is the jet matrix of the prime part $K_{\mathrm{prime}}$ of the scalar
kernel, so that $J_N=J_N^\infty+J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}$.  Then
\[
   J_N(z_0)=J_N^\infty(z_0)+J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}(z_0)\succeq0
   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   \lambda_{\max}(T_N(z_0))\le1 .
\]
In this range set
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0)
   :=\min_{c\ne0}\frac{c^*J_N(z_0)c}{c^*J_N^\infty(z_0)c}
   =1-\lambda_{\max}(T_N(z_0)).
\]
Consequently, for every $N\le N_*(t_0)$ in the range where $J_N^\infty(z_0)\succ0$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair}), $J_N(z_0)\succeq0$ is equivalent to $\lambda_{\max}(T_N(z_0))\le1$; the
reference-whitened observable of Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed} carries the equivalence with
RH to \emph{all} $N$ without restriction.  No archimedean-whitened operator is asserted outside the
positive-definite range of $J_N^\infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is congruence by the invertible matrix
$(J_N^\infty)^{-1/2}$:
\[
   J_N\succeq0
   \Longleftrightarrow
   I+(J_N^\infty)^{-1/2}J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}(J_N^\infty)^{-1/2}=I-T_N\succeq0.
\]
Since the displayed matrix is Hermitian, this is equivalent to
$\lambda_{\max}(T_N)\le1$.  The restriction on $N$ is exactly the condition under which the congruence
is defined.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[pole-pair obstruction in the archimedean gauge]\label{lem:pole-pair}
Write the archimedean scalar as $g_\infty=g_{\rm arch}+g_{\rm pole}$, where
$g_{\rm pole}(z)=\frac1{i/2-z}+\frac1{-i/2-z}$ is the contribution of the trivial factor $s(s-1)$ (each
pole $\pm i/2$ carrying weight $+1$, since $i(-\tfrac1s-\tfrac1{s-1})=\tfrac1{i/2-z}+\tfrac1{-i/2-z}$).
The pole pair has zero boundary density on $\R$ --- so the archimedean weight $w_A$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:signed-arch} does not see it --- but it is an interior conjugate pair for every gauge
$z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y>0$ and $z_0\ne-\tfrac i2$, with exterior Blaschke modulus
\[
   |b(-i/2)|=\Bigl(\tfrac{t_0^2+(y+1/2)^2}{t_0^2+(y-1/2)^2}\Bigr)^{1/2}
   =1+\tfrac{y}{t_0^2}+O(t_0^{-4}).
\]
Consequently the archimedean jet matrix $J_N^\infty(z_0)$ cannot be positive semidefinite for every
$N$.  Let $N_*(t_0)$ denote the largest order preceding the first failure of
strict positivity.  Then $J_N^\infty(z_0)\succ0$ for $N\le N_*(t_0)$ by definition, and
$N_*(t_0)<\infty$.  The growth of $N_*(t_0)$ is not determined here; the Blaschke modulus suggests the
scale $y^{-1}t_0^2\log t_0$, which is a heuristic.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The archimedean kernel $(g_\infty(z)-\overline{g_\infty(w)})/(z-\bar w)$ is
holomorphic--antiholomorphic near $(z_0,z_0)$ whenever $z_0\ne-\tfrac i2$ (the remaining poles of
$g_\infty$, coming from the Gamma factor, lie in the upper half-plane).  If all its jet sections at
$z_0$ were positive semidefinite, the Taylor argument in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}
would give Pick positivity of $g_\infty$ at every finite node set in a disk
$D_r(z_0)\subset\Omega_-\setminus\{-\tfrac i2\}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:prefix-extension} (stated for a
general meromorphic $G$ on $\Omega_-$), applied to a pairwise distinct node sequence accumulating in
this disk, would extend $g_\infty$ holomorphically to $\Omega_-$.  This contradicts the pole of
$g_\infty$ at $-\tfrac i2\in\Omega_-$, whose residue is $-1$.  Hence some jet section fails to be
positive semidefinite, and $N_*(t_0)$ is finite.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[terminal defect, reference-whitened]\label{def:terminal-defect-fixed}
For $z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y\ge\half$, let $J_N(z_0)=[C_{jk}(z_0)]_{j,k<N}$ be the scalar jet matrix of
Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel}(iii) and $G_N(z_0)$ the reference Gram of Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet},
positive definite for every $y>0$, in particular for $y\ge\half$.  Set
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0):=\min_{c\ne0}\,\frac{c^*J_N(z_0) c}{c^*G_N(z_0) c}.
\]
Since $G_N(z_0)\succ0$ for every $N$ and every such $z_0$, immediately
$\delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)\ge0\iff J_N(z_0)\succeq0$, at \emph{every} gauge $z_0=t_0-iy$, $y\ge\half$, without
restriction on $t_0$ --- in particular at the Li gauge $z_0=-\tfrac i2$ ($t_0=0$, $y=\half$).  Hence
$\delta_N^{\rm ref}\ge0\ \forall N\iff J_N\succeq0\ \forall N\iff$ RH (Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}, at any
$z_0$ satisfying its distance hypothesis; see Remark~\ref{rmk:defect-scope}), for
\emph{all} $N$ without restriction.

In global statements below, an unadorned $\delta_N$ is only a shorthand for
$\delta_N^{\rm ref}$.  The superscript $\mathrm{arch}$ is always written for the distinct,
finite-range observable of Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect}.

The sign-defect is deliberately kept separate from asymptotic scaling: where a height-normalized
magnitude is wanted (the large-height asymptotics of Theorem~\ref{thm:cluster} and the sampling
theorems), use the rescaled variant
\[
   \widetilde\delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0):=\frac{\delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)}{L},\qquad L=\log\tfrac{|t_0|}{2\pi},
\]
defined only in the regime $L>0$ (i.e.\ $|t_0|>2\pi$), where it has the same sign as $\delta_N$; there
the two normalizations differ by the positive factor $L$, so signs and orders of magnitude in $N$
agree.  The $J_N^\infty$-whitened coordinate $T_N$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal} is equivalent to this one exactly in the range $N\le N_*(t_0)$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair} only at the level of the sign condition $J_N\succeq0$ and is used only there;
the two defects are not identified numerically.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[global reference-whitened domination]\label{thm:reference-whitened-terminal}
For every $z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y\ge\half$ and every $N\ge1$, define
\[
   D_N^{\rm ref}(z_0):=G_N(z_0)-J_N(z_0),
   \qquad
   R_N^{\rm ref}(z_0):=
   G_N(z_0)^{-1/2}D_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)G_N(z_0)^{-1/2}.
\]
Then
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)
   =1-\lambda_{\max}\!\left(R_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)\right),
\]
and the following conditions are equivalent:
\[
\begin{aligned}
   \delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)&\ge0,\\
   J_N(z_0)&\succeq0,\\
   R_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)&\preceq I.
\end{aligned}
\]
This reference-whitened equivalence holds for all $N$.  In the range
$J_N^\infty(z_0)\succ0$, Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal} supplies a second, archimedean-whitened
representation of the same sign condition; it does not extend beyond that range.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since $G_N\succ0$,
\[
   G_N^{-1/2}J_NG_N^{-1/2}
   =I-G_N^{-1/2}(G_N-J_N)G_N^{-1/2}
   =I-R_N^{\rm ref}.
\]
The generalized Rayleigh quotient in Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed} is therefore
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm ref}
   =\lambda_{\min}(G_N^{-1/2}J_NG_N^{-1/2})
   =1-\lambda_{\max}(R_N^{\rm ref}).
\]
Congruence by the invertible matrix $G_N^{-1/2}$ preserves inertia, which proves all three
equivalences.  No property of $J_N^\infty$ is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[exact scope of the admissible gauge]\label{rmk:defect-scope}
Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed} defines $\delta_N(z_0)$ for every $z_0=t_0-iy$ with
$y\ge\half$ (the reference Gram $G_N(z_0)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet} is positive definite for
every $y>0$; the restriction $y\ge\half$ comes from the Pick domain).  The equivalence
$\delta_N(z_0)\ge0\ \forall N\iff\mathrm{RH}$ used in
Corollary~\ref{cor:arp-terminal-defect} additionally requires the distance hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}, namely $d_0(z_0):=\operatorname{dist}(z_0,Z(\Xi))>0$.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(a)--(b) this holds at \emph{every} such gauge: for $y>\half$ it holds
because $Z(\Xi)\subset\{|\Im z|<\half\}$ forces $d_0(z_0)\ge y-\half>0$, and at $y=\half$ (the Li line)
it holds unconditionally by the classical zero-free region, with $d_0(z_0)\ge c/\log(|t_0|+3)$.  Hence the admissible
scope of $z_0$ in Corollary~\ref{cor:arp-terminal-defect} is exactly
\[
   z_0=t_0-iy,\qquad y\ge\half,\qquad t_0\in\R,
\]
with no further restriction: the corollary holds at every gauge $z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y\ge\half$,
$t_0\in\R$, including $t_0=0$.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[ARP-P--terminal-defect equivalence]\label{cor:arp-terminal-defect}
Fix $z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y\ge\half$ (Remark~\ref{rmk:defect-scope}).  For the fixed scalar channel and the
reference-whitened defect of Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed} at this $z_0$,
\[
   \textnormal{ARP-P}
   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   \delta_N(z_0)\ge0\quad\text{for every }N.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed}, $\delta_N(z_0)\ge0$ for every $N$ is equivalent to
$J_N(z_0)\succeq0$ for every $N$, since the reference Gram $G_N(z_0)$ is positive definite at every
$y\ge\half$; by Remark~\ref{rmk:defect-scope} the distance hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets} holds at this $z_0$ (case (a) if $y>\half$, case (b) if $y=\half$), so
$J_N(z_0)\succeq0\ \forall N$ is equivalent to RH; and by
Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}, RH is equivalent to ARP-P.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[prime shift-operator representation]\label{thm:prime-shift}
On $L^2(0,\infty)$ let
\[
   (S_\lambda f)(\tau)=f(\tau-\lambda)\mathbf 1_{\tau\ge\lambda}
\]
and define the von Mangoldt shift form
\[
   \mathcal P_\Lambda
   :=
   \sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\bigl(S_{\log n}+S_{\log n}^*\bigr)
\]
on finite spans of exponentials $e^{-iz\tau}$ with $\Im z\le-(\half+\epsilon_0)$.  If
\[
   F(\tau)=\sum_j\bar c_j e^{-iz_j\tau},
\]
then the scalar prime part of the Pick form satisfies
\[
   c^*\mathsf P_{\mathrm{prime}}c
   =
   -\,\langle \mathcal P_\Lambda F,F\rangle_{L^2(0,\infty)}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On $\Re s>1$,
\[
   -\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(s)=\sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)n^{-s},
   \qquad
   n^{-s}=n^{-1/2}e^{-i(\log n)z}.
\]
For a single frequency $\lambda>0$, using
\[
   \frac1{z-\bar w}=i\int_0^\infty e^{-i(z-\bar w)\tau}\,d\tau
   \qquad(\Im(z-\bar w)<0),
\]
one obtains
\[
   \frac{i\bigl(e^{-i\lambda z}+e^{i\lambda\bar w}\bigr)}{z-\bar w}
   =
   -\int_0^\infty
      \left[
        e^{-iz(\tau+\lambda)}e^{i\bar w\tau}
        +
        e^{-iz\tau}e^{i\bar w(\tau+\lambda)}
      \right]d\tau .
\]
After summing against $\bar c_ic_j$, the right side is
\[
   -2\Re\int_0^\infty F(\tau+\lambda)\overline{F(\tau)}\,d\tau
   =
   -\langle(S_\lambda+S_\lambda^*)F,F\rangle .
\]
The sum over $n$ is absolutely convergent on the indicated spans because
$|F(\tau)|\le Ce^{-(\half+\epsilon_0)\tau}$ and hence
\[
   |\langle(S_{\log n}+S_{\log n}^*)F,F\rangle|
   \le C'n^{-\half-\epsilon_0},
\]
so it is dominated by $\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1-\epsilon_0}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[terminal spectral form]\label{thm:terminal-spectral}
Fix an interior-band accumulating scalar sequence and let $\mathcal E$ be the finite linear span of
the corresponding exponentials $e^{-iz_j\tau}$.  The terminal scalar input is equivalent to
\[
   \langle\mathcal P_\Lambda F,F\rangle\le \mathcal A(F,F)
   \qquad(F\in\mathcal E),
\]
where $\mathcal A$ is the exact archimedean/pole Pick form.  In a large-height fixed band,
\[
   \mathcal A(F,F)=\log\frac{|t_0|}{2\pi}\|F\|_{L^2(0,\infty)}^2+\mathcal R(F,F),
\]
with $\mathcal R$ the explicitly bounded Stirling remainder from Lemma~\ref{lem:arch-cluster}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift}, the prime contribution to the scalar Pick form is
$-\langle\mathcal P_\Lambda F,F\rangle$.  The remaining archimedean and pole terms define
$\mathcal A(F,F)$.  Therefore positivity of every finite scalar Pick prefix is exactly
\[
   \mathcal A(F,F)-\langle\mathcal P_\Lambda F,F\rangle\ge0
\]
on the finite exponential span.  The equivalence with RH is Theorem~\ref{thm:minimal-input}.  The
large-height expansion of $\mathcal A$ is the same Stirling/Szeg\H{o} expansion used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:arch-cluster}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Laguerre matrix elements of the terminal operator]\label{prop:laguerre-entries}
At $z_0=t_0-iy$, the leading Szeg\H{o} part of the archimedean jet Gram is diagonalized by the
orthonormal Laguerre basis
\[
   L_k^{(y,t_0)}(\tau)=e^{-it_0\tau}\sqrt{2y}\,\ell_k(2y\tau)e^{-y\tau},
\]
where $\ell_k$ are the standard orthonormal Laguerre polynomials on $[0,\infty)$ with weight
$e^{-u}du$.  In this basis, the whitened prime operator has entries
\[
   (T_N)_{jk}
   =
   +\frac1{\log(|t_0|/2\pi)}
   \sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
   \left[
      n^{-it_0}m_{jk}^{(y)}(\log n)
      +n^{it_0}\overline{m_{kj}^{(y)}(\log n)}
   \right]
   +O\!\left(\frac{E_N(y)}{\log |t_0|}\right),
\]
where
\[
   m_{jk}^{(y)}(\lambda)
   =
   \int_0^\infty
      \sqrt{2y}\,\ell_j(2y(u+\lambda))e^{-y(u+\lambda)}
      \sqrt{2y}\,\ell_k(2yu)e^{-yu}\,du .
\]
Each $m_{jk}^{(y)}(\lambda)$ is a polynomial in $\lambda$ times $e^{-y\lambda}$ and is computable in
closed form.  The error $E_N(y)$ is effective and comes only from the archimedean Stirling remainder.
Here $J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}$ is the jet matrix of $K_{\mathrm{prime}}$ as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal}, so that $J_N=J_N^\infty+J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}$ and, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift}, $c^*J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}c=-\langle\mathcal P_\Lambda F_c,F_c\rangle$;
hence $T_N=-(J_N^\infty)^{-1/2}J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}(J_N^\infty)^{-1/2}$ is the whitening of
$+\mathcal P_\Lambda$, with the convention $(T_N)_{jk}=\langle TL_j,L_k\rangle$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet} identifies the leading archimedean jet Gram with the Gram of
$\tau^ke^{-iz_0\tau}/k!$ in $L^2(0,\infty)$.  Orthonormalizing the polynomial factor against the
weight $e^{-2y\tau}d\tau$ gives the displayed Laguerre functions.  The shift representation
Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift} gives the matrix element of $S_{\log n}+S_{\log n}^*$ as the two lag
integrals above, with the phases $n^{\mp it_0}$ supplied by the carrier $e^{-it_0\tau}$.  The
Stirling remainder is holomorphic on a fixed polydisk in the interior band, so Cauchy's estimates make
the corresponding finite jet correction effective.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[effective $\log\log$ contractivity range]\label{cor:loglog-effective}
Fix $y\ge\half+\epsilon_0$.  There are an effective $\beta=\log(64y^2/\epsilon_0^2)$, an effective
constant $c_0$ and, for each $N$, an effective $T_2(N)$ such that, for $|t_0|\ge T_2(N)$,
\[
   \lambda_{\max}(T_N(t_0-iy))
   \le
   \frac{c_0N^2e^{\beta N}}{\log(|t_0|/2\pi)}
\]
for all $N\le N_*(t_0)$, the range where the archimedean jet matrix is positive
(Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair}).  In particular $\lambda_{\max}(T_N)\le1$ whenever $|t_0|\ge T_2(N)$,
$N\le N_*(t_0)$ and
\[
   N\le
   \frac{\log\log(|t_0|/2\pi)-\log(c_0N^2)}{\beta}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal},
\[
   \lambda_{\max}(T_N)\le\frac{\|J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}\|}{\lambda_{\min}(J_N^\infty)}.
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-window} bounds the prime jet entries by an exponential
$C(\epsilon_0)(2/\epsilon_0)^{2N}$, hence the matrix norm by $N^2$ times the same exponential.
Lemma~\ref{lem:arch-jet}, with the effective floor from Lemma~\ref{lem:pascal-effective}, gives
\[
   \lambda_{\min}(J_N^\infty)
   \ge
   4^{-N}(2y)^{-(2N-1)}\log(|t_0|/2\pi)-C_N'/|t_0|,
\]
with $C_N'$ effective by Cauchy estimates on the fixed polydisk.  Choosing $T_2(N)$ so that
$C_N'/|t_0|\le\frac12 4^{-N}(2y)^{-(2N-1)}\log(|t_0|/2\pi)$ for $|t_0|\ge T_2(N)$ absorbs the
remainder; since $C_N'$ depends on $N$, so does $T_2(N)$.  The quotient of the two exponentials is
$(2/\epsilon_0)^{2N}4^N(2y)^{2N}=(64y^2/\epsilon_0^2)^N$ up to a factor $O(y)$, which gives the stated
bound with $\beta=\log(64y^2/\epsilon_0^2)$.  The final range follows from
$c_0N^2e^{\beta N}\le\log(|t_0|/2\pi)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[archimedean terminal defect observable]\label{def:terminal-defect}
For a fixed one-point terminal gauge $z_0=t_0-iy$ and a scalar jet section, define
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0):=1-\lambda_{\max}(T_N(z_0)).
\]
This $J_N^\infty$-whitened defect is well defined only for $N\le N_*(t_0)$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair}).  Its sign is then the sign of $J_N$, but it is not an all-$N$ terminal
input at fixed $t_0$.  The reference-whitened observable $\delta_N^{\rm ref}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed} is defined for every $N$ and is the global form equivalent
to RH.  The two observables have the same sign wherever both are defined; their numerical values need
not agree.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[signed archimedean decomposition]\label{lem:signed-arch}
Let $h_A$ denote the archimedean part of
\[
   h(z)=-\frac{\Xi'(z)}{\Xi(z)}=h_A(z)+h_P(z),
\]
and set
\[
   w_A(u):=-\frac1\pi\Im h_A(u)
   =
   \frac1{2\pi}\log\frac{|u|}{2\pi}+O((1+u^2)^{-1})
   \qquad (u\in\R,\ |u|\to\infty).
\]
For $v_j(u)=(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$, let $G_N$ be the Lebesgue Gram matrix of
$v_0,\ldots,v_{N-1}$.  The archimedean jet matrix admits
\[
   A_N=\int_\R vv^*\,w_A(u)\,du+H_N,
\]
and, for each fixed $N$, the remainder satisfies
\[
   \|G_N^{-1/2}H_NG_N^{-1/2}\|
   \le C_N(y)\frac{\log |t_0|}{|t_0|}
\]
in a fixed interior band, with $C_N(y)$ depending on $N$.  No bound uniform in $N$ is claimed: $H_N$ contains the
contribution of the pole pair $\pm i/2$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair}, and $C_N(y)$ is not controlled
as $N\to\infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The cancellation of the elementary real parts on the critical line gives the displayed $w_A$ after
Stirling.  Subtracting the Cauchy transform of $w_A$ leaves a function with zero boundary imaginary
part on $\R$; by the Schwarz reflection principle and Morera's theorem, this continuation step (given
here as a sketch) extends the function analytically to a disk of radius comparable to
$|t_0|$ around the working point, with derivative $O(\log |t_0|/|t_0|)$.  Cauchy's estimates applied
to the two-variable divided difference kernel give entries bounded by
$M(t_0/4)^{-(j+k)}$, and comparison with the exact Szeg\H{o}/Lebesgue Gram gives the displayed
estimate for fixed $N$; the constant grows with $N$ because $G_N$ has condition number growing
exponentially in $N$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:pascal-effective}).  The rational terms of $h_A$ (the pole pair
$\pm i/2$) have zero boundary density and lie entirely in $H_N$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair} shows
only that $A_N$ fails to be positive semidefinite for some $N$; the growth in $N$ of the normalized
pole-pair contribution is not estimated here.

\emph{Herglotz normalization of the weight.}  Write the archimedean part as
$h_A(z)=i\bigl[-(\tfrac1s+\tfrac1{s-1})+\tfrac12\log\pi-\tfrac12\psi(s/2)\bigr]$, $s=\half+iz$, with
$\psi=\Gamma'/\Gamma$.  The digamma function is Nevanlinna: $\psi(s)=-\gamma+\sum_{n\ge0}
(\tfrac1{n+1}-\tfrac1{n+s})$ has $\Im\psi(s)>0$ for $\Im s>0$.  In $w_A(u)=-\tfrac1\pi\Im h_A(u)$ the
term $-\tfrac12\psi(s/2)$ contributes $\tfrac1{2\pi}\Re\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{iu}{2})=\tfrac1{2\pi}
\log\tfrac{|u|}{2}+O(u^{-2})$ (Stirling), the constant $\tfrac12\log\pi$ contributes the shift
$-\tfrac1{2\pi}\log\pi$, and the two rational terms $-(\tfrac1s+\tfrac1{s-1})$ are anti-Nevanlinna and
contribute only $O(u^{-2})$; together these give the asymptotic boundary density
$\tfrac1{2\pi}\log\tfrac{|u|}{2\pi}$.  Hence
\[
   w_A(u)=\frac1{2\pi}\log\frac{|u|}{2\pi}+O(u^{-2}).
\]
The weight $w_A$ is positive for $|u|\ge2\pi+1$ but not everywhere: numerically
$w_A(0)\approx-0.855$ and $w_A(2\pi)\approx-1.7\cdot10^{-4}$.  For the Hardy-space function
$f(u)=(u+i)^{-2}$ one finds numerically $\int_\R|f|^2w_A\,du\approx-0.875<0$, so the form
$f\mapsto\int|f|^2w_A$ is not positive on $H^2(\C^+)$; we expect, but do not prove, that
$\int vv^*w_A$ is not a positive form for large $N$.  What is proved is that $A_N$ itself fails to be
positive for some $N$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair}).  The asymptotic agrees to $10^{-7}$ at $u=300$:
$w_A(300)=0.6152779$, $\tfrac1{2\pi}\log\frac{300}{2\pi}=0.6152780$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[heuristic sampling model for the archimedean defect]\label{thm:defect-sampling}
Assume RH, fix $z_0=t_0-iy$ in an interior band, and restrict to $N\le N_*(t_0)$.  Put
\[
   L=\log\frac{|t_0|}{2\pi},
   \qquad
   \mathcal R_N(z_0)=
   \left\{
      R(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bar c_j(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}:\ c\in\C^N
   \right\}.
\]
Under RH the scalar zero-side jet form is
\[
   c^*J_Nc=\sum_\rho m_\rho |R_c(z_\rho)|^2,
   \qquad
   R_c(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bar c_j(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)},
\]
with real $z_\rho$, and the leading archimedean part of $A_N$ is $L$ times the Szeg\H{o} jet Gram,
which is the Lebesgue Gram of the same rational functions:
\[
   \frac1{2\pi}\int_\R
   \frac{du}{(u-z_0)^{j+1}(u-\bar z_0)^{k+1}}
   =
   i(-1)^k\binom{j+k}{j}(2iy)^{-(j+k+1)}
\]
(the real-line form of Lemma~\ref{lem:szego-jet}).  Define the constant-weight sampling quotient
\[
   \mathsf d_N(z_0)
   :=
   \min_{0\ne R\in\mathcal R_N(z_0)}
   \frac{\sum_\rho m_\rho |R(z_\rho)|^2}
        {\dfrac{L}{2\pi}\displaystyle\int_\R |R(u)|^2\,du}.
\]
For fixed $N\le N_*(t_0)$ and $|t_0|\to\infty$, Lemma~\ref{lem:arch-jet} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal} (which identifies $\delta_N^{\rm arch}$ with the minimum of
$c^*J_Nc/c^*A_Nc$) give
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0)=\mathsf d_N(z_0)\left(1+O_N(L^{-1})\right).
\]
Uniformity in $N$ is not proved: the remainder bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:signed-arch} depends on $N$,
and the weight $w_A$ is not positive on all of $\R$.  This display is a leading model formula, not an
identity for the reference-whitened defect.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[expected regimes of the sampling model]\label{thm:defect-sampling-v2}
In the setting of Remark~\ref{thm:defect-sampling}, the comparison
$\delta_N^{\rm arch}\approx\mathsf d_N$ is expected, but not proved, to hold with small relative error
in the two regimes
\[
   N\ge\tfrac32L
   \qquad\text{and}\qquad
   N\le(1-\varepsilon)\frac{L}{\log(16y^2)},
\]
with relative errors of the expected orders $O(N/|t_0|)+O(e^{-N})$ and $O((16y^2)^N/|t_0|)$
respectively.  The window between them, and any analogue for $\delta_N^{\rm ref}$, are open.  An
intermediate quotient with denominator $\int|R|^2w_A$ is not a well-defined minimum for large $N$,
since $\int|R|^2w_A$ can be nonpositive.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[finite-range interpolation bound]\label{thm:saturation}
Assume RH, fix $z_0=t_0-iy\in U_-$, and let $N\le N_*(t_0)$ so that
$A_N=J_N^\infty\succ0$.  If
$R_c(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bar c_j(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$ is chosen to vanish at the $N-1$ zero ordinates
nearest $t_0$, then
\[
   0\le\delta_N^{\rm arch}=1-\lambda_{\max}(T_N)
   \le
   \frac{\sum_{\rho\ {\rm far}}m_\rho |R_c(z_\rho)|^2}
        {c^*A_Nc}
   ,
\]
where the right side is the zero-tail energy after interpolation of the nearest atoms.  This proposition
makes no $N\to\infty$ assertion at fixed $t_0$: the archimedean whitening ceases to be defined after
$N_*(t_0)$.  Saturation of the global reference-whitened defect would require a separate all-$N$ tail
estimate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under RH, the scalar Pick jet matrix is the Gram matrix of the functions
$(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$ against the positive zero measure
\[
   d\Sigma_\Xi(u)=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,\delta_{z_\rho}(u),
\]
while
$J_N=A_N+J_N^{\mathrm{prime}}$.  For any nonzero $c$,
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}=1-\lambda_{\max}(T_N)
   \le
   \frac{c^*J_Nc}{c^*A_Nc}
   =
   \frac{\sum_\rho m_\rho |R_c(z_\rho)|^2}{c^*A_Nc}.
\]
Choose $c$ so that the numerator vanishes at the $N-1$ zero ordinates closest to $t_0$; this is
ordinary rational interpolation in the basis $(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$.  Only the far zero tail remains,
which gives the displayed inequality.  Positivity under RH gives
$\lambda_{\max}(T_N)\le1$.  No limiting conclusion follows without a uniform estimate of the displayed
far-tail quotient in a coordinate defined for all $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the no-fixed-margin claim is not proved]\label{cor:no-margin}
The proposed statement
\[
   \inf_{N\ge1}\delta_N^{\rm ref}=0
\]
under RH is not proved by Proposition~\ref{thm:saturation}.  The proposition supplies only a finite-range
archimedean interpolation bound.  An all-$N$ no-margin theorem must be formulated and proved in the
reference whitening.  Numerical experiments, not reproduced here, suggest it; it is not used as an
input.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[conditional Blaschke detection rate]\label{prop:blaschke-rate}
Suppose $\Xi$ has a non-real pair $\{z_w,\bar z_w\}$ with $\Im z_w<0$.  For the terminal base point
$z_0=t_0-iy$, set the exterior Blaschke factor
\[
   b(\zeta)=\frac{\zeta-\bar z_0}{\zeta-z_0}.
\]
Consider a sequence of gauges and orders $(t_N,N)$ for which $N\le N_*(t_N)$, so that $T_N(t_N-iy)$
is defined.  If the target pair remains the unique dominant off-line pair in Blaschke distance, the
normalization factors are subexponential, and the cost of avoiding the nearby real zeros is
subexponential, then the expected detection law is
\[
   \lim_{N\to\infty}\lambda_{\max}(T_N(t_N-iy))^{1/N}=|b_{t_N}(z_w)|,
\]
whenever the right side has a limit.  For several dominant pairs it is replaced by the maximal
Blaschke modulus, subject to a noncancellation hypothesis.  No fixed-gauge $N\to\infty$ assertion is
made, because $T_N$ ceases to be defined beyond $N_*(t_0)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Heuristic justification]
The pair contribution
\[
   g_{\rm pair}(z)=\frac1{z_w-z}+\frac1{\bar z_w-z}
\]
has real boundary values on $\R$, and its Pick kernel decomposes into the rank-two hyperbolic block
\[
   e_{z_w}\otimes\overline{e_{\bar z_w}}
   +
   e_{\bar z_w}\otimes\overline{e_{z_w}},
\]
so the form contains $2\Re[X\overline Y]$ with $X$ and $Y$ the evaluations of the rational test at
$z_w$ and $\bar z_w$.  Evaluation at $\bar z_w\in\C^+$ is bounded in the model space, while evaluation
at the exterior point $z_w\in\C^-$ has reproducing-kernel norm growing like
\[
   \frac{|b(z_w)|^{2N}-1}{4\pi|\Im z_w|}.
\]
This predicts the lower exponential rate, but a proof also needs uniform control of the positive tail,
the whitening normalization, and every additional pair.  Those estimates are not supplied here.  In
numerical experiments on Davenport--Heilbronn data, not reproduced here, the observed growth rate
appeared to agree with $|b(z_w)|$ to about four digits; this is consistent with the model, not a proof
of the uniform hypotheses.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[conditional finite detection scale]\label{cor:blaschke-detection}
An off-line zero at Blaschke radius $|b(z_w)|=e^\beta>1$ is detected in terminal sections on the scale
$N\asymp \beta^{-1}\log C$, where $C$ is the ratio of the positive tail energy to the pair
normalization, only under the uniform hypotheses of
Conjecture~\ref{prop:blaschke-rate}.  Two distinct terminal base points would then give two Blaschke
level curves and hence, in principle, triangulate the off-line zero.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[terminal defect law]\label{conj:defect-law}
Fix $z_0=t_0-iy$ in an interior band and set
\[
   L=\log\frac{|t_0|}{2\pi}.
\]
For the scalar one-point terminal gauge,
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0)=1-\lambda_{\max}(T_N(z_0))
   =
   \left(\frac{\kappa_{\rm sin}(y)L}{N}\right)^{2N(1+o(1))}
\]
as $N/L\to\infty$ along sequences with $N\le N_*(t_0)$.
Numerical experiments on the symmetric sine model at $y=1$, not reproduced here, suggest
$\kappa_{\rm sin}(1)\approx0.686$.  Asymmetric offsets display discrete left/right assignment transitions and should not be extrapolated by
the same smooth tail fit.  The formula is meant only along sequences satisfying
$N\le N_*(t_0)$; it is an empirical conjecture, not an all-$N$ fixed-gauge law and not an input to the
proof path.  A corresponding law for $\delta_N^{\rm ref}$ remains open.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{definition}[lattice defect constant]\label{def:kappa-lattice}
Let
\[
   \Gamma_L=\left\{u_0+\frac{2\pi k}{L}:k\in\Z\right\}
\]
be the exact lattice of density $L/(2\pi)$.  Define the model constant
\[
   \kappa_{\mathrm{lat}}(y):=
   \lim_{N/L\to\infty}
   \frac NL
   \left[
      \min_{0\ne R\in\mathcal R_N(t_0-iy)}
      \frac{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_L}|R(\gamma)|^2}
           {\dfrac L{2\pi}\|R\|_{L^2(\R)}^2}
   \right]^{1/(2N)},
\]
when the limit exists.  This is a Christoffel-type extremal problem for a lattice sampled against
Lebesgue measure in a rational Hardy model space.  It is independent of RH and is the clean model
problem for the constant in Conjecture~\ref{conj:defect-law}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[sine-model form of the lattice constant]\label{prop:sine-model}
The lattice defect of Definition~\ref{def:kappa-lattice} is the terminal defect of the sine model.  Let
\[
   \Xi_{\rm lat}(z)=\sin\!\left(\frac{L(z-u_0)}2\right),
   \qquad
   M_{\rm lat}(z)=-\frac{\Xi_{\rm lat}'(z)}{\Xi_{\rm lat}(z)}
   =-\frac L2\cot\!\left(\frac{L(z-u_0)}2\right).
\]
For $\Im z<0$,
\[
   M_{\rm lat}(z)
   =
   -\frac{iL}{2}
   -iL\sum_{m\ge1}e^{-imL(z-u_0)}.
\]
The constant term produces the leading archimedean/Szeg\H{o} form $L\,S$, while the oscillatory part
is a one-frequency shift model with lags $mL$.  Thus $\kappa_{\rm lat}(y)$ is a constant of a classical
approximation problem for $\sin$, with no arithmetic input.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The zeros of $\Xi_{\rm lat}$ are precisely the lattice
$u_0+2\pi k/L$.  The logarithmic derivative gives the displayed cotangent expression.  Since
$\Im z<0$, the standard expansion
\[
   \cot w=i+2i\sum_{m\ge1}e^{-2imw}
   \qquad(\Im w<0)
\]
with $w=L(z-u_0)/2$ gives the series for $M_{\rm lat}$.  The constant $-iL/2$ has one-node Pick
quotient $L/(2y)$ at $z=-iy$, matching the leading $L$ times Szeg\H{o} kernel.  The remaining terms
are shifts at lags $mL$ with coefficients $-Le^{imLu_0}$ (compare $+\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}$ in
Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift}); the same whitening applies and computes the lattice defect.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[sine concentration constant]\label{def:kappa-sine}
Let $\delta_N^{\rm lat}(y,L,u_0)$ be the terminal defect of the sine model of
Proposition~\ref{prop:sine-model}.  When the limit exists, define
\[
   \kappa_{\rm sin}(y)
   :=
   \lim_{N/L\to\infty}
   \frac NL
   \left(\delta_N^{\rm lat}(y,L,u_0)\right)^{1/(2N)}.
\]
Numerical experiments, not reproduced here, suggest the factorial law and, by smooth extrapolation
at symmetric offsets, $\kappa_{\rm sin}(1)\approx0.686$.  Asymmetric offsets may show discrete jumps in finite $N$ caused by left/right assignment changes of the
extremal zeros; these are recorded as finite-size transitions rather than a change in the limiting
constant.
Analytically, this is a constrained-equilibrium/prolate concentration problem: minimize
lattice-sampled energy against continuous $L^2$ energy over the rational model space
$\mathcal R_N\subset H^2(\C^+)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{problem}[constrained equilibrium form of $\kappa_{\rm sin}$]\label{prob:kappa-final}
Determine the constant $\kappa_{\rm sin}(y)$ by the following scaled constrained-equilibrium problem.
Among measures $\sigma\ge0$ with
\[
   \sigma(\R)=1,\qquad \sigma\le\tfrac12\,{\rm Leb},
\]
and obstacle condition
\[
   U^\sigma(t)\le\log|t|\qquad (t\in\R\setminus S(\sigma)),
\]
maximize the center field $U^\sigma(0)$.  If
\[
   G^*:=\sup_\sigma U^\sigma(0),
\]
the predicted relation is
\[
   \kappa_{\rm sin}(y)=\frac y\pi e^{-G^*}.
\]
The uniform interpolant gives $G=-1$; under the predicted relation this corresponds to the upper scale
$\kappa_{\rm sin}(y)\le ey/\pi$ (consistent with the heuristic upper bound of
Conjecture~\ref{thm:two-sided}).  Numerical extrapolation at $y=1$, not reproduced here, suggests
$G^*\approx-0.768$, consistent with $\kappa_{\rm sin}(1)\approx\pi^{-1}e^{0.768}\approx0.686$.  The expected extremal has a saturated core and free boundary/contact regions,
as in Dragnev--Saff constrained equilibrium for discrete orthogonal polynomial asymptotics.
\end{problem}

\begin{proposition}[quantitative saturation upper-bound reduction]\label{thm:sat-quant}
Assume RH and $N\le N_*(t_0)$.  Let $R_N=R_c$ be the rational interpolant in
Proposition~\ref{thm:saturation}, vanishing at the
$N-1$ zero ordinates nearest $t_0$.  If the far-zero product estimate
\[
   \sum_{\rho\ {\rm far}}m_\rho |R_N(z_\rho)|^2
   \le
   c^*A_Nc\left(\frac{C(y)L}{N}\right)^{2N}
\]
holds with $L=\log(|t_0|/2\pi)$, then
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0)\le\left(\frac{C(y)L}{N}\right)^{2N}.
\]
The hypothesis is the analytic form of the quantitative saturation calculation: it asks
for the Riemann--von Mangoldt zero density, viewed through the rational interpolator and the
Pascal--Laguerre floor, to supply a factorial tail bound.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is Proposition~\ref{thm:saturation} with the displayed product estimate substituted into the
zero-tail quotient.  All normalization loss is carried by the denominator $c^*A_Nc$, whose effective
lower control is Lemma~\ref{lem:pascal-effective}.  Thus the problem of proving the factorial upper
bound is reduced to the stated far-zero product estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[two-sided factorial saturation bounds]\label{thm:two-sided}
Assume RH.  For $L\le N\le\min(|t_0|^{1/4},N_*(t_0))$ we expect
\[
   \left(\frac{c_1(y)L}{N}\right)^{2N}
   \le
   \delta_N^{\rm arch}(z_0)
   \le
   \left(
      \frac{eyL}{\pi N}
      \left(1+O\!\left(\frac1{\log\log |t_0|}\right)\right)
   \right)^{2N},
\]
for some $c_1(y)>0$, whose effective value is not determined here.  Neither bound is proved; the
argument below is heuristic.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Heuristic justification]
For the upper bound, choose the rational interpolant vanishing at the $N-1$ zero ordinates nearest
$t_0$.  On the scale $W=\pi N/L$, the logarithmic potential of the uniform interpolation pattern has
far-field maximum at infinity, giving the factor $(eyL/\pi N)^{2N}$; the Riemann--von Mangoldt
fluctuation in the window contributes the stated $O(1/\log\log |t_0|)$ under RH.  For the lower bound,
among the $4N$ nearest zeros, a Tur\'an--Remez type selection is expected to give at least $N$ points whose product distance
against any degree-$N$ numerator is bounded below by $(c\,{\rm gap})^N$.  Combining this selection with
the pole field at depth $y$ would yield the lower factorial scale.  Neither the selection estimate
nor the passage to an explicit $c_1(y)$ is carried out here.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[why the factorial scale matters]\label{rmk:factorial-wall}
If Conjecture~\ref{conj:defect-law} holds, it would sharpen the expectation of
Remark~\ref{cor:no-margin}: the terminal positivity is close to the boundary at a factorial scale.  In
that case any proof of $J_N\succeq0$ by perturbation of a positive main term must control the error to
relative accuracy $\exp(-2N\log(N/L)(1+o(1)))$; errors of size $\exp(-o(N\log(N/L)))$ relative to the main
term cannot certify the sign.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[heuristic root recovery from the extremal vector]\label{prop:root-recovery}
Assume RH, let $N\le N_*(t_0)$ and let $c_N$ be a near-extremal vector for $\delta_N^{\rm arch}$.  In
the main interpolation window, one expects that
the numerator of
\[
   R_{c_N}(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\overline{(c_N)_j}(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}
\]
has zeros very close to the zero ordinates that carry the dominant sampling constraints, the
closeness being governed by the local logarithmic potential relative to the center field.  The
heuristic reason is that the sampling quotient of Remark~\ref{thm:defect-sampling} forces
$|R_{c_N}(\gamma)|^2$ to be small at every sampled zero ordinate in the active window, while the
potential profile suggests a lower bound on $R_{c_N}$ inside each gap, so that a Rouch\'e comparison
with the local linear factor would locate one zero of the numerator per gap.  This is not proved here;
in particular no quantitative form of the closeness is established.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[asymmetric lattice offsets]\label{rmk:asymmetric-offsets}
The lattice offsets $0$ and half-gap are symmetric relative to the projection of $z_0$ and give smooth
tail extrapolations.  Asymmetric offsets can undergo discrete left/right assignment transitions in the
extremal zero pattern.  Such transitions alter the finite-$N$ prefactor by
\[
   \Delta\kappa_N\sim \frac{\Delta\log A}{2N},
\]
where $A$ is the finite-$N$ prefactor in $\delta_N=A\,(\kappa_NL/N)^{2N}$.  This would account for
finite jumps in numerical experiments (not reproduced here), such as at an offset of a quarter of the lattice gap, without changing
the expected limiting sine constant.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[terminal wall as Weil positivity]\label{thm:wall-weil}
For the Laguerre unilateral test class
\[
   \widehat g(\tau)\sim e^{it_0\tau}q(\tau)e^{-y\tau}\mathbf 1_{\tau>0}
\]
convolved with its reflected partner, the terminal quadratic form satisfies
\[
   c^*J_Nc=\mathcal W_\Xi(g_c),\qquad g_c(u):=R_c(u)\overline{R_c(\bar u)}
\]
(so $g_c=|R_c|^2$ on $\R$), where $\mathcal W_\Xi$ is the functional of
Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}.  Consequently
\[
   J_N(z_0)\succeq0\ \text{for every }N
   \ \Longleftrightarrow\
   \mathcal W_\Xi(R(u)\overline{R(\bar u)})\ge0\ \text{for all }R\in\textstyle\bigcup_N\mathcal R_N(z_0)
   \ \Longleftrightarrow\
   \mathrm{RH},
\]
the first condition being equivalent to $\delta_N^{\rm ref}(z_0)\ge0$ for every $N$, and the last
equivalence being Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The equality of forms is the explicit formula applied to the test $g_c$ in the unilateral
Laguerre representation: the zero side is $c^*J_Nc$, while the archimedean and prime sides are exactly
the terminal decomposition used above; Lemma~\ref{lem:weil-continuity} below gives the continuity of
$\mathcal W_\Xi$ needed to apply the explicit formula to these tests.  The first equivalence is then the identity
$c^*J_Nc=\mathcal W_\Xi(g_c)$, and the second is Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}.  This is a one-point class
$\{|R|^2\}$, not the full class $g*\tilde g$ of Weil's criterion; the equivalence with RH for this
class comes from Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}, not from Weil's criterion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[continuity of the Weil functional on the completed Laguerre class]\label{lem:weil-continuity}
Equip the test class with the norm
\[
   \|g\|:=\sup_{|\eta|\le\half}\sup_{x\in\R}(1+x^2)|g(x+i\eta)|
   +\sup_\tau e^{(\half+\epsilon_0/2)|\tau|}|\widehat g(\tau)|,
\]
for $g$ holomorphic on a neighbourhood of the closed strip $|\Im z|\le\half$.  Then the three sides of
$\mathcal W_\Xi$ are $\|\cdot\|$-continuous, and $\mathcal W_\Xi$ extends continuously to the completion of the Laguerre class.
This gives the continuity used in Theorem~\ref{thm:wall-weil}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since every zero satisfies $|\Im z_\rho|<\half$, the zero side is bounded by
$\sum_\rho m_\rho|g(z_\rho)|\le\|g\|\sum_\rho m_\rho(1+(\Re z_\rho)^2)^{-1}$, and the last sum
converges by the Riemann--von Mangoldt formula.  The prime side is bounded by
$\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}|\widehat g(\pm\log n)|\le\|g\|\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1-\epsilon_0/2}<\infty$.
The archimedean side is the integral of $g$ against a weight $O(\log(2+|u|))$ plus the values of $g$
at the pole pair $\pm i/2$; it is bounded by
$\|g\|\bigl(\int_\R(1+u^2)^{-1}O(\log(2+|u|))\,du+2\bigr)$.  A Laguerre test at depth
$y\ge\half+\epsilon_0$ is a product of rational functions with poles at $z_0,\bar z_0$, $|\Im z_0|=y$,
so it is holomorphic on $|\Im z|\le\half$ with $(1+x^2)$-decay there, and
$|\widehat g(\tau)|\lesssim(1+|\tau|)^{2N}e^{-y|\tau|}$; hence $\|g\|<\infty$.  Continuity on the
Laguerre class gives the continuous extension to its completion.
\end{proof}



\subsubsection{The interpolation certificate tower}

\begin{proposition}[Nevanlinna--Pick certificate tower]\label{prop:np-tower}
Assume ARP-P, fix a finite channel $F$, and fix the node sequence used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh}.  For every $N$ there exists a matrix Pick-class interpolant $F_N$ satisfying
$F_N(z_j)=g^F(z_j)$ for $j\le N$.  Any admissible choice of such interpolants has the following
properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item $\{F_N\}$ has pinned-normal subsequential limits on $\Omega_-$ in the sense of
Lemma~\ref{lem:pinned-normality};
\item $F_N\to g^F$ locally uniformly on $U_-$;
\item the analytic bridge applies to the limiting target exactly as in Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh}.
\end{enumerate}
Conversely, the existence of any tower of Cauchy transforms of positive measures converging to $g^F$ on
$U_-$ implies ARP-P.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Existence for each $N$ is the matrix Nevanlinna--Pick theorem applied to Definition~\ref{def:ARP-P}.
Pinned normality and the identification of every subsequential limit with $g^F$ on $U_-$ are proved by
Lemma~\ref{lem:pinned-normality} as used in Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh}; since all subsequential limits
agree, the full sequence converges locally uniformly on $U_-$.  The bridge conclusion is the last
paragraph of that theorem.  The converse is Lemma~\ref{lem:cauchy-pick} plus closure of the positive
semidefinite cone under pointwise limits at finite node sets.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[role of the von Mangoldt tower]\label{rmk:vm-tower-role}
The von Mangoldt tower serves as a candidate certificate for ARP-P.  It is valuable because its
finite spectral measures are positive, ultimately reflecting $\Lambda\ge0$.  It is not the minimal
essential object: any proof of tower-ARP proves ARP-P by Theorem~\ref{thm:tower-implies-pick}, while
any proof of ARP-P supplies Pick-class interpolation certificates by Proposition~\ref{prop:np-tower}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[direct-sum tower exposes the placement problem]\label{prop:placement-problem}
In a fully decoupled direct-sum realization with one-dimensional free cells,
\[
   M_P^F(z)_{\alpha\beta}
   =
   \sum_{v\in\mathcal V_P}
   \frac{c_v\,\phi_\beta(\tau_v)\overline{\phi_\alpha(\tau_v)}}{\lambda_v-z}
   +M_{\infty,P}^F(z)_{\alpha\beta},
   \qquad
   \tau_v=k\log p,\quad c_v=(\log p)p^{-k/2}.
\]
Therefore tower-ARP for such a decoupled model is a spectral placement problem for the real numbers
$\lambda_v$ and the archimedean component: by Definition~\ref{def:ARP}, such a model is a positive
tower exactly when the matrix measures $\Sigma_P^F=\sum_{v\in\mathcal V_P}c_v[\phi_\beta(\tau_v)
\overline{\phi_\alpha(\tau_v)}]_{\alpha\beta}\,\delta_{\lambda_v}+\Sigma_{\infty,P}^F$, where $\Sigma_{\infty,P}^F$ is a matrix measure with
Cauchy transform $M_{\infty,P}^F$ (when one exists), are positive with uniformly bounded mass and their Cauchy transforms converge pointwise on $U_-$ to $g^F$.
The construction gives no rule for choosing the $\lambda_v$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a one-dimensional self-adjoint free cell, $A_v=\lambda_v I$ with $\lambda_v\in\R$, and the cell
resolvent is $(\lambda_v-z)^{-1}$.  Compressing against the source vector
$\sqrt{c_v}\phi_\alpha(\tau_v)e_v$ gives the displayed contribution.  Summing over the finite
cutoff and adding the archimedean compression gives the formula.  The limit is therefore controlled by
the weak convergence of the corresponding positive spectral measures.  Choosing the $\lambda_v$ to make
that limit equal to $g^F$ is precisely the missing arithmetic placement mechanism.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Target inequality and correlation form}

\begin{proposition}[Weil splitting of ARP-P]\label{prop:target-ineq}
Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel} decomposes $g^F$ on $U_-$ as a Weil explicit-formula functional:
\[
   g^F=g_\infty^F-g_{\mathrm{prime}}^F,
\]
where $g_\infty^F$ denotes the archimedean and pole/degree contribution and
$g_{\mathrm{prime}}^F$ denotes the von-Mangoldt prime contribution, both interpreted in the same
regularized Weil sense as Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}.  Since the Pick matrix is linear in the
function, ARP-P is equivalent to the finite-node dominance
\[
   \mathsf P[g_\infty^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]
   \succeq
   \mathsf P[g_{\mathrm{prime}}^F;z_1,\ldots,z_N]
\]
for every finite source plane $F$ and every finite node set in $U_-$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply the linear map
\[
   h\longmapsto
   \left[
      \frac{h(z_i)-h(z_j)^*}{z_i-\bar z_j}
   \right]_{i,j=1}^{N}
\]
to the Weil splitting of Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}.  The inequality is exactly the assertion
that the Pick matrix of $g^F=g_\infty^F-g_{\mathrm{prime}}^F$ is positive semidefinite.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Bochner correlation viewpoint]\label{rmk:bochner}
For $z\in U_-$,
\[
   \frac1{z_\rho-z}=-i\int_0^\infty e^{-izt}e^{iz_\rho t}\,dt.
\]
Thus the zero-side target can be written formally as
\[
   g^F(z)=-i\int_0^\infty e^{-izt}\psi^F(t)\,dt,
   \qquad
   \psi^F(t)_{\alpha\beta}
   =
   \sum_\rho
   m_\rho\,\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)
   \overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}
   e^{iz_\rho t}.
\]
Through the explicit formula, $\psi^F$ is represented by arithmetic distributions.  ARP-P is the
finite-node Pick form of the same positivity problem; in Fourier language it asks for the corresponding
extended correlation distribution to be positive definite.  This is a global condition, not a
prime-cell-by-prime-cell assertion.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Davenport--Heilbronn falsifier]\label{rmk:dh-pick}
The analogue of ARP-P must fail for Davenport--Heilbronn data.  Indeed, the Davenport--Heilbronn function
has off-critical zeros; if its arithmetic channel data satisfied ARP-P, the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pick-rh} would force those zeros onto the critical line.  Therefore some finite Pick
matrix at nodes in the absolute-convergence region must have a negative eigenvalue.  This gives a
finite-dimensional numerical falsifier for the criterion.
\end{remark}




\subsection{Numerical tests of the terminal defect, and the reduction to Li's criterion}\label{sec:h8-harness}

This subsection records numerical evaluations of the terminal observable, intended as tests
against which candidate arguments for the open input Step~5 (H8) can be checked.  The numbers in
this subsection come from floating-point computations whose code is not included with the paper;
they are reported as unverified observations, and nothing in the paper depends on them.  The
governing statements remain Theorems~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal}, \ref{thm:prime-shift} and
Proposition~\ref{thm:saturation}.

\subsubsection*{Two independent coordinates}

The terminal defect can be computed in two ways that use disjoint inputs, so that agreement is a
genuine cross-check of the explicit formula rather than a tautology.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\alph*)}]
\item \emph{Sampling coordinate} (the sampling form of Remark~\ref{thm:defect-sampling}, which is
heuristic and is used here only as a computational recipe): $\widetilde\delta_N=\delta_N/L=\min_c\ c^*J_{\rm zero}c\,/\,(L\,c^*Gc)$
--- the rescaled variant of Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed}, of the same sign as $\delta_N$
at these gauges since $L>0$ --- where
$G$ is the exact Szeg\H{o}/Lebesgue reference Gram
$G_{jk}=i(-1)^k\binom{j+k}{j}(2iy)^{-(j+k+1)}$, and $J_{\rm zero}$ is assembled from rank-one zero
atoms $v(\gamma)v(\gamma)^*$, $v_j(u)=(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$, plus off-line pair blocks.  This uses only the
\emph{zero} configuration.
\item \emph{Arithmetic coordinate} (the object a factorization would have to factor): the Pick jets
$A_N$ (archimedean, closed form via $\psi^{(p)}$) and $\mathcal P_\Lambda$ (prime), assembled from the
exact log-derivative $-\zeta'/\zeta$ through the coefficient recursion
$\delta\,J_{jk}+J_{j-1,k}-J_{j,k-1}=h_j\mathbf 1_{k=0}-\overline{h_k}\mathbf 1_{j=0}$,
$\delta=z_0-\bar z_0$.  Crucially this uses \emph{no zeros}: the prime part is obtained from
$\zeta^{(k)}(s_0)$ by series division, avoiding the impossible $n^{-(1/2+y)}$ Dirichlet truncation.
The terminal input is $J_{\rm arith}:=A_N-\mathcal P_\Lambda\succeq0$.
\end{enumerate}

By the explicit formula $J_{\rm arith}=J_{\rm zero}$; in the computations reported below the two
agreed at jet level up to the finite-zero truncation error.

\subsubsection*{Three numerical comparisons}

Computed at $y=1$ on the gauge $z_0=t_0-i$ with $t_0$ centered on the $\zeta$-zero window
$\#100$--$175$ ($t_0\approx297.238$, $L\approx3.88$; the value $L\approx3.88$ is the one used in the computation, whereas $\log(t_0/2\pi)=3.857$ at $t_0=297.238$, and the discrepancy is unexplained):

\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.30\linewidth}p{0.62\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
Law & Computed behaviour (unverified floating-point computation; values as computed, normalisation $\delta_N$ or $\delta_N/L$ not recorded) \\
\midrule
\endhead
$\zeta$: arithmetic PSD & $\delta_N(\text{arith})>0$ with rapid decay
$1.6\!\cdot\!10^{-2},\,8.0\!\cdot\!10^{-5},\,7.7\!\cdot\!10^{-8},\,1.8\!\cdot\!10^{-11}$
($N=4,6,8,10$); $J_{\rm arith}$ matches the $76$-zero Gram to the truncation tail. \\
DH: negative defect detected & Sampling $\delta_N$ crosses negative for the known off-line zero pair of the Davenport--Heilbronn function (displacement $v=0.308517$ from the line); the negative branch ratios converge to the Blaschke rate
$2.673\to2.256\to\mathbf{2.2558}$, matching the prediction of
Conjecture~\ref{prop:blaschke-rate}
($|b(z_w)|=2.2554$). \\
Sine/lattice: trivial & $\delta_N>0$ with rapid decay and empty cascade, as in
Remark~\ref{rmk:cascade-validation}. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsubsection*{Roots of the minimizing rational function}

Let $c$ be the generalized minimizer of the arithmetic pencil $(J_{\rm arith},A_N)$ realizing
$\delta_N$, and let $R_c(u)=\sum_j\overline{c_j}(u-z_0)^{-(j+1)}$ be the associated rational function.
The following table lists, for the roots of the numerator of $R_c$ lying in the active window
$|u-t_0|\le W=\pi N/L$, the maximal distance off the real axis and the maximal distance to the actual
$\zeta$-zero set (unverified floating-point computation).

\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.06\linewidth}p{0.14\linewidth}p{0.18\linewidth}p{0.26\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
$N$ & $W=\pi N/L$ & $\max|\Im\,\text{root}|$ & $\max$ recovery error in $W$ \\
\midrule
\endhead
$6$ & $4.86$ & $8.3\cdot10^{-59}$ & $0.43$ \\
$8$ & $6.48$ & $3.4\cdot10^{-55}$ & $0.64$ \\
$10$ & $8.10$ & $5.1\cdot10^{-53}$ & $0.23$ \\
$12$ & $9.71$ & $4.4\cdot10^{-49}$ & $0.45$ \\
$14$ & $11.3$ & $1.1\cdot10^{-44}$ & $0.65$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

Two facts are visible.  First, the computed minimizer roots, obtained from the arithmetic side
alone, are real to working precision at these five values of $N$.  Second, the innermost computed
roots coincide with actual $\zeta$-zeros to working precision (for $N=12$ the four central roots reproduce
$294.96537,\,295.57325,\,297.97928,\,299.84033$ exactly to eight figures); the larger window-error entries
are the expected edge roots that interpolate between consecutive zeros.  This is a numerical illustration of
Remark~\ref{prop:root-recovery}.

\begin{remark}[scope of these computations]\label{rmk:harness-scope}
These computations are numerical observations at finitely many $N$ and one gauge; any proposed
factorization $A_N-\mathcal P_\Lambda=B^*B$ can be tested against them on $\zeta$ and on the
Davenport--Heilbronn function.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[scalar level-one positivity]\label{thm:level-one-scalar}
For the scalar coordinate
\[
   M_\Xi(z)=i\left(-\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)\right),\qquad s=\half+iz,
\]
the one-node Pick inequality on $U_-$ is equivalent to
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)\ge0,\qquad \Re s>1.
\]
This inequality is classical; see Lagarias~\cite{Lagarias1999}, where it is shown that
$\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)>0$ on $\Re s>\half$ is equivalent to RH.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $z=x-iy\in U_-$ with $y>\half$.  Then $\Re s=\half+y>1$ and $\Im z=-y=-(\Re s-\half)$.  The diagonal Pick quotient is
\[
   \frac{M_\Xi(z)-M_\Xi(z)^*}{z-\bar z}
   =
   \frac{\Im M_\Xi(z)}{\Im z}
   =
   \frac{-\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)}{-(\Re s-\half)}
   =
   \frac{\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)}{\Re s-\half}.
\]
Since $\Re s-\half>0$, positivity is equivalent to the displayed inequality.

By the Hadamard product for $\xi$, with the constant term fixed as in
Davenport~\cite[Sec.~12]{Davenport},
\[
   \frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)
   =
   \sum_\rho\left(\frac1{s-\rho}+\frac1\rho\right)+B,
\]
with the usual real normalization in which the real parts of the constant terms cancel after pairing
zeros.  Taking real parts gives
\[
   \Re\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)
   =
   \sum_\rho
   \frac{\sigma-\beta}{|s-\rho|^2},
   \qquad s=\sigma+it,
\]
in the standard symmetric limiting sense.  The classical zero-free theorem for $\zeta$ on
$\Re s=1$ and the functional equation give $0<\beta<1$ for nontrivial zeros.  Hence for $\sigma>1$ every
summand has nonnegative real part, and the inequality follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[scope of the level-one theorem]\label{rmk:level-one-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:level-one-scalar} closes the scalar one-node seed.  It is not the full matrix-channel
ARP-P(1), because the channel target contains source weights
$\ell_{z_\rho}(\phi_\beta)\overline{\ell_{\bar z_\rho}(\phi_\alpha)}$.  The full channel hierarchy is the
remaining Pick-positivity problem.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{H8: the reduced contractivity, a candidate route, and its gap}

We record a candidate argument for the terminal input $\delta_N\ge0$ and the point where it stalls.

\emph{The exact reduction.}  The all-$N$ statement is the finite-dimensional reference whitening of
Theorem~\ref{thm:reference-whitened-terminal}:
\[
   \boxed{\ \delta_N\ge0\ \forall N
   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   R_N^{\rm ref}\preceq I\ \forall N.\ }
\]
This is the governing terminal condition because $G_N\succ0$ for every $N$.

There is a second representation only in the positive range of the archimedean jet.  Pass to the
additive model $L^2(0,\infty)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift}.  A rational
$R\in\mathcal R_N$ corresponds to $F(\tau)=\sum_jc_j\tau^je^{-iz_0\tau}$, and, for
$N\le N_*(t_0)$,
\[
   c^*(A_N-\mathcal P_\Lambda)c
   =\langle(\mathcal W_A-\mathcal T_\Lambda)F,F\rangle,
   \qquad
   \mathcal T_\Lambda=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\,(S_{\log n}+S_{\log n}^*),
\]
with $A_N=J_N^\infty\succ0$; here $\mathcal W_A$ is the positive form on
$\operatorname{span}\{\tau^je^{-iz_0\tau}\}_{j<N}$ with $\langle\mathcal W_AF,F\rangle=c^*A_Nc$, and
$\mathcal T_\Lambda$ is the form $\mathcal P_\Lambda$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-shift}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:whitened-terminal} then identifies the sign condition
with one-sided domination by the identity; write
$\mathcal T:=\mathcal W_A^{-1/2}\mathcal T_\Lambda\mathcal W_A^{-1/2}$ in this range.  This
archimedean coordinate is not defined as a positive
whitening after $N_*(t_0)$ and cannot replace the reference-whitened all-$N$ box above.

\emph{Remark on two-sidedness.}  Even in its valid range the domination is one-sided: in
floating-point computations (unverified) the two-sided norm exceeds $1$ at interior gauges (at
$t_0=30$, $y=1$ the computed bottom eigenvalue of the whitened jet compression is
$-3.34,-7.62,-11.90$ at $N=4,8,12$), so the condition is one-sided and is not $\|\mathcal T\|\le1$.

\emph{Candidate route (heuristic; Fej\'er--Riesz over-domination).}  Formally $\mathcal T$ is a
Toeplitz-type form whose symbol would be $2\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\cos(u\log n)$ divided by the
archimedean weight; this series does not converge on the real line, so the symbol is only formal.  The scalar level-one
theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:level-one-scalar}) gives only the diagonal datum
$\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)\ge0$ for $\Re s>1$; it does not by itself prove nonnegativity of the full Toeplitz
symbol.  If an independent identification and a nonnegative boundary symbol were proved, a
Fej\'er--Riesz spectral factor
$\mathcal W_A-\mathcal T_\Lambda=\mathcal D^*\mathcal D$ would follow in the valid archimedean range.
The required boundary nonnegativity is equivalent to the terminal positivity, so this is a conditional
reformulation rather than a proof.

\emph{Where it stalls.}  The scalar positivity of Theorem~\ref{thm:level-one-scalar} holds on
$\Re s>1$, i.e.\ for gauges $z_0=t_0-iy$ with $y>\half$.  The jet positivity required for all $N$ is
not a consequence of it: by Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(a), $C(z_0)\succeq0$ for all $N$ at any
single gauge with $y>\half$ is already equivalent to RH, and by Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(b) the
same holds at the boundary gauge $y=\half$ ($\Re s_0=1$).  We therefore record the target as
\[
   \textbf{(C)}\qquad \delta_N^{\rm ref}(t_0-\tfrac i2)\ge0\quad\text{for all }N,
\]
which is equivalent to RH by Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(b), since $G_N\succ0$.  (On the critical
line $\Re s=\half$ one has $\Re(\xi'/\xi)(s)=0$ identically, because $\xi(\half+it)$ is real, so the
scalar quantity carries no sign information there.)

\emph{Numerical observations bearing on (C)} (unverified floating-point computations).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(E\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Real roots.}  The computed arithmetic minimizer roots are real to working precision and
lie near the zeros (table above).
\item \emph{Analytic factor.}  The Cholesky factor $B_N(t_0)$ of $A_N-\mathcal P_\Lambda$ varies
smoothly across a zero gap (e.g.\ $B_{11}$ decreasing monotonically $1.72\!\cdot\!10^{-4}\to
6.2\!\cdot\!10^{-5}$ over $[295.57,297.98]$ at $N=8$), and its last diagonal entry decreases across the gap
in the computed range.
\item \emph{No-margin threshold.}  Displacing one zero off the line by $\varepsilon$ flips $\delta_N<0$
at a computed threshold that decreases rapidly with $N$ (three values):
$\varepsilon^*(N)\approx1.3\!\cdot\!10^{-2},\,5.2\!\cdot\!10^{-4},\,1.1\!\cdot\!10^{-5}$ for
$N=6,8,10$.
\end{enumerate}
The argument thus stands at inequality (C), which is equivalent to RH.

\subsubsection*{The terminal jets are the Li--Keiper coefficients pulled into the convergent region}

Inequality (C) is not new: by Theorem~\ref{thm:li-dictionary} below it is equivalent, through RH,
to the Li--Keiper criterion.

\begin{lemma}[boundary regularity of the fixed-$N$ jets]\label{lem:boundary-continuity}
Fix $t_0$, $N$, and $Y>\half$.  The maps
\[
   y\longmapsto J_N(t_0-iy),\qquad
   y\longmapsto G_N(t_0-iy),\qquad y\in[\half,Y],
\]
are continuous in operator norm, $G_N(t_0-iy)\succ0$ throughout the interval, and
\[
   \delta_N^{\rm ref}(t_0-\tfrac i2)=\lim_{y\downarrow1/2}\delta_N^{\rm ref}(t_0-iy).
\]
Consequently, if $J_N(t_0-iy)\succeq0$ for every $y$ in a punctured interval
$\half<y\le\half+\varepsilon$, then
\[
   J_N(t_0-\tfrac i2)\succeq0.
\]
Equivalently, fixed-$N$ interior positivity passes to the boundary whenever that interior positivity is
available.  The lemma does not assert interior positivity and does not interchange the limits
$y\downarrow\half$ and $N\to\infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:xi-basic} gives $|\Im z_\rho|<\half$ for every zero coordinate.  The zero set is discrete,
so the compact segment $\{t_0-iy:y\in[\half,Y]\}$ has positive distance from it.  The locally uniform
$O(|z_\rho|^{-2})$ majorant in Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel}(iii) permits entrywise dominated convergence
for the finitely many entries of $J_N$.  In finite dimension this is operator-norm continuity.  The
explicit reference Gram is continuous as well; since it is positive definite at every point of the
compact interval, its least eigenvalue has a positive minimum there.  Functional calculus therefore
makes
\[
   H_N(y):=G_N(t_0-iy)^{-1/2}J_N(t_0-iy)G_N(t_0-iy)^{-1/2}
\]
operator-norm continuous.  The smallest eigenvalue is $1$-Lipschitz with respect to the operator norm,
so $\delta_N^{\rm ref}(t_0-iy)=\lambda_{\min}(H_N(y))$ is continuous.

Finally, the cone of positive-semidefinite matrices is closed.  Thus
$J_N(t_0-iy)\succeq0$ for $y\downarrow\half$ implies
$J_N(t_0-\tfrac i2)\succeq0$.  No uniformity in $N$ has been used.
\end{proof}

\begin{convention}[symmetric zero summation]\label{conv:symmetric-sum}
Every sum $\sum_\rho$ over nontrivial zeros in this paper, when the individual terms are not summable
in absolute value, denotes the symmetric limit
\[
   \sum_\rho(\cdots)
   :=
   \lim_{T\to\infty}\sum_{|\Im\rho|\le T}(\cdots),
\]
zeros counted with multiplicity, and every pairing invoked (e.g.\ $\rho\leftrightarrow1-\rho$,
$\rho\leftrightarrow\bar\rho$) is applied within a common symmetric truncation before the limit is
taken.  This is the classical convention under which the Li generating function and the Li--Keiper
criterion are stated \cite{Li,Keiper1992,BombieriLagarias1999}, and it is the convention used for
$\lambda_n$, $S_m=\sum_\rho\rho^{-m}$, and every partial-fraction manipulation of these sums below.  The
absolutely convergent sums $\sum_\rho m_\rho|z_\rho|^{-2}$, $C_{jk}(z_0)$, and $K_\Xi(z,w)$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel} require no such convention; the symmetric limit is needed only where $S_1$
(equivalently $\lambda_1$) itself is involved, since $\sum_\rho|\rho|^{-1}$ diverges.
\end{convention}

\begin{theorem}[Li--Keiper dictionary for the terminal gauge]\label{thm:li-dictionary}
Fix the Cayley coordinate $w_{z_0}(z)=\dfrac{z-z_0}{z-\bar z_0}$ at a gauge $z_0=t_0-iy$, $y\ge\half$.
For $y=\half$, $t_0=0$ (the boundary point $z_0=-\tfrac{i}{2}$, i.e.\ $s_0=1$), and a nontrivial zero
$\rho$ with $z$-coordinate $z_\rho=(\rho-\half)/i$,
\[
   w_{-i/2}(z_\rho)\ =\ 1-\frac1\rho .
\]
Consequently, writing $a_\rho=(z_\rho-z_0)^{-1}$, the boundary terminal jet is
$C_{jk}(-\tfrac{i}{2})=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,a_\rho^{\,j+1}(z_\rho-\bar z_0)^{-(k+1)}$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel}(iii)); under RH this reads
$\sum_\rho m_\rho\,a_\rho^{\,j+1}\overline{a_\rho}^{\,k+1}$, the Gram
matrix of the vectors $(a_\rho^{\,j+1})_{j}$, $a_\rho=i/(\rho-1)$, against the weights $m_\rho$; and
\[
   \delta_N(-\tfrac{i}{2})\ge0\ \ \forall N
   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   \lambda_n:=\sum_\rho\Bigl(1-(1-\tfrac1\rho)^n\Bigr)\ge0\ \ \forall n,
\]
the zero sum taken in the symmetric sense of Convention~\ref{conv:symmetric-sum},
both being equivalent to RH --- the left side by the scalar one-point jet serialization,
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}, applied \emph{directly at the Li gauge} $z_0=-\tfrac i2$ (case (b): the
gauge lies on the boundary of the Pick domain $U_-$ but at positive, unconditional distance from the
zero set of $\Xi$, so it is a regular base point for the kernel), the right side by the
Li~\cite{Li}--Keiper~\cite{Keiper1992} criterion (see also Bombieri--Lagarias
\cite{BombieriLagarias1999}).  For $y>\half$ the entries $C_{jk}(t_0-iy)$ are real-analytic in $(t_0,y)$ and, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-continuity}, continuous up to $y=\half$; positivity for all $N$ at any single
such gauge is again equivalent to RH by Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(a).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $z_\rho=(\rho-\half)/i=-i(\rho-\half)$.  Then $z_\rho+\tfrac{i}{2}=-i(\rho-\half)+\tfrac{i}{2}
=-i\rho+i=-i(\rho-1)$ and $z_\rho-\tfrac{i}{2}=-i(\rho-\half)-\tfrac{i}{2}=-i\rho$, so
\[
   w_{-i/2}(z_\rho)=\frac{z_\rho-(-\tfrac{i}{2})}{z_\rho-(+\tfrac{i}{2})}
   =\frac{-i(\rho-1)}{-i\rho}=\frac{\rho-1}{\rho}=1-\frac1\rho,
\]
the Li variable, of modulus $1$ under RH.  The scalar jet identity
$C_{jk}(z_0)=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,(z_\rho-z_0)^{-(j+1)}(z_\rho-\bar z_0)^{-(k+1)}$ is
Lemma~\ref{lem:mxi-kernel}(iii); under RH, $z_\rho-\bar z_0=\overline{z_\rho-z_0}$, so with
$a_\rho=(z_\rho-z_0)^{-1}$ it reads $C_{jk}=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,a_\rho^{\,j+1}\overline{a_\rho}^{\,k+1}$
and exhibits $C(z_0)$ as the Gram matrix of the vectors
$(a_\rho^{\,j+1})_j$ against the positive weights $m_\rho$, hence positive semidefinite whenever the
$z_\rho$ are real.  Its positivity for all $N$ is equivalent to RH by
Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}, applied directly at $z_0=-\tfrac i2$: the distance hypothesis is case (b)
of that theorem --- a zero of $\Xi$ within distance $\delta$ of $-\tfrac i2$ would be a zero of $\zeta$
within $\delta$ of $s=1$, excluded unconditionally by the classical zero-free region --- so no passage
of positivity from the interior gauges to the boundary is used in either direction.
The Li--Keiper positivity $\lambda_n\ge0$ for all $n$ is equivalent to
RH by \cite{Li,Keiper1992}.  The two positivity families are therefore equivalent to each other, and
the boundary jet is built from the very Li variable $1-1/\rho$ by the first identity.  For $y>\half$ the entries are real-analytic in $(t_0,y)$ (they depend on $z_0$ and $\bar z_0$) and
continuous up to $y=\half$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-continuity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the dictionary is an all-orders equivalence, not a levelwise transform]\label{rmk:li-levelwise}
The equivalence of Theorem~\ref{thm:li-dictionary} holds between the \emph{complete} families
($\forall N$ versus $\forall n$), through RH; it is not a finite-order positivity-preserving triangular
transform, and the two truncated hierarchies are genuinely different at each finite level.  This is
visible already at $N=2$.  Partial fractions against the pairing $\rho\leftrightarrow1-\rho$
(so $\sum_\rho1/(\rho-1)=-\lambda_1$ and $\sum_\rho1/\rho^2=2\lambda_1-\lambda_2$) give, for the
boundary jets $C_{jk}=i^{\,j+k+2}\sum_\rho m_\rho(\rho-1)^{-(j+1)}\rho^{-(k+1)}$,
\[
   C_{00}=2\lambda_1,\qquad
   C_{01}=\overline{C_{10}}=i\,(4\lambda_1-\lambda_2),\qquad
   C_{11}=8\lambda_1-2\lambda_2,
\]
hence the exact identity
\[
   \det J_2(-\tfrac i2)
   =
   C_{00}C_{11}-|C_{01}|^2
   =
   \lambda_2\,(4\lambda_1-\lambda_2).
\]
Thus $J_1\succeq0$ is exactly $\lambda_1\ge0$ (with the harmless factor $2$), while $J_2\succeq0$ is
equivalent to $\lambda_1\ge0$, $\lambda_2\ge0$, \emph{and} $\lambda_2\le4\lambda_1$ --- strictly
stronger than finite Li positivity at that level, and not implied by it: every jet entry lies in the
integer span of $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{j+k+1}$, yet the two truncated positivity hierarchies
coincide only in the $\forall N/\forall n$ limit, where both are RH.
Numerically $4\lambda_1-\lambda_2=3.71\cdot10^{-5}>0$ and
$\det J_2=\lambda_2(4\lambda_1-\lambda_2)\approx3.4\cdot10^{-6}>0$, consistent with RH.  This is one
more manifestation of conservation of difficulty: the dictionary changes coordinates on the same
terminal positivity, it does not decompose it into weaker levelwise statements.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[what the dictionary buys]\label{rmk:li-buys}
The Li--Keiper coefficients live exactly at the edge $\Re s=1$, where the prime series does not converge
absolutely and, at $s=1$ itself, diverges (the pole is compensated as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-kernel}).  For $y>\half$ the jets are evaluated where the prime series converges
absolutely, and their $1\times1$ entry is nonnegative by Theorem~\ref{thm:level-one-scalar}.
Positivity of all finite sections at any such gauge is still equivalent to RH
(Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(a)), and the passage to $y=\half$ at fixed $N$ is a continuity
statement (Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-continuity}); the difficulty is not in the boundary passage.
Numerically: the closed-form $\tfrac1{(n-1)!}\frac{d^n}{ds^n}
[s^{n-1}\log\xi]_{s=1}$ gives $\lambda_{1..5}=0.023096,\,0.092346,\,0.207639,\,0.368790,\,0.575543$,
the known values, and $|w_{-i/2}(u)|=1$ for real $u$ (exactly, since $|u+\tfrac i2|=|u-\tfrac i2|$).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[the boundary passage is regular at fixed $N$]
\label{rmk:boundary-regular}
In floating-point computations (unverified), fixing $N$ and lowering $y$ toward the Li boundary, the
sampling margin $\delta_N(t_0-iy)$ stayed positive with no visible degeneration near $y=\half$; e.g.\ at
$t_0\approx297.24$ (sampling coordinate, finite-zero truncation; the values at $y=1$ differ in the second digit from
those of the three-law table, which were computed in the arithmetic coordinate; the normalisation,
$\delta_N$ or $\delta_N/L$, of these unverified values was not recorded),
\[
   \begin{array}{c|ccc}
      y & N=6 & N=8 & N=10\\\hline
      1.00 & 7.9\!\cdot\!10^{-5} & 7.6\!\cdot\!10^{-8} & 1.8\!\cdot\!10^{-11}\\
      0.60 & 3.9\!\cdot\!10^{-7} & 4.0\!\cdot\!10^{-11} & 1.2\!\cdot\!10^{-15}\\
      0.505& 6.1\!\cdot\!10^{-8} & 3.1\!\cdot\!10^{-12} & 4.6\!\cdot\!10^{-17}
   \end{array}
\]
Analytically, Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-continuity} proves continuity and conditional sign
transfer, not positivity of every fixed-$N$ section.  The remaining content includes establishing the
positivity hypothesis for all $N$ at the boundary --- equivalently a statement about
$\lambda_n=\lambda_n^{\rm arch}+\lambda_n^{\rm prime}$, where the archimedean part
$\lambda_n^{\rm arch}\sim\tfrac{n}{2}\log n$ (Bombieri--Lagarias \cite{BombieriLagarias1999}) must
not be exceeded in absolute value by a negative prime part.  The open statement is
$\lambda_n^{\rm prime}\ge-\lambda_n^{\rm arch}$ for all $n$, i.e.\ Li's criterion, equivalent to RH.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{Chain $\Omega$: the end-to-end path and the shape of its single residue}

We assemble the whole route as one linear chain, marking each $\Omega$-link Closed or, for the one
terminal residue, stating it exactly.  The
$\Omega$-chain is the third and deepest level of the logical hierarchy of \S\ref{sec:status-map}: it
lives below the H-level analysis of Step~5, and its entries are typed --- $\Omega_1$--$\Omega_6$ are
proved characterizations or scoped conditional transport statements, while $\Omega_7$ is the terminal
statement the analysis isolates.  The table is not an unconditional level-by-level propagation of
positivity from $\Omega_4$ through $\Omega_6$.

\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.05\linewidth}p{0.68\linewidth}p{0.21\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
& Statement & Logical type\\\midrule
$\Omega_1$ & RH $\Longleftrightarrow$ all $\Xi$-zeros real (Lemma~\ref{lem:rh-translation}) & Closed\\
$\Omega_2$ & real zeros $\Longleftrightarrow$ ARP-P (Steps 1--15, Corollary~\ref{cor:live-equivalence}) & Closed\\
$\Omega_3$ & ARP-P $\Longleftrightarrow$ terminal positivity $\delta_N(z_0)\ge0\ \forall N$ in the
reference-whitened defect (Corollary~\ref{cor:arp-terminal-defect}; the observable is
Definition~\ref{def:terminal-defect-fixed}) & Closed, in the reference-whitened form\\
$\Omega_4$ & $\delta_N\ge0\ \forall N\Longleftrightarrow$
$R_N^{\rm ref}\preceq I\ \forall N$ in the global reference whitening
(Theorem~\ref{thm:reference-whitened-terminal}) & Closed for every $N$; the separate
$J_N^\infty$-whitening is valid only for $N\le N_*(t_0)$\\
$\Omega_5$ & for fixed $N$, operator-norm continuity at $y=\half$ and the conditional implication
$[J_N(t_0-iy)\succeq0\text{ for }y\downarrow\half]\Rightarrow
J_N(t_0-\tfrac i2)\succeq0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:boundary-continuity}) & Closed in this conditional,
fixed-$N$ form; no $N$-uniform positivity asserted\\
$\Omega_6$ & boundary positivity $\Longleftrightarrow$ Li--Keiper $\lambda_n\ge0\ \forall n$, via
$w_{-i/2}=1-1/\rho$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:li-dictionary}) & Closed\\
$\Omega_7$ & $\lambda_n\ge0$ for all $n$, equivalently
$\lambda_n^{\rm prime}\ge-\lambda_n^{\rm arch}$ for all $n$ & \textbf{terminal residue}: open;
$\equiv$ RH (Li)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The links $\Omega_1$--$\Omega_6$ are proved above with the scopes displayed in the table; $\Omega_5$
is a conditional sign-transfer statement, not a proof of its positivity hypothesis.  The links
$\Omega_3$ and $\Omega_6$ are equivalences between statements each of which is separately
equivalent to RH (Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-jets}(a),(b) and Li's criterion); they are not levelwise
implications (Remark~\ref{rmk:li-levelwise}).  $\Omega_4$ is a levelwise restatement, immediate from
$G_N\succ0$, and $\Omega_5$ is not used by $\Omega_6$.  The open entry
$\Omega_7$ is exactly the classical Li criterion $\lambda_n\ge0$ for all $n$, i.e.\ RH itself; the
chain does not reduce RH to a weaker statement.  The following observations concern $\Omega_7$.

\begin{remark}[numerical observation: prime truncations are indefinite]
\label{thm:no-truncation}
For the whitened truncated form
$m_N(X):=\lambda_{\min}\bigl(A_N^{-1/2}(A_N-\mathcal T_\Lambda^{\le X})A_N^{-1/2}\bigr)$, where
$\mathcal T_\Lambda^{\le X}=\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}(S_{\log n}+S_{\log n}^*)$, at the gauge
$t_0\approx297.24$, $y=1$, a floating-point computation (unverified) gives $m_N(50)=-0.53,-0.66,-0.73$
for $N=6,8,10$, while $\delta_N>0$.  This suggests that positivity of $A_N-\mathcal P_\Lambda$ is not
obtained by perturbing a positive finite prime truncation; it is not proved here.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[heuristic remarks on perturbative routes]\label{rmk:omega-shape}
Remark~\ref{thm:no-truncation} suggests that the archimedean part does not dominate the prime part
termwise or up to a controllable tail.  Two related perturbative routes face the same issue
heuristically: mollifying the symbol (a deeper gauge) is the $X\to\infty$ end of the same truncation;
and a backward heat flow from $t>\Lambda_{\rm dBN}$, where Polymath15 \cite{PolymathDBN} gives real
zeros, would have to propagate a margin that, heuristically and without proof, behaves like
$\delta_N\asymp(\kappa L/N)^{2N}$ against a polynomial drift, which would control only a range
$N\le N_*(L)$.  None of this is proved here.
The chain ends at the Li criterion, which is equivalent to RH.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{$\Omega_7$ up close: exact structure, the archimedean trend, and the detection mechanism}

We record the structure of $\Omega_7$, together with some numerical values (floating-point
computations whose code is not included; unverified unless stated otherwise).

\begin{proposition}[the pairing and the on-line sum-of-squares]\label{prop:li-sos}
Nontrivial zeros pair as $\rho\leftrightarrow1-\rho$, and the Li variables are reciprocal:
$w_{1-\rho}=1/w_\rho$, $w_\rho=1-1/\rho$.  Moreover
\[
   |w_\rho|=\frac{|\rho-1|}{|\rho|}
   \begin{cases} =1,&\Re\rho=\tfrac12,\\ >1,&\Re\rho<\tfrac12,\\<1,&\Re\rho>\tfrac12.\end{cases}
\]
Consequently, if RH holds then, pairing $\rho=\tfrac12+i\gamma$ with $\bar\rho=1-\rho$ and writing
$w_\rho=e^{i\theta_\gamma}$,
\[
   \lambda_n=\sum_{\gamma>0}\bigl(2-2\cos n\theta_\gamma\bigr)
   =4\sum_{\gamma>0}\sin^2\!\tfrac{n\theta_\gamma}{2}\ \ge\ 0,
\]
a manifest sum of squares.  Thus $\Omega_7$ is trivial \emph{given} RH; its content is establishing the
same nonnegativity from the arithmetic definition of $\lambda_n$, in which the zeros do not appear.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$w_\rho w_{1-\rho}=\frac{\rho-1}{\rho}\cdot\frac{-\rho}{1-\rho}=1$; and $|w_\rho|=|\rho-1|/|\rho|$ with
$|\rho-1|^2-|\rho|^2=1-2\Re\rho$ gives the trichotomy.  For $\Re\rho=\tfrac12$, $1-\rho=\bar\rho$, so
the pair contributes $[1-w^n]+[1-\overline{w}^{\,n}]=2-2\Re w^n=2-2\cos n\theta_\gamma$.  Summing over
$\gamma>0$ and using $\theta_\gamma=O(1/\gamma)$ (whence the terms are $O(n^2/\gamma^2)$ for
$\gamma\gg n$) gives an absolutely convergent nonnegative series.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the exact trend and the computed margin]\label{rmk:li-trend}
Splitting $\log\xi=\bigl[\log s-\tfrac s2\log\pi+\log\Gamma(\tfrac s2)\bigr]+\log\bigl((s-1)\zeta(s)
\bigr)$ gives, through $\lambda_n=n\sum_{k=1}^n\binom{n-1}{k-1}\sigma_k$ with
$\sigma_k=[(s-1)^k]\log\xi(s)$ (closed form: polygamma for the archimedean part, Stieltjes constants
$\gamma_j$ for the $\zeta$ part), an \emph{unconditional} exact decomposition
$\lambda_n=\lambda_n^{\rm arch}+\lambda_n^{\rm prime}$.  In floating-point computations to $n=120$ (unverified): every $\lambda_n>0$; the
archimedean part follows the asymptotic trend
$\lambda_n^{\rm arch}=\tfrac n2\log\tfrac n{2\pi}+\tfrac{\gamma-1}{2}n+\tfrac34+O(n^{-1})$
(at $n=40,80$ the exact values $29.313$, $85.604$ agree with the trend to about $10^{-3}$); and the prime oscillation is a small
relative fluctuation, $|\lambda_n^{\rm prime}|/\lambda_n^{\rm arch}$ falling to
$0.002$--$0.04$ for $n\ge30$ (still $0.083$ at $n=20$).  Under RH this smallness is forced by $|w_\rho|=1$; unconditionally it is
exactly what is at stake.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[sign range of the archimedean Li contribution]\label{lem:li-arch-sign}
For the split used in Remark~\ref{rmk:li-trend},
\[
   \lambda_n^{\rm arch}
   =1-\frac n2\bigl(\gamma+\log(4\pi)\bigr)
    +\sum_{\substack{r\ge1\\ r\ \mathrm{odd}}}
      \left[\left(1-\frac1r\right)^n-1+\frac nr\right].
\]
Moreover,
\[
   \lambda_n^{\rm arch}<0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad1\le n\le7.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The displayed series follows by applying the Li binomial transform to
$\log s-\tfrac s2\log\pi+\log\Gamma(s/2)$ and using the series $\psi(z)=-\gamma+\sum_{k\ge0}(\frac1{k+1}-\frac1{k+z})$.  Expanding
$(1-1/r)^n$ binomially and summing over odd $r$ shows that the series equals the finite sum
