 \sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 \left[1+e^{t(A_n+b_n+p_n(\varepsilon))}
 \prod_{m\ge2}\exp\!\left(
 -{t\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right)\right]^{-1}.}              \tag{FD4}
\]
The exponential auxiliary partition has the parallel exact form
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal Z_t(X)
 &=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
 \sum_{n\le X}{e^{-t(A_n+p_n(\varepsilon))}\over n}
 \exp\!\left(
 t\sum_{m\ge2}{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right)\\
 &=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
 \sum_{n\le X}{e^{-t(A_n+p_n(\varepsilon))}\over n}
 \prod_{m\ge2}\exp\!\left(
 {t\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right).
\end{aligned}}                                                            \tag{LS5}
\]
For each $\varepsilon>0$ the product is absolutely convergent and positive.
Formulas (FD4) and (LS5) retain the pole, all prime powers, and their
multiplicative interactions until after the nonlinear observable is formed.
They are exact coordinates, not estimates.  The criterion is
the limit in (FD2); neither that limit nor the stronger bound in (LS4) is
proved here.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under RH, $\lambda_n\ge0$, so the summand in (FD1) is at most
$(1+e^{tb_n})^{-1}\to0$; its logarithmic mean tends to zero.  Under
$\neg\RH$, Proposition~\ref{prop:li-syndetic-density-criteria} supplies a
syndetic set $\mathcal S$ of positive logarithmic density on which
$\lambda_n\le-cR^n$.  Since $b_n=e^{o(n)}$, the summand in (FD1) tends to
one along $\mathcal S$, and therefore
$\liminf_X\mathfrak F_{t,b}(X)>0$.  This proves (FD2).

For the auxiliary criterion, RH gives
$\mathcal Z_t(X)\le\sum_{n\le X}n^{-1}$.  Under $\neg\RH$, choose
$n\in\mathcal S$; then
\[
 \mathcal Z_t(n)\ge n^{-1}\exp(tcR^n),
\]
which excludes an $O_t(\log n)$ bound.  The finite Laguerre expansion and
absolute convergence of $-\zeta'/\zeta$ in $\Re s>1$ give the displayed
formula for $\mathcal E_{n,\varepsilon}$.  Exponentiating it and summing over the
finite set $n\le X$ proves (LS5); inserting the same identity into the
continuous logistic function proves (FD4).  Absolute convergence permits
both product notations.  Finally, the single $n$th summand in an
$O_t(\log n)$ bound for $\mathcal Z_t(n)$ gives (FD3).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Fourier form of the Fermi criterion and its microfrequency scale]
\label{rmk:fermi-microfrequency-gate}
For $\ell(y)=(1+e^y)^{-1}$ one has the exact Fourier formula
\[
 \ell(y)={1\over2}-\int_0^\infty{\sin(sy)\over\sinh(\pi s)}\,ds.          \tag{FM1}
\]
With $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon}=A_n+p_n(\varepsilon)-Q_{n,\varepsilon}$,
where
\[
 Q_{n,\varepsilon}=\sum_{m\ge2}{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m),
\]
the characteristic lift
\[
 \mathcal C_{t,X,\varepsilon}(s)
 ={1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{e^{ist(A_n+b_n+p_n(\varepsilon))}\over n}
 \prod_{m\ge2}\exp\!\left(
 -{ist\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right)
                                                                    \tag{FM2}
\]
has modulus at most one and gives
\[
 \mathfrak F_{t,b}(X)={1\over2}-\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
 \int_0^\infty{\Im\mathcal C_{t,X,\varepsilon}(s)\over\sinh(\pi s)}\,ds.
                                                                    \tag{FM3}
\]
Thus there is an exact unitary prime--Laguerre lift, but unitarity does not
control the required limit.  Indeed, for an excursion of depth $Y$ and
$S\ge1$, $Y\ge\pi S$,
\[
 \left|\int_0^{S/Y}{\sin(sY)\over\sinh(\pi s)}\,ds-{1\over2}\right|
 \le {2\over\pi S}+{\pi S^2\over2Y^2},
 \qquad
 \left|\int_\delta^\infty{\sin(sY)\over\sinh(\pi s)}\,ds\right|
 \le {2\over Y\sinh(\pi\delta)}.                         \tag{FM4}
\]
The off-line signal therefore lives at the microfrequency $s=O(Y^{-1})$;
if $Y\asymp R^n$, every polynomial frequency floor misses it.

The diagonal substitution $s=e^{-nv}$ moves that transition to
$v=\log R$, but does not sum it.  The Li generating function controls the
geometric product
\[
 \exp\!\left(iu\sum_{n\ge1}{z^n\lambda_n\over n}\right)
 =\prod_{n\ge1}\exp\!\left({iu z^n\lambda_n\over n}\right),             \tag{FM5}
\]
only in the disk of convergence $|z|<1/R$ (with $R=\max_\rho|1-1/\rho|$;
$R=1$ under RH).  If $\neg\RH$ and
$|z|R\ge1$, the coefficient series in (FM5) does not converge; a value
obtained from $\xi'/\xi$ there is analytic continuation, not the displayed
series.
By contrast, (FM3) requires an arithmetic mean of the individual nonlinear
phases.  At $zR=1$ every Hadamard power is of order one, so no fixed-order
expansion bridges (FM5) to that mean.  This is an obstruction for the
unitary microfrequency and diagonal approach; it is not a proof of A1 or RH.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[cross-degree energy and the obstruction for bare Parseval--CD bounds]
\label{prop:cross-degree-energy-gate}
Put
\[
 \mathcal E_N:=\sum_{N<n\le2N}{\lambda_n^2\over n},
 \qquad
 r_0:=\min_{\Re\rho>1/2}\left|1-{1\over\rho}\right|,
\]
with the convention $r_0=1$ when there is no such zero.  Then
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathcal E_N^{1/(4N)}={1\over r_0}.}               \tag{EN1}
\]
For each fixed $N$ there is also the exact regularized Parseval identity
\[
 \mathcal E_N=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}{1\over2\pi}
 \int_0^{2\pi}\left|
 \sum_{N<n\le2N}{\lambda_{n,\varepsilon}\over\sqrt n}e^{int}
 \right|^2dt.                                                           \tag{EN2}
\]
The corresponding Laguerre kernel is the Christoffel--Darboux difference
\[
 \mathcal K_N(x,y)=\sum_{n=N+1}^{2N}
 {L_{n-1}^{(1)}(x)L_{n-1}^{(1)}(y)\over n}
 =K_{2N-1}^{(1)}(x,y)-K_{N-1}^{(1)}(x,y).                               \tag{EN3}
\]
These positive identities do not bound the Abel limit.  If
$c_{n,\varepsilon}=A_n+p_n(\varepsilon)$, then each of
\[
 \sum_{N<n\le2N}{c_{n,\varepsilon}^2\over n},\qquad
 \sum_{N<n\le2N}{Q_{n,\varepsilon}^2\over n},\qquad
 \sum_{N<n\le2N}{c_{n,\varepsilon}Q_{n,\varepsilon}\over n}
\]
equals
$\varepsilon^{-4N}(2N)^{-1}(1+O_N(\varepsilon))$, while only their complete
signed combination tends to $\mathcal E_N$.  Triangle, Bessel, or separate
CD estimates therefore lose a cancellation through order
$\varepsilon^{-4N}$.  This is a quantitative obstruction for bounds using only Parseval,
Laguerre orthogonality, and Christoffel--Darboux positivity; (EN1)--(EN3)
alone do not prove A1 or RH.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The generating function
\[
 G(z)=\sum_{n\ge1}\lambda_nz^n
 ={z\over(1-z)^2}{\xi'\over\xi}\!\left({1\over1-z}\right)
\]
has radius exactly $r_0$, because a zero $\rho$ contributes a nonzero pole
at $z=1-1/\rho$.  Cauchy--Hadamard, applied above and below on dyadic
blocks, proves (EN1).  Equation (EN2) is finite Parseval before taking
$\varepsilon\downarrow0$, and Laguerre orthogonality gives (EN3).  Finally
$p_n(\varepsilon)=(-1)^{n-1}\varepsilon^{-n}(1+O_n(\varepsilon))$ and
$Q_{n,\varepsilon}=p_n(\varepsilon)+A_n-\lambda_n+o(1)$ give the three
displayed asymptotics and the obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Abelized Fermi criterion and a single diagonal regulator]
\label{prop:fermi-abel-diagonal}
The logarithmic Ces\`aro--Abel bridge below is a positive zero-limit
specialization of classical logarithmic summability
\cite{BinghamGashi}; its application to the Li--Fermi detector is the point
used here.
Set
\[
 a_n={1\over1+(n+1)e^{\lambda_n}},\qquad
 L(h)=-\log(1-e^{-h}),
\]
and
\[
 \mathfrak A(h)={1\over L(h)}
       \sum_{n\ge1}{e^{-hn}a_n\over n}.                    \tag{FA1}
\]
Then
\[
 {1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{a_n\over n}\longrightarrow0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \mathfrak A(h)\longrightarrow0,                           \tag{FA2}
\]
and, more weakly but equivalently for the Li sequence,
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
        \liminf_{h\downarrow0}\mathfrak A(h)=0.\quad}      \tag{FA3}
\]
Thus it is enough to prove decay on any one prescribed sequence
$h_j\downarrow0$ (for example $h_j=2^{-j}$), because under $\neg\RH$ the
positive lower bound is uniform as $h\downarrow0$.  This is a reduction of
quantifiers, not an estimate.

There is also a single admissible Euler regulator.  With
\[
 \varepsilon(h)=e^{-1/(100h)},\qquad
 r={199\over200},\qquad
 \eta={1\over100}-\log{200\over199}>0,                     \tag{FA4}
\]
with the constant $M=M_r$ defined in the proof, for $0<h\le1$,
\[
 \sup_{1\le n\le1/h}|\lambda_{n,\varepsilon(h)}-\lambda_n|
 \le {2M\over h}e^{-\eta/h}=o(1).                          \tag{FA5}
\]
Consequently (FA3) remains true with every $\lambda_n$ in (FA1) replaced
by $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon(h)}$.  For each $h>0$ this replacement lies in
the half-plane of absolute convergence of the Euler sum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For the bounded nonnegative sequence $a_n$, summation by parts gives the
forward implication in (FA2).  Conversely, setting $h=X^{-1}$ gives
\[
 \sum_{n\le X}{a_n\over n}\le eL(1/X)\mathfrak A(1/X).
\]
Under RH, $a_n\le(n+2)^{-1}$, whereas under $\neg\RH$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-syndetic-density-criteria} gives a set of positive
logarithmic density on which $a_n\to1$.  This proves (FA3).

For the diagonal assertion, put $F(s)=(s-1)\zeta(s)$ and $s(z)=(1-z)^{-1}$.
The paired generating function is
\[
 \sum_{n\ge1}{\lambda_{n,\varepsilon}-\lambda_n\over n}z^n
 =\log{F(s(z)+\varepsilon)\over F(s(z))}
  -\log{F(1+\varepsilon)\over F(1)}.                        \tag{FA6}
\]
The classical bound $|\Im\rho|>14$ makes
$F(s(z)+u)$ zero-free for $|z|<\sqrt{195/196}$ and
$0\le u\le1$: if $s(z)+u=\rho=\beta+i\gamma$ then
$|z|^2=1+(1-2(\beta-u))/|\rho-u|^2\ge1-1/\gamma^2>195/196$.
Put $M=\max\{|F'/F(s(z)+u)|:|z|=r,\ 0\le u\le1\}$; then
$|\log F(s(z)+\varepsilon)-\log F(s(z))|\le M\varepsilon$ on $|z|=r$ for
$0<\varepsilon\le1$, and Cauchy's estimate gives
\[
 |\lambda_{n,\varepsilon}-\lambda_n|
 \le2Mn\varepsilon r^{-n},
\]
which yields (FA5).  The logistic derivative is at most $1/4$ and the
Abel tail $n>h^{-1}$ is $O(1)/L(h)=o(1)$, proving the last claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[double-logarithmic boundary and conditioning wall]
The diagonal in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermi-abel-diagonal} removes an
exchange of limits, but it does not make the arithmetic estimate easier.
For the model off-line quartet $w=2i$ one expects that imposing a lower
cut-off $s\ge\delta_\alpha(h)$ in the Fourier integral (FM3), with
\[
 \delta_\alpha(h)=\exp(-h^{-\alpha}),\qquad0<\alpha<1,
\]
discards a positive proportion of the off-line signal, which is expected to
extend down to $s=\exp(-c/h)$; we do not prove this here (heuristic).

Moreover
\[
 p_n(\varepsilon)=1+(-1)^{n-1}
       \left({1-\varepsilon\over\varepsilon}\right)^n.     \tag{FA7}
\]
For $n\asymp h^{-1}$ and (FA4), the polar and prime channels separately
have size $\exp(1/(100h^2)+o(h^{-2}))$, while their paired difference has
only $\exp(O(1/h))$ growth.  A separated estimate would require relative
precision $\exp(-1/(100h^2)+O(1/h))$.  This is a quantitative obstruction,
not a proof that no future signed estimate of the complete product exists.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[finite local filters do not weaken the exterior mode]
\label{prop:li-local-filter-gate}
Let $E$ denote degree shift and let $P\in\mathbb R[z]$ be fixed.  If RH is
false, write the dominant expansion as
\[
 \lambda_n=-R^nS(n)+O(R_1^n),\qquad1<R_1<R,
\]
as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:li-syndetic-density-criteria}
(where $S$ is the trigonometric polynomial denoted $P$ there), with exterior modes $u_j=Re^{i\phi_j}$.  Then either
$P(u_j)=0$ for every maximal mode, or there are $c_P>0$ and a syndetic set
$D_P$ of positive natural density such that
\[
 (P(E)\lambda)_n\le-c_PR^n\qquad(n\in D_P)                 \tag{FF1}
\]
for all sufficiently large $n$.  In particular fixed finite differences
$P(z)=(z-1)^k$ and uniform moving averages
\[
 P(z)={1+z+\cdots+z^J\over J+1}
\]
never annihilate an exterior mode and retain the same exponential
obstruction.

The alternative of annihilation is real.  For
\[
 Q_n=4-2\Re\{(2i)^n+(2i)^{-n}\},
\]
the filter $P(E)=E^2+4$ gives
\[
 Q_{n+2}+4Q_n=20-{15\over2}\Re( (2i)^{-n})>0,              \tag{FF2}
\]
although $Q_{4k}<-1$ for every $k\ge1$.  Hence a filter which kills an unknown exterior mode
has merely encoded that mode; it has not proved a block criterion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Filtering the dominant expansion multiplies each character by $P(u_j)$.
If the resulting real trigonometric polynomial is nonzero, its Haar mean on
the compact monothetic phase group is zero.  It is therefore positive on a
nonempty open set.  Returns to that set have positive density and are
syndetic, and the $R^n$ term dominates there, proving (FF1).  The two stated
filter polynomials have no zero of modulus greater than one.  Formula (FF2)
is direct substitution.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[bilateral syndetic excursions]
\label{prop:li-bilateral-excursions}
If RH is false, there are $R>1$, $c>0$, and two syndetic sets
$D_+,D_-$, each of positive natural density, such that
\[
 \lambda_n\ge cR^n\quad(n\in D_+),\qquad
 \lambda_n\le-cR^n\quad(n\in D_-)                         \tag{FB1}
\]
eventually.  Consequently each of
\[
 \lambda_n\le e^{\sqrt n},\qquad
 \lambda_n\ge-e^{\sqrt n},\qquad
 |\lambda_n|\le e^{\sqrt n}                               \tag{FB2}
\]
holding on consecutive blocks of unbounded length is equivalent to RH.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the dominant phase group, the nonzero trigonometric polynomial $S$ has
Haar mean zero: all its characters are nontrivial because nontrivial zeta
zeros have nonzero ordinate.  Hence $S$ is positive and negative on
nonempty open sets.  The two return sets are syndetic, and the two signs in
(FB1) follow from the dominant expansion.  Under RH,
$\lambda_n=O(n\log n)$; under $\neg\RH$, (FB1) defeats each subexponential
barrier in (FB2).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[deterministic windows and the noninteracting polymer]
\label{prop:li-deterministic-polymer}
Let
\[
 I_L=\{L^2,L^2+1,\ldots,L^2+L-1\},\qquad
 a_n={1\over1+(n+1)e^{\lambda_n}},
\]
and $P_L=\prod_{n\in I_L}(1-a_n)$.  Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \sum_{n\in I_L}a_n\longrightarrow0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad P_L\longrightarrow1.\quad} \tag{FP1}
\]
If RH is false, the corresponding limits satisfy
$\liminf_L\sum_{I_L}a_n\ge1$ and $P_L\to0$.

Writing $z_n=e^{-\lambda_n}/(n+1)$ gives the exact finite polymer identity
\[
 P_L^{-1}=\prod_{n\in I_L}(1+z_n)
 =\sum_{S\subseteq I_L}
 {\exp(-\sum_{n\in S}\lambda_n)\over\prod_{n\in S}(n+1)}. \tag{FP2}
\]
Its regulated prime--Laguerre lift is
\[
\begin{aligned}
 P_L^{-1}=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
 \sum_{S\subseteq I_L}
 &{\exp(-\sum_{n\in S}\{A_n+p_n(\varepsilon)\})
   \over\prod_{n\in S}(n+1)}\\
 &\times\prod_{m\ge2}\exp\!\left(
 {\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 \sum_{n\in S}L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right).
\end{aligned}                                               \tag{FP3}
\]
The inner limit can also be removed.  Put
\[
 N_L=L^2+L-1,\qquad \varepsilon_L=e^{-N_L/100}.
\]
If $P_{L,\varepsilon_L}$ denotes the same product with
$\lambda_n$ replaced by $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_L}$, then
\[
 |P_{L,\varepsilon_L}-P_L|
 +\left|\sum_{n\in I_L}(a_{n,\varepsilon_L}-a_n)\right|
 \le MLN_Le^{-\eta N_L}=o(1),                             \tag{FP4}
\]
with $M,\eta$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermi-abel-diagonal}.
Thus (FP1) remains equivalent after this diagonal replacement, and (FP3)
becomes a single absolutely convergent Euler expression at
$\varepsilon=\varepsilon_L$, with no inner limit.
This is a noninteracting gas: all activities are positive and
$\log P_L^{-1}=\sum_{I_L}\log(1+z_n)$.  Thus (FP3) is an exact deterministic
reduction, not a new source of cancellation between degrees.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under RH, $a_n\le(n+2)^{-1}$, so
$\sum_{I_L}a_n\le L/(L^2+2)$.  If RH is false, the negative syndetic set
in Proposition~\ref{prop:li-syndetic-density-criteria} meets every $I_L$
for large $L$, at an index $d_L$ where $a_{d_L}\to1$.  The product is then
at most $1-a_{d_L}\to0$.  The elementary inequalities
\[
 1-\sum a_n\le\prod(1-a_n)\le1,
 \qquad {z\over1+z}\le\log(1+z)
\]
give the equivalences.  Expanding the finite product proves (FP2), and
substitution of $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon}$ proves (FP3).  The Cauchy estimate
(FA5), the $1/4$-Lipschitz property of the logistic function, and the
telescoping bound
$|\prod x_j-\prod y_j|\le\sum|x_j-y_j|$ for $x_j,y_j\in[0,1]$
give (FP4).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[entropic dual of the deterministic block]
\label{prop:li-entropic-block}
For $n\in I_L$ put $\pi_n=(n+2)^{-1}$ and let
$\nu_L=\bigotimes_{n\in I_L}{\rm Bern}(\pi_n)$.  If
\[
 Y_L(B)=\sum_{n\in I_L}B_n\lambda_n,\qquad
 Z_L=\mathbb E_{\nu_L}e^{-Y_L},
\]
then
\[
 P_L^{-1}=C_LZ_L,\qquad
 C_L={L^2+L+1\over L^2+1}.                               \tag{EV1}
\]
Moreover the finite Gibbs variational identity reduces exactly to Bernoulli
marginals:
\[
\boxed{\quad
 \log Z_L
 =\sup_{q\in[0,1]^{I_L}}
 \left\{-\sum_{n\in I_L}q_n\lambda_n
 -\sum_{n\in I_L}d(q_n\Vert\pi_n)\right\},\quad}          \tag{EV2}
\]
where
\[
 d(q\Vert p)=q\log{q\over p}+(1-q)\log{1-q\over1-p}.
\]
The maximizer is $q_n=[1+(n+1)e^{\lambda_n}]^{-1}$.

With $N_L=L^2+L-1$, $\varepsilon_L=e^{-N_L/100}$, and
\[
 G_{L,q}(x)=\sum_{n\in I_L}q_nL_{n-1}^{(1)}(x),
\]
the regulated variational functional is the absolutely convergent expression
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal V_{L,\varepsilon_L}(q)
={}&\sum_{m\ge2}{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon_L}}
       G_{L,q}(\log m)
 -\sum_{n\in I_L}q_n\{A_n+p_n(\varepsilon_L)\}\\
 &-\sum_{n\in I_L}d(q_n\Vert\pi_n).
\end{aligned}                                               \tag{EV3}
\]
If RH is false, then for some $R>1,c>0$,
\[
 \log Z_L\ge cR^{L^2}-O(\log L).                          \tag{EV4}
\]
Consequently RH follows if, on any unbounded subsequence of $L$, one proves
\[
 \sup_q\mathcal V_{L,\varepsilon_L}(q)\le B_L,\qquad
 B_L\ge0,\qquad \log(1+B_L)=o(L^2).                       \tag{EV5}
\]
This permits $B_L=\exp(o(L^2))$.  However, (EV2) creates no connected
interaction: every correlated law pays its multiinformation and the
maximizer is the product law above.  Thus (EV3)--(EV5) are a weak,
uniformly linearized target, not its proof.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For one Bernoulli site,
\[
 \mathbb E e^{-B_n\lambda_n}
 ={n+1\over n+2}\left(1+{e^{-\lambda_n}\over n+1}\right).
\]
Multiplication and
$\prod_{n=L^2}^{L^2+L-1}(n+1)/(n+2)=C_L^{-1}$ prove (EV1).
The finite Gibbs formula gives
\[
 \log Z_L=\sup_\mu\{-\mathbb E_\mu Y_L-D(\mu\Vert\nu_L)\}.
\]
If $q_n=\mathbb E_\mu B_n$ and
$\nu_q=\bigotimes{\rm Bern}(q_n)$, the entropy chain rule is
\[
 D(\mu\Vert\nu_L)=D(\mu\Vert\nu_q)
   +\sum_nd(q_n\Vert\pi_n).
\]
The first term is nonnegative and energy depends only on $q$, proving
(EV2) and the product optimizer.  Substituting the regulated
prime--Laguerre identity proves (EV3).  The uniform coefficient estimate
behind (FP4) gives
$|Y_{L,\varepsilon_L}(B)-Y_L(B)|\le
2MLN_Le^{-\eta N_L}=o(1)$ for every configuration $B$; hence
$|\log Z_{L,\varepsilon_L}-\log Z_L|=o(1)$.  Under non-RH, a syndetic bad site
$d_L\in I_L$ satisfies $\lambda_{d_L}\le-cR^{d_L}$, whence
\[
 Z_L\ge {e^{-\lambda_{d_L}}\over C_L(d_L+1)},
\]
which proves (EV4) and the contrapositive (EV5).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[bounded pressure-ratio criterion]
\label{prop:li-bounded-pressure-ratio}
Fix $\tau>0$ and set
\[
 g_\tau(x)={1\over\tau}\log{1+e^{-x}\over1+e^{-(x+\tau)}}
 ={1\over\tau}\int_0^\tau{ds\over1+e^{x+s}},\qquad
 G_L=\sum_{n\in I_L}g_\tau(\lambda_n+\log(n+1)).          \tag{PR1}
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad G_L\to0.\quad} \tag{PR2}
\]
More precisely, $G_L=O_\tau(L^{-1})$ under RH, whereas under non-RH
there is a finite syndetic gap $M$ such that
$\liminf G_L/L\ge1/M$.
Writing $z_n=e^{-\lambda_n}/(n+1)$ gives the bounded pressure ratio
\[
 e^{\tau G_L}
 =\prod_{n\in I_L}{1+z_n\over1+e^{-\tau}z_n},
 \qquad 1\le{1+z_n\over1+e^{-\tau}z_n}\le e^\tau.         \tag{PR3}
\]
Thus a deep bad site contributes only the saturated factor $e^\tau$, not
the unbounded activity of (EV1).

The same criterion has one convergent Euler diagonal.  Replacing
$\lambda_n$ by $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_L}$, with
$\varepsilon_L=e^{-(L^2+L-1)/100}$, changes $G_L$ by
$O_\tau(LN_Le^{-\eta N_L})=o(1)$ and yields
\[
 e^{\tau G_{L,\varepsilon_L}}
 =\prod_{n\in I_L}
 {1+z_{n,\varepsilon_L}\over
  1+e^{-\tau}z_{n,\varepsilon_L}},                       \tag{PR4}
\]
where every $z_{n,\varepsilon_L}$ is the absolutely convergent
prime--Laguerre activity in (FP3).

This saturation does not solve the transition.  If
\[
 C=A_n+p_n(\varepsilon)+\log(n+1),\qquad Q=Q_{n,\varepsilon},
\]
then a factor in (PR4) is
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(C,Q)
 ={1+e^{Q-C}\over1+e^{-\tau}e^{Q-C}}.                    \tag{PR5}
\]
It depends only on the complete signed difference $Q-C$.  On the diagonal
$n=N_L$, the two separated channels have magnitude
$\exp(N_L^2/100+o(N_L^2))$; locating the transition $Q-C=O_\tau(1)$
therefore requires relative precision
$\exp(-N_L^2/100+O(1))$.  Equations (PR3)--(PR5) retain density while
removing depth, but they do not estimate the paired difference.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The integrand in (PR1) lies in $[0,1]$.  Under RH,
$e^{-(\lambda_n+\log(n+1))}\le(n+1)^{-1}$ and
$g_\tau(x)\le e^{-x}/\tau$, giving $G_L=O_\tau(L^{-1})$.
Under non-RH, every interval of $M$ sufficiently large indices contains
$d$ with $\lambda_d\le-cR^d$; at those indices $g_\tau\to1$.  Disjoint
$M$-blocks in $I_L$ prove the lower density and (PR2).
Summing the logarithmic identity proves (PR3).  The derivative satisfies
\[
 |g_\tau'(x)|\le{\tanh(\tau/4)\over\tau}<\frac14,
\]
so the uniform diagonal estimate proves (PR4).  Finally (PR5) is direct
algebra, and (FA7) gives the displayed conditioning scale.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[finite logarithmic Fermi criterion on one Euler diagonal]
\label{prop:li-finite-diagonal-fermi}
Put
\[
 \mathfrak F(X)={1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 {1\over1+(n+1)e^{\lambda_n}},\qquad
 \varepsilon_X=e^{-X/100}.                                 \tag{FX1}
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
       \liminf_{X\to\infty}\mathfrak F(X)=0.\quad}        \tag{FX2}
\]
Let $\mathfrak F_{\varepsilon_X}(X)$ denote (FX1) with
$\lambda_n$ replaced by $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_X}$.  With the constants
$M,\eta$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:fermi-abel-diagonal},
\[
 \sup_{n\le X}|\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_X}-\lambda_n|
 \le2MXe^{-\eta X},\qquad
 |\mathfrak F_{\varepsilon_X}(X)-\mathfrak F(X)|
 \le {M\over2}Xe^{-\eta X}.                               \tag{FX3}
\]
Therefore (FX2) remains equivalent after this replacement, and for every
finite $X$ it becomes the single absolutely convergent Euler formula
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak F_{\varepsilon_X}(X)
 ={1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 \Bigg[1+(n+1)e^{A_n+p_n(\varepsilon_X)}
 \prod_{m\ge2}\exp\!\left(
 -{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon_X}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m)\right)\Bigg]^{-1}.
\end{aligned}}                                               \tag{FX4}
\]
The formula has no inner Abel limit.  Its separated polar and prime pieces
reach $\exp(X^2/100+o(X^2))$ at $n=X$; recovering their paired finite part
requires relative precision $\exp(-X^2/100+O(X))$.  Formula (FX4) is an
exact finite-$X$ expression; the statement $\liminf_X\mathfrak F(X)=0$ is
equivalent to RH and is not proved here.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation (FX2) is Proposition~\ref{prop:li-nonlinear-degree-partition} with
$t=1$ and $b_n=\log(n+1)$; the stronger liminf form follows from the same
positive-density obstruction.  Apply (FA5) with $h=X^{-1}$ to obtain the
first estimate in (FX3), and use the $1/4$ Lipschitz constant of the
logistic function for the second.  Substituting the regulated
prime--Laguerre identity gives (FX4).  Formula (FA7) at
$\varepsilon_X=e^{-X/100}$ gives the conditioning statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[variable temperature does not remove the polar conditioning]
\label{rmk:variable-temperature-fermi-gate}
Reducing the temperature does not remove the polar conditioning wall.
Let $t_n>0$, $b_n\ge0$ satisfy
\[
 t_nb_n\to\infty,\qquad t_nR^n\to\infty,\qquad
 b_n/R^n\to0\quad\hbox{for every }R>1.                    \tag{FT1}
\]
Then the same syndetic-mode proof gives
\[
 \RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 {1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 {1\over1+\exp(t_n(\lambda_n+b_n))}\longrightarrow0.     \tag{FT2}
\]
For example $t_n=e^{-\sqrt n}$ and $b_n=e^{2\sqrt n}$ satisfy (FT1).
On the other hand, the exact polar identity (FA7) implies the universal
incompatibility
\[
 \varepsilon_n\to0,\quad
 t_n|p_n(\varepsilon_n)-1|\le K
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 t_nR^n\le K\left({R\varepsilon_n\over1-\varepsilon_n}\right)^n
 \longrightarrow0                                      \tag{FT3}
\]
for every $R>1$.  Thus every temperature sensitive to all possible
exterior radii necessarily leaves the separated polar channel unbounded.
For the example above and $\varepsilon_X=e^{-X/100}$ its logarithmic size
is $X^2/100-\sqrt X+o(1)$, and the relative prime--polar precision remains
$\exp(-X^2/100+O(X))$.  This is an exact limitation of temperature
regularization, not an estimate of the paired product.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[hard logarithmic transition and deep overshoot]
\label{prop:li-hard-log-density}
Put
\[
 x_n=\lambda_n+\log(n+1),\qquad
 \Delta_X(B)={1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
                 {\bf1}_{\{x_n\le B\}}.                 \tag{HD1}
\]
For every prescribed function $B_X\to\infty$ with $B_X=o(\log X)$,
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
              \Delta_X(B_X)\longrightarrow0.\quad}      \tag{HD2}
\]
More weakly, for every fixed $0<\alpha<1$,
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 {1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 {\bf1}_{\{x_n\le-e^{X^\alpha}\}}\longrightarrow0.\quad} \tag{HD3}
\]
On the single Euler diagonal $\varepsilon_X=e^{-X/100}$, (HD3) with
$\alpha=1/2$ is equivalent to the entirely arithmetic assertion
\[
 \boxed{
 {1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 {\bf1}_{\left\{
 Q_{n,\varepsilon_X}\ge
 A_n+p_n(\varepsilon_X)+\log(n+1)+e^{\sqrt X}
 \right\}}\longrightarrow0,}                            \tag{HD4}
\]
where
\[
 Q_{n,\varepsilon}
 =\sum_{m\ge2}{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log m).
\]
Each series in (HD4) is absolutely convergent.  Thus (HD4) asks neither
for a sign in every degree nor for additive $O(1)$ resolution: it only
excludes overshoots of size $e^{\sqrt X}$ on a set of positive logarithmic
density.  Each of (HD2)--(HD4) is a reformulation of Li's criterion: the
forward implications use only $\lambda_n\ge0$, the converse implications use
only the exponential negative excursions of
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-bilateral-excursions}.  In particular (HD4) is
equivalent to RH and carries its full difficulty.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under RH, $\lambda_n\ge0$.  The event in (HD1) then implies
$n+1\le e^{B_X}$, and hence
\[
 \Delta_X(B_X)\le {H_{\lfloor e^{B_X}\rfloor}\over H_X}
 ={B_X+O(1)\over\log X+O(1)}=o(1).
\]
The event in (HD3) is empty.  If RH is false,
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-bilateral-excursions} supplies $c>0$, $R>1$ and
a syndetic set $D$ such that $\lambda_n\le-cR^n$ on $D$ from some point
onward.  Discarding the indices
$n<n_0+\log(B_X+\log(X+1))/\log R=O(\log\log X)$, whose harmonic mass is
$o(H_X)$, every remaining $n\in D$ with $n\le X$ satisfies
$x_n\le-cR^n+\log(n+1)\le B_X$, so the event $x_n\le B_X$ holds on a
positive lower logarithmic proportion of $D$, proving
the converse in (HD2).  Likewise, after removing
$n<KX^\alpha$, with $K>1/\log R$, one has
$cR^n\ge2e^{X^\alpha}+\log(X+1)$ for large $X$.  A syndetic set has
positive lower harmonic mass on $[KX^\alpha,X]$, so the liminf in (HD3)
is positive.

Finally, (FX3) gives
\[
 \sup_{n\le X}|\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_X}-\lambda_n|
 =O(Xe^{-\eta X})=o(1),
 \qquad
 \lambda_{n,\varepsilon}=A_n+p_n(\varepsilon)-Q_{n,\varepsilon}.
\]
Shifting the threshold $e^{\sqrt X}$ by $o(1)$ does not affect either
direction of the preceding argument, and substitution gives (HD4).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[height localization of the deep tail]
\label{prop:li-deep-height-localization}
Group the nontrivial zeros into complete functional-equation orbits and let
$\lambda_n^{(>Y)}$ denote the contribution of the orbits with positive
ordinate greater than $Y$.  Uniformly for $2\le Y\le2X$ and $1\le n\le X$,
\[
 \boxed{
 |\lambda_n^{(>Y)}|\ll
 X\log(X+2)\{1+\exp(X/(2Y^2))\}.}                         \tag{HL1}
\]
In particular,
\[
 \sup_{n\le X}|\lambda_n^{(>X^{1/4})}|
 \ll X\log(X+2)e^{\sqrt X/2}.                            \tag{HL2}
\]
Consequently, if every zero of ordinate at most $X^{1/4}$ is on the
critical line, then for all sufficiently large $X$ the indicator in (HD4)
vanishes for every $n\le X$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose in each four-point orbit the representative
$\rho=\beta+i\gamma$ with $\beta\ge1/2$, and put
\[
 w_\rho=1-\rho^{-1}=e^{-a_\rho+i\theta_\rho}.
\]
Its orbit contributes
\[
 q_n(\rho)=4-2\Re\{w_\rho^n+w_\rho^{-n}\}
 =4-4\cosh(na_\rho)\cos(n\theta_\rho),                  \tag{HL3}
\]
with half this value on the critical line.  Directly,
\[
 0\le a_\rho\le{1\over2\gamma^2},
 \qquad |q_n(\rho)|\le4+4e^{n/(2\gamma^2)}.              \tag{HL4}
\]
The finitely many orbits with $Y<\gamma\le2X$, together with
$N(T)\ll T\log(T+2)$, give the right side of (HL1).  For
$\gamma>2X$, expansion of $\log(1-1/\rho)$ gives
$|\theta_\rho|\ll\gamma^{-1}$; hence (HL3) gives
$|q_n(\rho)|\ll n^2/\gamma^2$.  Partial summation yields
\[
 \sum_{\gamma>2X}{m_\rho\over\gamma^2}
 \ll{\log(X+2)\over X},
\]
so this infinite tail is absolutely convergent and contributes
$O(X\log(X+2))$.  This proves (HL1)--(HL2).

Under the stated partial RH hypothesis, all lower orbit contributions are
nonnegative.  Thus
$\lambda_n\ge-CX\log(X+2)e^{\sqrt X/2}$ for $n\le X$.
This is greater than $-e^{\sqrt X}+o(1)$ for large $X$; (FX3) transfers the
same inequality to $\lambda_{n,\varepsilon_X}$, which is exactly the
negation of the event in (HD4).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the low-height box and the positive part]
\label{rmk:deep-height-box-markov}
The localization in Proposition~\ref{prop:li-deep-height-localization}
has a converse scale; the statements below that ignore the other zeros are
heuristic.  For a four-point orbit write
\[
 w_\rho={\rho-1\over\rho}=e^{-a_\rho+i\theta_\rho},
 \qquad
 a_\rho={1\over2}\log {|\rho|^2\over|\rho-1|^2}.
\]
Its Li contribution is
\[
 q_n(\rho)=4-4\cosh(na_\rho)\cos(n\theta_\rho).
\]
On returns with $\cos(n\theta_\rho)\ge1/2$,
\[
 q_n(\rho)\le4-e^{na_\rho}.
\]
Therefore, in degrees $n\in[X/2,X]$, and ignoring the contributions of
the other zeros, the deep threshold $S_X=e^{\sqrt X}$ is crossed as soon as
\[
 a_\rho>2X^{-1/2}+O(X^{-1}\log X).
\]
Since
\[
 a_\rho\asymp{\beta-\frac12\over\gamma^2}
\]
away from bounded-height constants, the operational obstruction is the
zero-free box
\[
 0<\gamma\lesssim X^{1/4},
 \qquad
 \beta-\frac12\gtrsim \gamma^2X^{-1/2}.                  \tag{HB1}
\]
The boundary of (HB1) tends to the critical line as $X\to\infty$.

Equivalently, if $D_n(Y)$ denotes the low-zero contribution oriented so
that an exterior quartet contributes positively to $D_n(Y)$, then under RH
$D_n(Y)_+=0$ for every $n$.  Heuristically, if instead a single exterior
zero lies in (HB1) and dominates the other low zeros, the return set above
has positive harmonic density and one expects
\[
 \sum_{n\le X}{D_n(X^{1/4})_+\over n}
 \gg e^{Xa_\rho/2}-O(X^{1/4}\log X).
\]
Markov's inequality gives no information here: the positive part is zero
under RH and, heuristically, larger than the deep scale otherwise.  In
particular, writing $\mathcal D^{\rm deep}_X$ for the left side of (HD4), a bound
\[
 \limsup_{X\to\infty}\mathcal D^{\rm deep}_X\le c
 \qquad(c>0)
\]
has no RH content by itself, because in the finite-factor models (OC2)
the bad density can be made arbitrarily small.  For instance, take the
points $a=Re^{\pm ij\theta}$, $1\le j\le K$, with $0<R<1$, multiplicities
$K+1-j$ and $\theta/\pi$ irrational.  Then
$\sum_a a^{-n}=R^{-n}(K+1)\bigl(F_K(n\theta)-1\bigr)$, where $F_K$ is the
Fej\'er kernel, since $\sum_{j\le K}(K+1-j)\cos jx={K+1\over2}(F_K(x)-1)$;
and $F_K(x)>1$ forces $\sin^2(x/2)<1/(K+1)$, which by equidistribution of
$n\theta$ modulo $2\pi$ holds only on a set of density $O(K^{-1/2})$.  The useful target is the limit $c=0$, not an
improved positive constant.

Putting $T=X^{1/4}$, (HB1) reads
\[
 0<\gamma\lesssim T,\qquad
 \beta-\frac12\gtrsim{\gamma^2\over T^2}.                \tag{HB2}
\]
This explains why the classical inputs do not exclude the box.  The known
zero-free regions exclude only a neighborhood of $\Re s=1$; (HB2) touches
the critical line whenever $\gamma=o(T)$.  Zero-density estimates bound
the aggregate count $N(\sigma,T)$ and remain positive at
$\sigma=1/2+o(1)$; they do not prove that a single boxed zero is absent.
Verification to a finite height $H$ makes the box empty only while
$X^{1/4}\le H$, hence only up to a finite scale $X\le H^4$.  What is
needed asymptotically is therefore an exclusion theorem for (HB2), not a density theorem,
zero-free-region theorem, or finite-height propagation.

The same box has a disk detector.  Consider the Cayley-side function
$E(z)=((1-z)^{-1}-1)\zeta((1-z)^{-1})$ (compare the function $F_\varepsilon$
of Remark~\ref{rmk:maximal-signed-mangoldt-identity}) and its factorization
$E(z)=B(z)O(z)$ into a Blaschke product and an outer function.  Here we use
that $E$ belongs to the Smirnov class of the disk with no singular inner
factor; this is expected from the polynomial growth of $(s-1)\zeta(s)$ in
$\Re s\ge1/2$ and $\zeta(\sigma)\to1$ as $\sigma\to\infty$, and is assumed
here without a written proof.  Under this assumption the exterior zeros give
the points $w_\rho=(\rho-1)/\rho$, and
\[
 P_z(\log|E^*|)-\log|E(z)|
 =\sum_{\Re\rho>1/2}m_\rho
 \log\left|{1-\overline{w_\rho}z\over z-w_\rho}\right|.  \tag{HB3}
\]
A zero in (HB2) gives a positive Green-potential spike of order one at a
nearby point \(z\).  Thus, under the assumption above, Poisson--Jensen
detects the box without loss.
But proving that (HB3) vanishes in a cofinal family of such boxes is
exactly proving \(B\equiv1\), hence RH.  The detector is a clean coordinate
for the obstacle, not an independent source of sign.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[a Bernstein representation of the bounded pressure]
\label{rmk:bernstein-pressure-width}
The bounded pressure itself has an exact positive representation.  For
$D=Q-C$, $\tau>0$ and $q=e^{-\tau}$,
\[
 \Phi_\tau(D)=\log{1+e^D\over1+e^{D-\tau}}
 =\int_0^\infty(1-e^{-te^D}){e^{-t}-e^{-t/q}\over t}\,dt. \tag{BW1}
\]
The measure in (BW1) is positive and has mass $\tau$.  This does not
linearize the paired difference: every global affine majorant of
$\Phi_\tau$ is the trivial constant $\tau$, while its Mellin majorants
return the exponential Li partitions already present above.  Now let the
width $\tau$ vary.  Since $|\partial_D\Phi_\tau|\le\tanh(\tau/4)$, the
normalized pressure $\Phi_\tau/\tau$ has Lipschitz constant
$\tanh(\tau/4)/\tau=1/w(\tau)$, where
\[
 w(\tau)={\tau\over\tanh(\tau/4)}.                        \tag{BW2}
\]
Heuristically, a width that still separates every fixed exterior mode
$R^{N_L}$ keeps the relative prime--pole cost
\[
 \exp\{-N_L^2/100+O(1)\},                              \tag{BW3}
\]
where $N_L=L^2+L-1$ is the block endpoint of
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-deterministic-polymer} (compare
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-bounded-pressure-ratio}),
while a width large enough to remove it suppresses those modes; we do not
formalize this.  This is a limitation (an observation) for affine,
Jensen--Bernstein and width-only estimates, not for a direct signed
estimate of (HD4).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Hadamard--Jensen and Poisson first moments]
\label{rmk:hadamard-jensen-poisson-gates}
Let $\mathcal G(z)=\sum_{n\ge1}\lambda_nz^n$ and let $\odot$ denote
coefficientwise multiplication.  The formal Hadamard exponential is
\[
 \operatorname {Exp}_{\odot}(-t\mathcal G)(z)
 =\sum_{n\ge1}e^{-t\lambda_n}z^n.                        \tag{HJ1}
\]
Under RH, $\lambda_n=\frac n2\log n+O(n)$ \cite{Lagarias2007}, so (HJ1) is
entire of order $2/t$; under non-RH its radius is zero,
by the same syndetic negative excursions used in (HD2).  For
$z_n=e^{-\lambda_n}/(n+1)$ and
$D_L(\omega)=\prod_{n\in I_L}(1+\omega z_n)$, Jensen's formula relates the
pressure to the radial positions of the zeros $-z_n^{-1}$.  Heuristically, a
contour $|z|=r_N$ chosen to avoid the points $w_\rho$ should give
\[
 \lambda_n=C_{n,N}-\sum_{|w_\rho|<r_N}m_\rho w_\rho^{-n},
 \qquad C_{n,N}\ \text{of polynomial size in }N\quad(N\le n\le2N),  \tag{HJ2}
\]
so that every exponential rate would remain in the finite interior-residue
sum; we do not prove this bound here.

Poissonization closes the linear arithmetic transform explicitly:
\[
 e^{-t}\sum_{n\ge1}{t^n\over n!}Q_{n,\varepsilon}
 =\sum_{m\ge2}{\Lambda(m)\over m^{1+\varepsilon}}
   \sqrt{t\over\log m}\,J_1(2\sqrt{t\log m}),           \tag{HJ3}
\]
and the corresponding polar mean is $1-e^{-t/\varepsilon}$.  But the
off-line quartet $q_n=4-2\Re\{(2i)^n+(2i)^{-n}\}$ has linear Poisson mean
tending to $4$, while its Fermi detector tends to $1/4$ and the deep
$e^{\sqrt X}$ detector in (HD3) tends to $1/8$.  Consequently a first-moment
estimate based on (HJ3), by itself, cannot prove the unilateral tail (HD4).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[unit-renewal Euler countermodel]
\label{rmk:unit-renewal-deep-countermodel}
The abstract multiplicative axioms still do not orient (HD4).  Let
\[
 M_d(r)={1\over d}\sum_{e\mid d}\mu(e)r^{d/e},\qquad
 \pi_d=M_d(6)+M_d(1)-M_d(3)-M_d(2).                     \tag{UE1}
\]
The integers \(\pi_d\) are nonnegative: choose disjoint subalphabets of
sizes three and two inside an alphabet of size six and compare primitive
necklaces.  The free commutative monoid with \(\pi_d\) primes of norm
\(6^d\) has
\[
 Z(T)=\prod_{d\ge1}(1-T^d)^{-\pi_d}
 ={(1-3T)(1-2T)\over(1-T)(1-6T)},\qquad
 Z(1/(6T))=Z(T).                                         \tag{UE2}
\]
Every prime tower has unit multiplicity, its Mangoldt weights are positive,
and the divisor renewal identity is exact.  Moreover its normalized
degree-$k$ prime mass is
\[
 \Psi_k=6^k+1-3^k-2^k.                                  \tag{UE3}
\]
Nevertheless its completion has the two real zero families with real parts
\(\log_6 3\) and \(\log_6 2=1-\log_6 3\).  The nearest Li singularity is
\[
 w_0=-{\log2\over\log3},\qquad
 \lambda_n=-w_0^{-n}+O(R_1^n),\quad
 1<R_1<{\log3\over\log2}.                             \tag{UE4}
\]
Consequently the regulated deep detector is triggered on the even degrees
above a constant times \(\sqrt X\), and its logarithmic density tends to
\(1/4\).

This is a countermodel only for the abstract package just listed.  It is
reticular, has periodic poles on \(\Re s=1\), has no Riemann Gamma factor,
and does \emph{not} satisfy the continuous PNT:
\(\psi_{\mathcal P}(x)/x\) oscillates between distinct limits.  Thus it
does not refute an estimate specific to the ordinary support \(\{\log p\}\);
it proves that unit renewal, tower positivity, functional symmetry and a
degreewise prime law alone cannot supply that estimate.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[truncation under the zeta distribution]
\label{rmk:zeta-law-deep-truncation}
There is also an exact probability representation using the real Mangoldt
weights.  For
\[
 \mathbb P_s(N=m)={m^{-s}\over\zeta(s)},\qquad
 Y\sim{\rm Exp}(s-1),
\]
independent, put
\[
 J_n(N)=\sum_{d\mid N}\Lambda(d)L_{n-1}^{(1)}(\log d),
 \qquad
 U_n(y)=\int_0^yL_{n-1}^{(1)}(u)\,du,
 \qquad Z_{n,s}=J_n(N)-U_n(Y).                           \tag{ZT1}
\]
Since \(\mathbb P_s(d\mid N)=d^{-s}\), Fubini and the Laguerre transform
give the exact identity
\[
 \mathbb E_s Z_{n,s}=Q_{n,s-1}-p_n(s-1).                \tag{ZT2}
\]
It does not yield concentration on the diagonal.  With
\(s=1+e^{-X/100}\), \(X/2\le n\le X\), and \(S_X=e^{\sqrt X}\), the
prime fiber \(N=p\) and a squarefree semiprime fiber \(N=pq\) have
opposite signs; a heuristic estimate on these fibres, with $\log p,\log q$
of size $\varepsilon^{-1}$, suggests
\[
 \mathbb E_s[(Z_{n,s}-S_X)_+]\ge e^{X^2/500},\qquad
 \mathbb E_s[(-Z_{n,s}-S_X)_+]\ge e^{X^2/500}.          \tag{ZT3}
\]
We do not give a proof of (ZT3); it is recorded as an observation.
Their probabilities tend to zero, but their amplitudes are expected to be
\(e^{\Theta(X^2)}\).  Hence, heuristically, bounding or truncating the two tails
separately cannot transport (ZT2) to (HD4): the mean depends on their
signed cancellation.  Equation (ZT3) does not rule out a new joint
arithmetic identity which performs that cancellation.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[maximal signed Mangoldt identity and its nonremovable pole]
\label{rmk:maximal-signed-mangoldt-identity}
The joint cancellation left open by
Remark~\ref{rmk:zeta-law-deep-truncation} can be performed exactly.  Put
\[
 t={z\over1-z},\qquad s_\varepsilon(z)=1+\varepsilon+t,
 \qquad F_\varepsilon(z)=
 (s_\varepsilon(z)-1)\zeta(s_\varepsilon(z)).
\]
The Laguerre generating function and absolute convergence for
$\varepsilon>0$ give
\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{n\ge1}\{Q_{n,\varepsilon}-p_n(\varepsilon)\}z^{n-1}
 =-{F_\varepsilon'(z)\over F_\varepsilon(z)}.}           \tag{MI1}
\]
Indeed, the two terms on the left sum respectively to
\[
 {1\over(1-z)^2}\left(-{\zeta'\over\zeta}\right)
       (s_\varepsilon(z)),\qquad
 {1\over(1-z)^2}{1\over s_\varepsilon(z)-1}.             \tag{MI2}
\]
The same formula follows from $\Lambda=\mu*\log$ and
\[
 L_{n-1}^{(1)}(x+y)=
 \sum_{j=0}^{n-1}L_j(x)L_{n-1-j}(y):                    \tag{MI3}
\]
Möbius toggling cancels every multiprime fibre and leaves exactly the
prime-power towers in $-\zeta'/\zeta$.  Thus (MI1) is the complete
algebraic cancellation, not a separation of its channels.

It does not bound (HD4).  A nontrivial zero $\rho$ of multiplicity
$m_\rho$ gives
\[
 z_{\rho,\varepsilon}
 ={\rho-1-\varepsilon\over\rho-\varepsilon},\qquad
 |z_{\rho,\varepsilon}|<1
 \Longleftrightarrow
 \Re\rho>{1\over2}+\varepsilon,                         \tag{MI4}
\]
and locally
\[
 -{F_\varepsilon'(z)\over F_\varepsilon(z)}
 ={m_\rho/z_{\rho,\varepsilon}
    \over1-z/z_{\rho,\varepsilon}}+O(1).                \tag{MI5}
\]
Hence its degree-$n-1$ coefficient is
$m_\rho z_{\rho,\varepsilon}^{-n}$.  Any rearrangement of the absolutely
convergent series on the left of (MI1) has the same sum, hence the same
meromorphic continuation, so it retains the poles (MI5).
Zeros at the same point add positive multiplicity; the polar term is
already included in $F_\varepsilon$, and the Gamma channel is regular at a
nontrivial zero.

Consequently the requested joint identity exists, but proving from it that
the logarithmic deep tail in (HD4) vanishes is exactly proving that the
interior poles (MI4) are absent as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$.  Identity
(MI1) therefore does not by itself bound (HD4), A1 or RH.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[on/off-line controls for the deep observable]
\label{rmk:on-off-deep-controls}
The detector is not forced to vanish by normalization.  For
\[
 \mathcal D_X(\ell)={1\over H_X}\sum_{n\le X}{1\over n}
 {\bf1}_{\{\ell_n+\log(n+1)\le-e^{\sqrt X}\}},          \tag{OC1}
\]
the finite-factor identity
\[
 F(z)=\prod_{j=1}^{N}(1-z/a_j),\qquad
 -{F'(z)\over F(z)}
 =\sum_{n\ge1}\left(\sum_j a_j^{-n}\right)z^{n-1}       \tag{OC2}
\]
assigns $\ell_n=N-\sum_j a_j^{-n}$, with the sign of (MI1).
The critical multiset $\{i,-i,i,-i\}$ gives
\[
 \ell_n=4-4\cos(\pi n/2)\ge0,\qquad \mathcal D_X(\ell)=0
                                                               \tag{OC3}
\]
for every $X$.  The reciprocal off-line quartet
$\{Ri,-Ri,i/R,-i/R\}$ instead gives
\[
 \ell_n=4-2(R^n+R^{-n})\cos(\pi n/2).                  \tag{OC4}
\]
Only $4\mid n$ is deeply negative.  Since the event begins at
$n=(\sqrt X-\log2)/\log R+O(1)$,
\[
 \mathcal D_X(\ell)\longrightarrow {1\over8}.           \tag{OC5}
\]
The choice $R=201/200<e^{1/100}$ survives the diagonal regulator since
$O(Xe^{-X/100}R^X)=o(1)$.

There is also a positive-Euler pair:
\[
 Z_+(T)={1+4T^2\over(1-T)(1-4T)},\qquad
 Z_-(T)={(1-3T)(1-2T)\over(1-T)(1-6T)}.                \tag{OC6}
\]
Writing $M_d(q)=d^{-1}\sum_{e\mid d}\mu(e)q^{d/e}$, the product $Z_+$
has prime multiplicities
\[
 \pi_d^+=M_d(4)+M_d(1)+{\bf1}_{2\mid d}M_{d/2}(4)
                    -{\bf1}_{4\mid d}M_{d/4}(16).
\]
They are nonnegative: only $4\mid d$ needs checking, and the necklace
bounds give
\[
 M_d(4)-M_{d/4}(16)
 \ge {2^d(2^d-d-4)\over d}\ge0.
\]
Both have products over formal primes with nonnegative integral
multiplicities and satisfy respectively
$Z_+(1/(4T))=Z_+(T)$ and $Z_-(1/(6T))=Z_-(T)$.
Their completions can be taken as
\[
 \Xi_+(s)=4^s+4^{1-s},\qquad
 \Xi_-(s)=6^s-5+6^{1-s}.                               \tag{OC7}
\]
The zeros of $\Xi_+$ all lie on $\Re s=1/2$.  Its even canonical product
gives paired Li terms $2(1-\cos(n\theta))\ge0$, hence
$\mathcal D_X(\lambda^+)=0$ for every $X$.  The zeros of $\Xi_-$ lie on
$\Re s=\log_6 2,\log_6 3$; its dominant interior pole gives
$\lambda_n^-=-(1+o(1))(\log3/\log2)^n$ on the even degrees and
$\mathcal D_X(\lambda^-)\to1/4$.

Thus the observable distinguishes both critical/off-critical quartets
and positive-Euler on/off-line systems.  This shows that the observable
has the expected sign in these models, but does not prove (HD4) for the ordinary $\Lambda(m)$: the formal
Euler controls are lattice systems without the continuous PNT or the
Riemann Gamma factor.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[summary of the reductions of this subsection]\label{rmk:a1-status-current}
The proved direct chain is
\[
 \text{explicit PNT}\Longrightarrow\mathrm{A0},\qquad
 \mathrm{A0}+\mathrm{A1}+\{\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_7>0\}
 \Longrightarrow\Omega_7\Longleftrightarrow\RH.
\]
The last equivalence is Li's criterion \cite{Li}; A1 is therefore a
sufficient condition for an inequality that is itself equivalent to RH, and
it remains unproved.
Every arrow displayed here is proved, but uniform A1 is not the only
sufficient quantifier.  Corollary~\ref{cor:a0-cofinal-quarter-margin}
separates three statements, which must be kept distinct:
\begin{enumerate}
\item for a cutoff fixed only through the floor $T\ge1000$, the coefficient
      $2002/501$ and the slack $A_n/1001$ are valid sufficient bookkeeping;
\item for the cofinal A0 family, the intrinsic strict sufficient threshold is
      $D_n^{[4]}=4\lambda_n-A_n>0$, because the cutoff may be increased
      separately at each index;
\item $D_n^{[4]}\ge0$ for all remaining indices already proves RH directly
      by Li and does not need to be transported through A1.
\end{enumerate}
Thus (X), (RT), and (RU) remain valid fixed-cutoff sufficient routes, but
none is the uniquely necessary formulation, and a proportional coercive gap is
not logically required for Corollary~\ref{cor:a1-to-omega7}.

The incomplete-zero coordinate suggests a second localization.
Writing $L_n^<=\lambda_n(\sqrt n)$ and
$\widetilde\varepsilon_n=A_n-\lambda_n+L_n^<$, the proof sketch
in Remark~\ref{rmk:lagarias-sign-erratum} would give
\[
 \lambda_n=A_n+L_n^<-\widetilde\varepsilon_n,
 \qquad |\widetilde\varepsilon_n|\ll\sqrt n\log n.          \tag{AD6}
\]
The low block contains
\[
 2N(\sqrt n)={\sqrt n\over2\pi}\log n+O(\sqrt n)
\]
symmetrically labelled zeros.  If (AD6) holds, it is a budget identity,
not a sign: all RH-strength information would be confined to this finite
low block, and any bound with error larger than $\sqrt n\log n$ would miss
the scale supplied by the high block.
The prime-only Selberg identity (SP4) preserves the linear--quadratic coupling, but its explicit
Hankel kernel has both signs; Selberg positivity supplies no coercive gap.  The optimal adjacent
taper does not repair this: Remark~\ref{rmk:a1-tapered-selberg-gate} gives a negative two-point
Hankel minor for every taper, including the Schur minimizer, and
Remark~\ref{rmk:a1-selberg-square-cancellation} shows that the centered Selberg square cancels
identically before it can pay the Schur term.  The exact global
Cholesky vector (CH1) also exists in a safe half-plane, but its norm diverges at the Li boundary by
(CH5); optimizing over all positive translation-invariant preconditioners only changes this to the
logarithmically divergent modulus cost of Remark~\ref{rmk:a1-euler-resolvent-gate}.  Thus neither
Cauchy--Schwarz nor positive Fourier preconditioning can replace the signed projection.  Keeping the
complete phase does prove the genuinely strong Euler-side estimate (AG), but (AH) shows that its
homotopy to the Li point costs exactly the off-line residues.  Allowing every positive
noncommutative preconditioner does not repair this algebraically: (AI) optimizes back to the unknown
moment, while the canonical degree compression has gain $\sqrt{n/2}>1$.  Finally,
Proposition~\ref{prop:a1-zeta-size-bias} realizes the exact weights $\Lambda(d)$ through a divisor
Markov selector, but that selector has zero spectral gap and the transfer from scaled to unscaled
Laguerre modes costs $\exp((1+o(1))n\log n)$.  Proposition~\ref{prop:a1-crossed-stein-flow}
constructs the resulting crossed expression exactly.  Its first-order kernel is canonical, independent
of the coupling, and has three signs already around the first prime.  Although the true Laplace order
(AT) holds on every completely monotone test, (AU) proves that it cannot be differentiated into the
Laguerre coefficient sign.  The exact homotopy (AW) likewise integrates back to the unknown moment.
What survives these limitations is therefore narrower than ``a positive energy'': it is a crossed, signed
estimate for the fixed unscaled Laguerre vector, retaining the divisor selector, the polar--Gamma
selector, and the unit multiplicities of all prime towers in the same expression.  The flag quotient (R) is
exact, but its boundary representation (AB), which is conditional on the existence of the
symmetric boundary limit, retains the off-line residue sum.  The remaining statements of this
paragraph summarize computations that are not reproduced in this paper; they are recorded as
unproved observations, and the symbols $\Phi_n$, $\kappa_r$, $m_r$, $D_f$, $M_s$ are used only
informally.  Grouping each complete prime tower does not appear to supply the missing sign: its
Poisson formula appears to give only the lower bound
\(\Phi_n(x)>x^{-1}-n/2\), whose direction is wrong for A1 and whose prime sum diverges.  Enforcing
the global unit identity \(\Lambda*{\bf 1}=\log\) does distinguish the shifted-quartet falsifier, but
its exact moment--cumulant split still cancels the intermediate moment,
\[
 \left(\kappa_r-\frac{m_r}{r}\right)
 +\left(\frac{m_r}{r}-(r-1)!\varepsilon^{-r}\right)
 =\kappa_r-(r-1)!\varepsilon^{-r},
\]
and the Laguerre combination recomposes the original unknown moment.  Thus a further argument cannot be obtained merely by raising this identity to finitely many moments.  At second
order the centred \({\rm lcm}\)-kernel cancels every pair of distinct prime towers, whereas the
uncentred completion has the exact form
\[
 \operatorname {Cov}(D_f,D_g)+M_s(f)M_s(g).
\]
Double M\"obius inversion diagonalizes its Gram matrix, but the difference between the pre- and
post-inversion energies is indefinite for the infinite kernel; on the real Laguerre vector its finite
divisor cutoff already has both signs (no corresponding sign is claimed for the full infinite
Laguerre form).  More generally, in the universal moment Hopf algebra every polynomial primitive is
a linear combination of cumulants; the zeta cumulants are single-tower sums.  This suggests that every exact
polynomial additive lift cancels its disconnected multi-prime reserve.  The three-mark determinant
and cubic central moment add no orientation.  The normalization by
$1501/2002$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:a1-adjacent-schur} remains a valid fixed-cutoff calculation, not an intrinsic target after
Corollary~\ref{cor:a0-cofinal-quarter-margin}.  Classical majorization before the logarithm does not
escape the same limitation: the zeta law and its couplings were already present in the divisor-selector
coordinate, while the Laguerre tests are neither monotone nor convex and the real arithmetic
comparator has both orientations.  This suggests that Schur--convexity, Karamata, and stochastic/convex
order are not automatic sign mechanisms; it does not exclude an arithmetic signed comparison specific
to $\Lambda$.

The complete-phase estimate (AG) also cannot be transported to $a=1$ by averaging over the degree.
If RH fails, the dominant off-line residues give an exponentially negative trigonometric mode on a
set of positive lower density, and that obstruction persists in every fixed-length degree window.
Thus one does not expect a density version of the phase homotopy that omits the residues in (AB).

Proposition~\ref{prop:li-syndetic-density-criteria} changes the logical
quantifier more substantially: one need not prove A1, or even a positive
quarter margin, at every index.  A subexponential lower barrier outside a
logarithmic-density-zero set, or on consecutive blocks of unbounded length,
already suffices.  Proposition~\ref{prop:li-bilateral-excursions} proves the
corresponding statement in both directions: under $\neg\RH$ the Li sequence
has positive and negative exponential excursions on two syndetic sets of
positive density.  Hence any one of the three block bounds in (FB2) is
equivalent to RH.  These are exact consequences of a hypothetical exterior
zero, not estimates for the actual zeta coefficients.

Fixed local smoothing does not exploit this relaxation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-local-filter-gate} shows that a finite filter
$P(E)$ preserves the dominant exterior mode and its syndetic negative
excursions unless $P$ annihilates every maximal mode.  Finite differences
and fixed moving averages have no exterior roots and therefore fail; an
annihilating filter merely encodes the unknown mode, as the explicit
example (FF2) demonstrates.

The nonlinear Fermi detector does admit two exact changes of quantifier.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermi-abel-diagonal} identifies its logarithmic mean
with the Abel mean (FA1) and shows that it is enough to obtain decay along
one sequence $h_j\downarrow0$.  The same proposition removes the inner
Euler limit with the single diagonal
\[
 \varepsilon(h)=e^{-1/(100h)},\qquad
 \sup_{n\le h^{-1}}|\lambda_{n,\varepsilon(h)}-\lambda_n|
 \le {2M\over h}e^{-\eta/h}.
\]
The heuristic double-logarithmic test following it suggests, however, that a fixed
shallower frequency floor discards a positive fraction of an off-line
signal; (FA7) shows simultaneously that separating the polar and prime
channels creates an $\exp(1/(100h^2))$ conditioning wall.  Thus this
diagonal is a rigorous reduction, not the missing signed estimate.
Remark~\ref{rmk:variable-temperature-fermi-gate} shows that this wall cannot
be removed by cooling the detector: any temperature which damps the polar
channel along $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ also damps every fixed exterior mode
and ceases to distinguish non-RH.

There is also a completely deterministic block version.
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-deterministic-polymer} uses
$I_L=[L^2,L^2+L-1]\cap\mathbb Z$ and proves
\[
 \RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \sum_{n\in I_L}{1\over1+(n+1)e^{\lambda_n}}\longrightarrow0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad P_L\longrightarrow1.
\]
Its finite product has the exact prime--Laguerre lift (FP3), and (FP4)
places the entire moving block on one convergent Euler diagonal, but that lift
is a noninteracting polymer: taking its logarithm returns the sum of the
one-degree activities and supplies no new cross-degree cancellation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-entropic-block} weakens the required upper bound
dramatically---a slack $\exp(o(L^2))$ on one unbounded subsequence suffices---
and rewrites it as the single combined Laguerre test $G_{L,q}$.  But the
entropy chain rule is decisive: every correlated law pays nonnegative
multiinformation, so the optimizing law is again a product and no connected
reserve is created.
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-bounded-pressure-ratio} keeps the genuinely bounded
content instead: its pressure quotient saturates each bad site at $e^\tau$
and under non-RH grows only with the density of such sites.  This removes the
double-exponential domination of the worst excursion.  Nevertheless the
transition of each bounded factor is exactly $Q-C=O_\tau(1)$, so separating
the prime and polar channels still demands relative precision
$\exp(-N_L^2/100+O(1))$.
Likewise the unitary microfrequency lift of
Remark~\ref{rmk:fermi-microfrequency-gate} loses the signal at frequency
zero, while Proposition~\ref{prop:cross-degree-energy-gate} separates the
finite cross-degree energy into pieces of size $\varepsilon^{-4N}$ whose
signed combination alone has a boundary value.  Local filters, bare
Parseval/Christoffel--Darboux positivity, and the noninteracting product are
therefore recorded as limitations (observations), not as arguments that
close the problem.

Proposition~\ref{prop:li-finite-diagonal-fermi} gives the single finite
pressure limit
\[
 \boxed{\quad\RH\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \liminf_{X\to\infty}\mathfrak F_{\varepsilon_X}(X)=0,
 \qquad \varepsilon_X=e^{-X/100},\quad}                  \tag{FX5}
\]
where every fixed-$X$ quantity is the absolutely convergent Euler product
in (FX4), and (FX3) transfers it to the unregulated Li coefficients with
error $O(Xe^{-\eta X})$.  Its polar and prime factors are separately of
size $\exp(X^2/100+o(X^2))$, so the required paired limit still contains
the full RH-strength cancellation.

Among the equivalent forms of Li's criterion recorded here, (HD4) has the
weakest quantifiers; it is still equivalent to RH.  It permits an error of size $e^{\sqrt X}$, discards every
individual degree, and only asks that the violating degrees have logarithmic
density tending to zero.  The Bernstein, Hadamard--Jensen and Poisson--Bessel
representations in Remarks~\ref{rmk:bernstein-pressure-width} and
\ref{rmk:hadamard-jensen-poisson-gates} are exact, but the quartet shows that
their positive or first-moment data do not imply this unilateral tail bound.
Proposition~\ref{prop:li-deep-height-localization} removes every zero above
$X^{1/4}$ with an exponential margin; the only remaining zero-side block is
the finite low block.  Remark~\ref{rmk:unit-renewal-deep-countermodel}
shows that unit renewal and an abstract positive Euler system do not orient
that sum, while also recording that continuous PNT and the Riemann Gamma
factor are absent from the countermodel.  For the actual Mangoldt weights,
Remark~\ref{rmk:zeta-law-deep-truncation} indicates that the separate prime and
semiprime tails have excess $e^{\Theta(X^2)}$ in both signs.  Thus what remains is a joint signed cancellation specific to the
ordinary prime support, pole and Gamma factor.
Remark~\ref{rmk:maximal-signed-mangoldt-identity} performs the exact
Möbius--Laguerre cancellation and shows that it is the logarithmic derivative
of $(s-1)\zeta(s)$.  It leaves each exterior zero as a nonremovable interior
pole of residue $-m_\rho$, whose Taylor coefficients are
$m_\rho z_\rho^{-n}$.  Hence rearranging this series cannot by itself yield (HD4); what is needed
is an argument excluding those poles, i.e.\ RH.

No estimate in this paper proves (HD4), (FX5), the deterministic block
limit, the pressure-ratio limit (PR2), or uniform A1.  These statements are
equivalences, reductions of quantifiers, and quantitative limitations; none
is a proof of RH.  Uniform A1 remains a sufficient condition, while (HD4) is
the equivalent form of RH with the weakest quantifiers recorded here, with
the unresolved cancellation displayed explicitly.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Step 5 substructure (continued): closure attempts on \texorpdfstring{$\Omega_7$}{Omega7} and a closure specification}\label{subsec:omega7-attack}

The terminal residue $\Omega_7$ --- equivalently $\delta_N\ge0$ for all $N$ --- is the single open mathematical
input of the whole route.  We record here several approaches to it, each carried to the point where
it fails, together with the unconditional statements those approaches leave behind.  Most of the
failure analyses below are heuristic; where a statement is proved, this is said.  By
Remark~\ref{lem:live-H0}, each approach must contain an unproved step; we record where it is.

\subsubsection*{Route elimination}

The following obstructions, not all of them proved, bear on this route: fixed margins (Remark~\ref{cor:no-margin}, not proved), losses $e^{o(N\log N)}$ (the
factorial wall, conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:defect-law}), zero-location inputs (Proposition~\ref{prop:unboundable}), pointwise bounds on
$\psi(y)-y$ (the $\Theta(n)$ deficit of Remark~\ref{rmk:pointwise-insufficient}), and term-by-term
$\Lambda\ge0$ positivity (the growth of $S_{\rm abs}$ observed in Remark~\ref{rmk:whitened-interference}).  Three
further mechanisms are dismissed quickly.  \emph{Krein extension}: short-window positivity
($\operatorname{supp}<\log2$, no primes) is provable, and Krein extends any interval-positive-definite
function to a positive-definite function on $\R$; but the route needs \emph{our} kernel to be the
positive extension, not the mere existence of one.  \emph{Fej\'er--Riesz}: factoring
$\mathcal A-\mathcal T_\Lambda=B^*B$ requires the boundary symbol to be nonnegative $\iff$ RH (circular,
already recorded).  \emph{Reflection positivity}: the per-prime symbol $2\Re[x/(1-x)]$, $|x|=p^{-1/2}$,
changes sign, so positivity lives in cross-prime interference.  What remains unexplored is the
\emph{gauge structure}: unconditional positivity at large height for each fixed $N$ (band closure)
against geometric negativity near an off-line zero ($\neg$RH).  Between them lies only a quantifier
exchange, whose natural tool is almost-periodicity of the prime side.

\subsubsection*{Gauge transport and its obstruction}

\begin{conjecture}[gauge transport of the arithmetic form]\label{lem:transport}
Fix $c\in\C^N$ and gauges $z_0=t_0-iy$, $z_0''=t_0''-iy$ with $N\le N_*(\min(t_0,t_0''))$.  Then
\[
   c^*J_N(t_0'')c
   =
   c^*J_N(t_0)c+(L''-L)\,c^*S_Nc
   +\mathcal E_{\rm arch}+\mathcal E_{\rm prime},
\]
where $|\mathcal E_{\rm arch}|\le C(y)\bigl(\tfrac{\log t_0}{t_0}+\tfrac{\log t_0''}{t_0''}\bigr)
c^*(LS_N)c$ \textnormal{[modulo Lemma~\ref{lem:signed-arch}]}, and
\[
   |\mathcal E_{\rm prime}|
   \le
   \Bigl(\sup_{n\le X}\bigl|n^{-it_0''}-n^{-it_0}\bigr|\Bigr)\,C_1(N)\,\|c\|^2
   +C_2\,X^{-(y-\frac12)}{\rm poly}(N)\,\|c\|^2 .
\]
\end{conjecture}
\noindent No proof of this estimate is given here; the archimedean error depends on
Lemma~\ref{lem:signed-arch}, and the prime error is the expected bound from truncating the Dirichlet
series at $X$.

\begin{conjecture}[conditional geometric detection]\label{lem:geom-detection}
Under $\neg$RH with an off-line zero $z_w=\gamma_w-i\delta_w$, at the gauge $t_0=\gamma_w$ there is a
witness $c$ with $c^*J_Nc\le-m_N$, $m_N\asymp|b|^{2N}$, $|b|=\frac{y+\delta_w}{y-\delta_w}>1$ ---
geometric growth in $N$, provided the whitening, avoidance, and tail hypotheses of
Conjecture~\ref{prop:blaschke-rate} hold.  The rate is not proved uniformly here.
A numerical experiment on Davenport--Heilbronn (no script accompanies the paper; not reproduced) agrees with this rate.
\end{conjecture}

The intended contradiction runs as follows.  By Kronecker, the recurrence times of the phases
$\{n^{-it}\}_{n\le X}$ to precision $\varepsilon$ are syndetic with gap $\ell(X,\varepsilon)$.  Transport
the hypothetical negative witness of Conjecture~\ref{lem:geom-detection} to a gauge $t_0''$ with paired phases, away from
the quartet: there the zero side gives $c^*J_N(t_0'')c\ge-C$ (the local real zeros give a Gram form
$\succeq0$; the quartet from distance $\Delta\ge\sqrt N$ contributes $e^{2Ny\delta_w/\Delta^2}=O(1)$),
while Conjecture~\ref{lem:transport} would give $c^*J_N(t_0'')c\le-m_N+(L''-L)c^*S_Nc+\mathcal E$.  If the
geometric $m_N$ dominates the polynomial errors and the controlled term $(L''-L)c^*S_Nc$ (chosen via
$t_0''\in[t_0,2t_0]$), the two estimates collide and RH follows.

\subsubsection*{The recurrence--detection imbalance (heuristic)}

The following order-of-magnitude analysis, which is not a proof, indicates where the argument
fails.  The $N$-jets see primes up to $X=e^{cN}$, and the margin
requires $\varepsilon\le m_Ne^{-CN}$.  Heuristically, the Kronecker recurrence gap is
\[
   \ell(X,\varepsilon)\;\approx\;\varepsilon^{-\pi(X)}\;=\;\exp\!\bigl(e^{cN}\cdot C'N\bigr)\;=\;
   \operatorname{Tower}(N),
\]
while the detection gain $m_N=e^{c\delta_wN}$ is only geometric.  The admissible window $[t_0,2t_0]$
contains a recurrence time only if $t_0\ge\ell(X,\varepsilon)$, i.e.\ $\gamma_w\ge
\operatorname{Tower}(\log\gamma_w/\delta_w)$, unsatisfiable for every $\gamma_w$: full phase matching
costs a tower, detection pays geometric, and the two never meet.  One need not match all phases, only
force a single scalar $F(t):=c^*P_N(t)c$ to return near its value at $t_0$ --- a level-return of an
almost-periodic function, cheap (positive density) if the value is not extremal.  Whether $F(t_0)$ is
extremal is decided by the following rigidity.

\begin{proposition}[ceiling rigidity]\label{prop:ceiling}
Fix $c$ and let $F(t):=c^*P_N(t)c$, an almost-periodic--structured function of the gauge with frequency
module $\{\log n\}$.  If all zeros within the detection window of $t$ are real, then
\[
   F(t)\;\le\;c^*A_N(t)c\;+\;E_{\rm far}(t),
\]
where $E_{\rm far}(t)$ is the far-pair tail, because the local part of
$c^*J_N(t)c=\sum_\gamma|R_c^{(t)}(\gamma)|^2$ is then a nonnegative Gram form.
\end{proposition}

\noindent Heuristically, the converse also holds up to the tails: the event $F(t)>c^*A_N(t)c$ --- the
prime form exceeding the archimedean ceiling --- should occur only at gauges detecting an off-line
zero.  This converse is not proved.  If it holds, $F(t_0)=c^*A_Nc+m_N$ sits \emph{above} the ceiling,
and the returns of the required super-ceiling deviation are other off-line zeros, so the recurrence
argument would require producing the object whose nonexistence it seeks to prove.

\begin{remark}[heuristic: recurrence--detection imbalance]\label{thm:W4}
The two preceding heuristics suggest that gauge transport of the arithmetic form is not a viable route
to $\Omega_7$: full phase-matching transport appears to require recurrence times of size
$\exp(\pi(e^{cN})\log(1/\varepsilon))$, much larger than the conjectural geometric detection gain
$|b|^{2N}$ of Conjecture~\ref{lem:geom-detection}; and scalar-level transport requires returns of a
super-ceiling deviation of $c^*P_N(t)c$, which by the heuristic converse of
Proposition~\ref{prop:ceiling} occur only at off-line zeros.  This is not a theorem about all
possible proofs: the recurrence gap is an order-of-magnitude estimate and the detection gain is
conjectural.
\end{remark}

The classical landing is the most useful by-product: the question ``how often can
$F(t)=c^*P_N(t)c$ be large?'' is, in classical coordinates, the theory of \emph{large values of
Dirichlet polynomials} (Montgomery~\cite{MontgomeryTopics}; Huxley, Invent.\ Math.\ \textbf{15} (1972); Guth and
Maynard~\cite{GuthMaynard}).  Large-value estimates bound the \emph{measure} of the bad-gauge set;
along this route one expects zero-density--type statements, not RH (heuristically: scalar transport
$\Longleftarrow$ optimal large values $\Longleftarrow$ a density hypothesis
$\Longleftarrow\cdots\Longleftarrow$ RH).  We expect, but do not prove, that large-value estimates for
Dirichlet polynomials~\cite{GuthMaynard} yield a statement of the form
\[
   \delta_N(t_0)\ge0\ \text{ for all }t_0\in[T,2T]\text{ except measure }O(T^{1-c}),\quad
   \text{uniformly for }N\le(\log T)^\theta,
\]
which would be sharper than the deterministic $\log\log$ range of
Corollary~\ref{cor:loglog-effective}; this is left open.

\subsubsection*{The function-field dictionary}

RH for curves over finite fields is proved (Weil) through positivity of the Hodge index on
$C\times C$ --- the Castelnuovo inequality --- and Weil positivity for $\zeta$ is its numerical shadow.
Executing the dictionary in the present coordinates, every piece transfers except one, and it is always
the same piece.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.44\linewidth}p{0.5\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
Function field (proved) & Present framework \\
\midrule
Correspondence algebra, $\mathrm{Fr}_n\circ\mathrm{Fr}_m=\mathrm{Fr}_{nm}$ & shift semigroup
$S_{\log n}S_{\log m}=S_{\log nm}$ \; (exists) \\
Lefschetz fixed-point formula & explicit formula (Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}) \; (exists) \\
Trivial classes $H^0,H^2$ (fibre/plane) & the two pole atoms at $\pm i/2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:pole-pair})
\; (exists) \\
Projecting off the trivial plane & Schur shorting of the pole vector \; (exists) \\
Intersection form on $H^1$ & the form $J_N$ \\
Ample class $\theta$ & the archimedean form $\mathcal A$ \\
\textbf{Riemann--Roch with effectivity} (Castelnuovo engine) & $\varnothing$ --- does not exist \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{definition}[closure specification]\label{def:closure-spec}
A \emph{positivity-bearing realization} is a graded object $H=H^0\oplus H^1\oplus H^2$ with a pairing
$\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ and an algebra action of the shift semigroup, such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(X\arabic*)}]
\item the semigroup $\{S_{\log n}\}$ acts as correspondences with
$S_{\log n}\circ S_{\log m}=S_{\log nm}$;
\item the pairing of correspondences is computed by the Weil explicit formula
(Theorem~\ref{thm:explicit-channel}), with $\dim H^0=\dim H^2=1$ realized by the pole atoms at
$\pm i/2$;
\item \textnormal{(effectivity/Riemann--Roch)} every class $\alpha\perp H^0\oplus H^2$ with
$\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle>0$ admits an effective representative, and effective classes pair
nonnegatively against the archimedean ample class $\theta=\mathcal A$;
\item the finite jets $J_N(z_0)$ are compressions of the $H^1$-pairing.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[a Castelnuovo-type closure does not follow from \textnormal{(X1)--(X4)}]\label{thm:conditional-closure}
An implication from \textnormal{(X1)--(X4)} to $\delta_N(z_0)\ge0$ is not proved by
the stated axioms.  The contradiction starts with a class $\alpha$ satisfying
$\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle<0$, whereas \textnormal{(X3)} asserts effectivity only for classes with
$\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle>0$.  It therefore cannot be applied to the class produced by a negative
jet direction.  Moreover, the displayed Hodge-index lower bound does not become nonnegative merely by
shorting the trivial plane.  A valid closure theorem would need an additional axiom acting on negative
classes, and that axiom would carry the missing RH-strength content.
\end{remark}

Two consistency checks: Davenport--Heilbronn fails at (X1) (no Euler
product, no correspondence semigroup), so this falsifier is excluded at the axiom where one expects;
the sine model satisfies (X1)--(X2) trivially with $H^1$ of controlled rank (empty cascade);
and any (X3) candidate can be tested numerically against the Cholesky flow and factorial defect
law.  Two other coordinate systems lead to the same gap (heuristically).  The \emph{Nyman--Beurling}
approach ($\mathrm{RH}\iff d_N\to0$, with a Gram matrix of Vasyunin sums; V.~I.~Vasyunin, St.\
Petersburg Math.\ J.\ \textbf{7} (1996)) does not help, because every inequality between the two frames
factors through the common spectral measure, i.e.\ through the zeros; it does, however, supply the
M\"obius mollifier as an explicit test (a certificate must be compatible with Burnol's lower bound
$d_N^2\gg 1/\log N$; J.-F.~Burnol, Adv.\ Math.\ \textbf{170} (2002), 56--70).  The
\emph{multiplicative-independence/large-values} approach leads to the measure-theoretic statement
above, not to RH.

\subsubsection*{Creating (X3): the collapse obstruction and the \texorpdfstring{$\theta$}{theta}-square}

\begin{lemma}[the collapse obstruction]\label{lem:collapse}
In commutative rings, $\Z\otimes_\Z\Z=\Z$, hence
$\operatorname{Spec}\Z\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\Z}\operatorname{Spec}\Z=\operatorname{Spec}\Z$: the
diagonal is an isomorphism and the square degenerates to the curve itself.
\end{lemma}

\noindent Consequently a realization of \textnormal{(X3)} requires a base category other than
commutative rings over $\Z$ (a base ``below'' $\Z$): before Riemann--Roch can be formulated on the
square, the square itself has to be defined.

Weil had the surface $C\times_{\mathbb F_q}C$ for free; here it must be manufactured.  Three known
substrates de-collapse the product: the scaling site of Connes and Consani (C.\ R.\ Math.\ Acad.\ Sci.\
Paris \textbf{354} (2016); Selecta Math.\ \textbf{23} (2017)), Borger's $\lambda$-rings (J.~Borger,
\emph{$\lambda$-rings and the field with one element}, arXiv:0906.3146), and Deninger's foliated
dynamical systems (C.~Deninger, Doc.\ Math.\ Extra Vol.\ ICM 1998, Vol.~I).  The table records an
informal comparison.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.2\linewidth}p{0.08\linewidth}p{0.1\linewidth}p{0.28\linewidth}p{0.24\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
Substrate & X1 & X2 & X3 (effectivity) & Test vs.\ our data \\
\midrule
Connes--Consani (scaling site, char.\ $1$) & $\surd$ & $\surd$ (trace formula) & partial: tropical
RR exists, but idempotent semirings have no subtraction $\Rightarrow$ no \emph{signature} (Hodge index
needs an abelian category) & restricted-support positivity $=$ our finite-$N$ regime \\
Borger ($\lambda$-rings / Witt: the square as $W(\Z)$ with two Frobenius families) & $\surd\surd$
(the $\psi_p$ commute: our shift semigroup) & unproved & no finite cohomology theory & right structure,
no engine \\
Deninger (foliated dynamical system) & $\surd$ (periodic orbits of length $\log p=$ our lags) &
conjectural & needs leafwise Hodge theory & his flow $=$ our gauge translation; ceiling rigidity (Remark~\ref{thm:W4}) $=$
his recurrence structure (heuristic) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The construction starts from the one thing that already exists exactly in dimension one: arithmetic
Riemann--Roch by theta invariants (G.~van der Geer and R.~Schoof, Selecta Math.\ \textbf{6} (2000);
J.-B.~Bost).  For a metrized line bundle $\bar L$
over $\overline{\operatorname{Spec}\Z}$,
\[
   h^0_\theta(\bar L):=\log\sum_{x\in L}e^{-\pi\|x\|^2},\qquad
   h^0_\theta(\bar L)-h^0_\theta(\bar\omega-\bar L)=\deg\bar L\quad\text{(exact)},
\]
where Serre duality is literally Poisson summation and the effectivity engine is Minkowski ($\deg$ large
$\Rightarrow$ short vector $\Rightarrow$ effective section).  In dimension one the full Weil dictionary
is thus already proved, with Poisson as duality and geometry of numbers as effectivity; (X3) is its lift
to the square.  The divisors of the square have a natural candidate here: correspondences are the
Dirichlet polynomials (finite shift combinations), the ample class is $\theta=\mathcal A$, and the (X2)
pairing is Poisson along the diagonal.  Three definitions of ``effective'' were tested.  \emph{(i) The
cone of real-point evaluation atoms} is circular (it presupposes real zeros).  \emph{(ii) The numerically observed
Cholesky factor $B_N(t_0)$} exists $\iff$ positivity (presupposes the conclusion), though it records the
shape an effective section would take.  \emph{(iii) The prime-shift cone}
$E:=\operatorname{cone}\{\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}S_{\log n}\}\cup(\text{positive archimedean cone})$ survives
the circularity tests: it is defined from $\Lambda(n)\ge0$ alone, with no zeros, and discriminates
Davenport--Heilbronn at the correct axiom.  With this cone, (X3) becomes a single statement of
geometry-of-numbers type.

\begin{conjecture}[M2: Minkowski for correspondences]\label{conj:M2}
Let $D$ be a correspondence class orthogonal to the trivial plane $H^0\oplus H^2$ (the pole atoms at
$\pm i/2$) with $\langle D,D\rangle_W>0$ under the Weil pairing.  Then the class of $D$, modulo the
trivial plane, contains an effective representative in the shift cone $E$; and every element of $E$
pairs nonnegatively against the ample class $\theta=\mathcal A$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[the proposed M2 closure is conjectural]\label{thm:M2-closure}
\textnormal{M2} is a proposed positive-class effectivity principle.  Even together with
\textnormal{(X1)}, \textnormal{(X2)}, and \textnormal{(X4)}, it does not imply
$\delta_N\ge0$, because the missing step concerns a negative class;
Remark~\ref{thm:conditional-closure} records the sign mismatch.  M2 may remain of geometric interest,
but it is not a closure lemma for $\Omega_7$ in its present form.
\end{remark}

\begin{problem}[M2$_N$: finite version]\label{prob:M2N}
In the $N$-jet compression at gauge $z_0$: does every $c\perp(\textnormal{pole plane})$ with
$c^*J_Nc>0$ admit a decomposition $c=e_+-e_-$ with $e_\pm$ in the compressed shift cone $E_N$ and
$\langle e_+,e_+\rangle\ge\langle c,c\rangle-\varepsilon_N$?  This is a conic-feasibility problem
(SDP), numerically decidable for small $N$.  Suggested tests: \textnormal{(i)} verify the ampleness pairings
$\langle\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}S_{\log n}\,v,\ \mathcal A\,v\rangle\ge0$ numerically on the Laguerre frame;
\textnormal{(ii)} run feasibility on $\zeta$ across gauges; \textnormal{(iii)} confirm structural
failure on Davenport--Heilbronn at the cone-existence step; \textnormal{(iv)} check that the extremal
$e_+$ reproduces the numerically observed (unverified) Cholesky flow.
\end{problem}

\subsubsection*{A candidate construction of (X3): thermal Riemann--Roch (Gibbs effectivity)}

We record one concrete proposal for the missing effectivity engine (X3).  Everything in this
subsubsection is \emph{conjectural}; its value is that it is precise enough to be attacked, and
it comes with the map of where to attack it.

\emph{The idea.}  Every attempt to build the square
$\operatorname{Spec}\Z\times_{\mathbb F_1}\operatorname{Spec}\Z$ as a \emph{geometric} object meets the
collapse $\Z\otimes\Z=\Z$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:collapse}).  The proposal here is that the square is not a
space but a \emph{thermodynamic system}: its points are KMS states, its Serre duality is temperature
inversion $\beta\mapsto1/\beta$, its trivial plane $H^0\oplus H^2$ is a phase transition, and --- the
core --- effectivity is not a count of sections but \emph{positivity of free energy}.  The role played by
Minkowski in dimension one (and by Riemann--Roch in Weil's proof) is taken by the unconditional Gibbs
variational principle; the positivity engine ceases to be spectral (where every argument is circular) and
becomes entropic (where positivity is structural --- a variance).

\emph{The objects.}  \textnormal{(D1)} The family $\{[n]\}_{n\ge1}$ realizes the shift semigroup; its analytic carrier already exists as the Bost--Connes system, whose
$\mathrm{KMS}_\beta$ states, twisted by the archimedean weight $\kappa$ (below), are the proposed family
$\omega_\beta$.  Bost--Connes has a phase transition at $\beta=1$ with partition function $\zeta(\beta)$.

\begin{definition}[thermal sections and effectivity]\label{def:thermal-effectivity}
For a finitely supported real class $D=\sum_n c_n[n]$, define the free energy
\[
   F_\beta(D):=\log\,\omega_\beta\!\left(e^{D}\right),
   \qquad e^{D}:=\sum_{k\ge0}\frac{D^k}{k!},
\]
the exponential being defined by the norm-convergent series since $D$ is bounded.
$D$ is \emph{effective} iff $F_\beta(D)\ge0$ for all $\beta$ in the self-dual window.
\end{definition}
\noindent In the realization below one has $\mu_m\mu_n=\mu_{mn}$ but $[m][n]=[mn]+\mu_m\mu_n^*+\mu_m^*\mu_n\neq[mn]$,
